# US broad program continuity ledger v1

**Latest South Africa cultural-structure rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Stats SA Census 2022 Cultural Dynamics record](records/comparative-south-africa-statssa-census-cultural-dynamics-1996-2022-language-religion-household-marriage.json) adds population-scale cultural change: multiracial households rose from 1.3% in 2001 to 11.4% in 2022, multilingual households from 3.5% in 1996 to 9.4% in 2022, and legal marriage fell from 35.6% to 23.8%. Christian affiliation was 84.5% in 2022 while traditional African religious affiliation reached 9.5%. This supports a changing-identity and household-coordination hypothesis, not a direct cohesion, practice, or consumer-welfare estimate.

**Latest Singapore cultural-credit rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [MCCY/NAC SG Culture Pass record](records/comparative-singapore-mccy-sg-culture-pass-2026-audience-reach-multicultural-access.json) adds a policy-to-audience route: more than 1.2 million citizens registered, seven in ten had not attended a local arts and culture programme in five years, and nine in ten users expressed interest in another programme. NAC’s companion survey separates 59% physical attendance, 66% digital consumption, and 28% active participation, while childhood exposure is associated with higher later engagement. This supports an audience-conversion hypothesis while preserving administrative, stated-interest, cross-sectional, and noncausal limits.

**Latest UK observed digital-finance rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Open Banking Limited activity and fraud record](records/comparative-uk-open-banking-limited-2026-api-payments-users-fraud.json) adds a live behavior-and-risk layer: payments rose 45% year over year from August 2025 to July 2026, July API calls reached 2.94 billion, and cumulative payments passed 1 billion in June. The same ecosystem’s fraud monitor reports more than two-thirds of reported cases as authorised push-payment fraud, with investment fraud among the largest categories by value. This is aggregate ecosystem and provider-covered evidence, not unique-user, reimbursement, switching, or causal-safety evidence.

**Latest Australia data-portability rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ACCC Consumer Data Right data-quality record](records/comparative-australia-accc-consumer-data-right-data-quality-2026-switching-usability.json) adds a platform-governance middle stage: 1.3 million Australians were using the CDR by July 2026, while stakeholder ratings were 3.5/5 for consumer-data quality, 3.2/5 for product-data quality, and 3.1/5 for resolving consumer-data issues. Inconsistent fields, stale rates, invalid links, workarounds, and tickets open more than six months show why formal portability does not automatically become usable comparison or switching power. It preserves the small stakeholder sample, administrative-user count, descriptive, and noncausal boundaries.

**Latest Brazil demand-side cultural rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Fundação Itaú/Datafolha cultural-habits record](records/comparative-brazil-fundacao-itau-datafolha-cultural-habits-2025-remote-presence-class-gap.json) adds a current hybrid-access test: 90% reported virtual cultural activity and 84% in-person activity, but in-person participation fell to 70% among respondents with elementary education and 71% among D/E classes versus 96% with higher education and 93% in A/B. Cinema and theatre gaps remain especially stratified. It preserves the flow-location, self-report, class, education, and noncausal boundaries.

**Latest Brazil cultural-economy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [IBGE SIIC 2013-2024 record](records/comparative-brazil-ibge-cultural-indicators-2013-2024-work-formality-trade.json) adds a production-side cultural layer: 5.9 million people worked in the cultural sector in 2024, cultural work was 44.6% informal versus 40.6% across sectors, and only 25.4% of people with culture-related degrees worked in the cultural sector. The record connects regional supply, labor precarity, education conversion, entrepreneurship, and trade without treating sector size as audience welfare.

**Latest digital-capacity rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [World Bank Digital Progress and Trends 2025 record](records/comparative-world-bank-digital-progress-trends-2025-infrastructure-speed-data-skills.json) adds infrastructure quality beneath the GSMA and Findex access layers: internet use reached 54% in LMICs and 27% in LICs in 2024, 5G coverage was 35% and 4%, and annual data traffic ranged from 100 GB per capita in LMICs to 5 GB in LICs. It preserves the aggregate, mixed-source, income-group, quality-versus-use, and noncausal boundaries.

**Latest LMIC digital-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [GSMA Mobile Gender Gap 2026 record](records/comparative-gsma-mobile-gender-gap-2026-access-affordability-safety.json) adds a cross-country platform/infrastructure layer: women were 12% less likely than men to use mobile internet in LMICs, leaving about 200 million fewer women users, while regional female use ranged from 35% in Sub-Saharan Africa to 77% in Latin America and the Caribbean. The record preserves the modelled, industry-research, binary-gender, and access-versus-effective-use boundaries.

**Latest England time-and-care rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [SACE 2025-26 record](records/comparative-england-dhsc-sace-2025-2026-carer-time-work-social-capacity.json) adds official carer-level evidence: only 16.8% felt able to spend time on valued or enjoyable things, 19.5% were out of paid employment because of caring, 34.7% provided 100+ hours weekly, and 26.2% always felt involved in support-service discussions. It strengthens the hidden-time and public-voice routes while preserving the local-authority sampling and noncausal boundaries.

**Latest UK consumer-recourse rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [CMA Annual Report 2025-26 record](records/comparative-uk-cma-annual-report-2025-2026-consumer-redress-digital-markets.json) adds the regulator-side middle stage: the CMA reports £3.3bn in average annual direct consumer benefits over three years, 14 new-regime investigations, more than 80,000 ordered learner-driver refunds averaging about £9, and 7,636 public correspondence items. It supports an enforcement-to-redress route, but the record keeps modeled benefit, individual receipt, remedy adequacy, and durable compliance open.

**Latest Australia current cultural-connection rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Creative Australia National Arts Participation Survey 2025 record](records/comparative-creative-australia-national-arts-participation-2025-cultural-background-cost.json) adds current cultural-connection and cost-conversion evidence: own-cultural-background arts engagement rose to 40%, while live attendance reached 74% alongside 60% naming cost as the main barrier and 55% missing a wanted event because of cost. It narrows the gap left by the historical migrant baseline without claiming current migrant-specific attendance.

The full 2025 report adds a current CALD agency layer: general-sample CALD respondents reported higher creative participation than the overall estimate across music, dance, theatre, and writing; the separate translated sample was even more active but is not representative. This keeps cultural production, audience attendance, and migrant status as distinct measurement stages.

It also shows audience conversion: CALD respondents were more likely than non-CALD respondents to attend arts events for their own cultural background or community (49% versus 32%), with music and festivals prominent. The record preserves the self-identified CALD, cross-sectional, and non-country-of-birth limits.

The same survey adds an AI-authenticity route: 40% report using AI tools for creative work or ideas, 82% want creative AI use disclosed, and 93% report at least one concern about AI and creativity. Adoption, provenance, and legitimacy remain separate stages.

**Latest Australia historical cultural-baseline rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ABS migrant cultural-attendance baseline record](records/comparative-abs-migrant-cultural-attendance-2005-06-english-country-arrival-baseline.json) restores a direct culture-by-migrant layer: 2005–06 attendance was higher among migrants from main English-speaking countries than among migrants from non-main-English-speaking countries, with venue-specific differences for cinemas and libraries. It is explicitly held as a historical baseline because newer settlement and GSS releases do not provide the same current cross-tabs.

**Latest Australia migrant-social-connection continuation — 2026-09-30:** the existing [ABS General Social Survey 2025 record](records/comparative-abs-general-social-survey-2025-cultural-participation-financial-stress-connection.json) now includes a migrant subgroup route: weekly outside-household face-to-face contact is 46% among migrants versus about 53% overall, and reported discrimination is 20% versus 17% among people born in Australia. This sharpens the settlement record's economic pathway into social connection and public-friction questions while preserving cross-sectional and self-report limits.

**Latest Australia migration-and-room rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ABS Migrant Settlement Outcomes 2026 record](records/comparative-abs-migrant-settlement-outcomes-2026-visa-stream-income-rent-language.json) adds a linked administrative route from visa stream and arrival cohort through employment, income, rent burden, English proficiency, and GP contact. Skilled and humanitarian migrants occupy sharply different economic positions, so migration should not be treated as one consumer or cultural-access segment. The record preserves observational, selection, rounding, and non-cultural-outcome limits.

**Latest Spain household-room rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [INE Household Budget Survey 2025 record](records/comparative-spain-ine-household-budget-2025-recreation-culture-quintile-room.json) adds household-budget distribution: recreation, sport, and culture averaged €1,772 per household and rose 4.7% in current prices, while the lowest-spending quintile devoted 61.5% to housing/energy plus food and the highest quintile devoted 33.6% to transport, hospitality, and recreation/culture combined. This is budget room, not realized attendance or equal cultural welfare.

**Latest Australia continuation — 2026-09-30:** the [ABS August 2026 household-spending record](records/comparative-abs-monthly-household-spending-august-2026-culture-reversal-transport.json) follows the July transaction signal: total spending was flat month over month and 6.8% higher year over year, but recreation and culture fell 1.4% while transport rose 2.3%. The continuation supports category rotation and event-timing hypotheses, not a household-level leisure retreat or welfare estimate.

**Latest Australia observed-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ABS Monthly Household Spending Indicator July 2026 record](records/comparative-abs-monthly-household-spending-july-2026-card-culture-volume.json) adds aggregated, de-identified transaction behavior: total current-price spending rose 1.1% month over month and 7.0% year over year, recreation and culture rose 1.5% month over month, and real recreation-and-culture volume rose 1.7% in the June quarter versus 0.7% for total spending. This is observed spending, not unique-household welfare, equal access, or cultural attendance.

**Latest Norway inclusion-and-conversion rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Norway immigrant-background cultural-use record](records/comparative-norway-ssb-immigrant-cultural-use-2025-library-museum-concert-gap.json) adds a distinct subgroup denominator: 1,331 completed interviews, with 60% visiting a public library, 53% a museum, and 58% a cinema. Participation was higher in libraries and museums but lower in theatre and concerts than the total population, while 62% wanted more cinema and 61% more concerts. This sharpens venue-specific inclusion and unmet-demand testing; it does not prove assimilation, discrimination, or causal barriers.

**Latest UK mobility-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [VisitBritain Domestic Sentiment Tracker September 2026 record](records/comparative-visitbritain-domestic-sentiment-september-2026-travel-intentions-cost-barriers.json) shows UK overnight-trip intention recovering to 78%, while 53.8% identify costs and finances as a barrier and intended trips skew toward one-to-three-night short breaks (62.1%) rather than four-plus-night holidays (34.3%). It supports a demand-recovery-with-constrained-conversion hypothesis, not realized travel, cultural attendance, welfare, or causal cost effects.

**Latest England hybrid-culture rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [DCMS Community and Engagement Survey Quarter 2 record](records/comparative-england-dcms-community-engagement-q2-2025-26-cultural-civic-hybrid-shift.json) shows in-person arts engagement falling from 90% to 83% while digital arts engagement moved from 34% to 36%; physical library use rose from 27% to 31% and digital library use from 16% to 18%. Monthly formal volunteering rose from 16% to 18% and local-official contact from 13% to 15%. It supports a selective hybridization-and-civic-action hypothesis, not causal substitution, institutional response, or durable cohesion.

**Latest EU consumer-conditions rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [European Commission Consumer Conditions Scoreboard record](records/comparative-eu-consumer-conditions-scoreboard-2025-online-problems-scams-trust-sustainability.json) adds a cross-national consumer-protection baseline: online buying reached 76%, but 26% of online buyers reported a legitimate complaint problem versus 16% offline; 45% encountered online scams, 66% fake reviews, and 61% misleading discounts; only 61% trusted public organisations to protect consumer rights, while environmental purchasing fell 13 points since 2022. It supports an adoption-with-protection-gap hypothesis, not causal fraud loss, welfare, or platform attribution.

**Latest Australia physical-culture pricing rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ACCC Dendy Cinema record](records/comparative-australia-accc-dendy-cinema-drip-pricing-2025-booking-fee-transparency.json) adds a venue-level drip-pricing case: Dendy paid an A$19,800 penalty after the unavoidable per-ticket booking fee was omitted from the prominently displayed online ticket price until late checkout; the ACCC examined cinema practices more broadly. It supports a cross-jurisdiction price-interface hypothesis, not an admission of contravention, attendance-loss, or consumer-harm estimate.

**Latest EU marketplace implementation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [European Commission/CPC Vinted record](records/comparative-eu-cpc-vinted-secondhand-marketplace-buyer-fee-refund-controls.json) adds an implemented-control case: Vinted changed its website/app to disclose total price including buyer protection, clarify nondelivery and counterfeit refunds, improve seller identity and review information, and remove misleading free-purchase impressions; delivery-fee disclosure remained unresolved. It supports an implemented-control-with-open-gap hypothesis, not verified refund completion, affordability, or welfare effects.

**Latest EU ticket-resale governance rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [European Commission/CPC Viagogo record](records/comparative-eu-cpc-viagogo-2024-ticket-resale-transparency-pressure-remedy.json) adds a cross-jurisdiction platform-control layer: seller identity, marketplace role, total-price clarity, ranking explanation, seat information, countdown reduction, fraud/cancellation handling, legal jurisdiction, and refund-window commitments. It supports a platform-governance and remedy-reachability hypothesis, not verified implementation, affordability, or participation effects.

**Latest UK ticketing-remedy comparison — 2026-09-30:** the [CMA Ticketmaster/Oasis record](records/comparative-uk-cma-ticketmaster-oasis-2024-2025-dynamic-pricing-undertakings.json) contrasts with the StubHub refund case: Ticketmaster accepted 24-hour notice for tiered pricing, better price information in online queues, no misleading labels, and two years of CMA reporting, but the case page states no direct refund total. It supports a prospective-transparency-versus-retrospective-redress hypothesis, not a remedy-effectiveness estimate.

**Latest UK live-event remedy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [CMA StubHub UK hidden-fees record](records/comparative-uk-cma-stubhub-live-event-hidden-fees-2026-automatic-refund-remedy.json) adds a named cultural-consumer episode: mandatory fees were added at checkout, 51,350 customers were ordered more than £590,000 in automatic refunds, StubHub UK was fined £889,200, and the firm ended the conduct. It supports a hidden-price-to-recourse hypothesis, not proof of complete refund receipt, deterrence, or equal live-event access.

**Latest France cultural-recovery rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Ministry of Culture/CREDOC 2024 cultural-outings record](records/comparative-france-ministry-culture-credoc-2024-cultural-recovery-stratification.json) adds a recovery-with-stratification layer: cinema reached 60%, live performance 49%, monuments 64%, and libraries 37%, broadly returning to pre-pandemic levels; yet museum attendance was 25% below the baccalaureate versus 62% with bac+3 or more, and 30% for low-income people versus 54% for high-income people. It supports a recovery-with-persistent-access-gaps hypothesis, not a causal price, time, or welfare estimate.

**Latest England cultural-layering rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [DCMS Participation Survey 2024/25 record](records/comparative-uk-dcms-participation-survey-2024-2025-arts-libraries-digital-layering.json) adds a physical-digital conversion test: 91% engaged with the arts, 34% engaged both physically and digitally, only 1% engaged exclusively digitally, and library engagement reached 31% overall with 26% physical and 16% digital. It supports a layering rather than replacement hypothesis, not a causal affordability, time, or welfare estimate.

**Latest Spain cultural-platform rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Ministry of Culture cultural-habits record](records/comparative-spain-ministry-culture-cultural-habits-2024-2025-reading-attendance-digital-participation.json) adds direct cultural participation and platform mediation: 64.9% read a book, 47.6% visited a museum/exhibition/gallery, 47.1% attended a live cultural performance, 48.5% went to the cinema, and 74.1% had a household subscription to at least one digital platform. It supports a hybrid-access and platform-mediated cultural-recovery hypothesis, not a causal affordability, substitution, or welfare estimate.

**Latest ASEAN digital-trust rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [GSMA ASEAN Consumer Scam record](records/comparative-asean-gsma-consumer-scam-2026-resolution-recovery-digital-trust.json) adds a cross-channel consumer-recourse layer: 8% reported recent scam exposure, 68% of victims lost money, 82% of those recovered none, and 45% of reported cases remained unresolved. It supports a prevention-to-resolution gap hypothesis, with industry-survey and access limitations preserved.

**Latest EU physical-activity rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Special Eurobarometer sport record](records/comparative-eu-eurobarometer-sport-physical-activity-2026-commuting-community-gaps.json) adds a routine-and-social-infrastructure layer: 39% never exercise, while active commuting is 37% and fitness-centre attendance 39%; perceived mental-health and community value reaches 93% and 86%. It supports a belief-to-capability conversion hypothesis, not a health or cohesion outcome estimate.

**Latest New Zealand domestic-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [MBIE Domestic Visitor Survey record](records/comparative-new-zealand-mbie-dvs-october-2025-domestic-travel-culture-cost-barriers.json) adds a resident travel and cultural-conversion layer: 61% took an overnight trip, 36% of overnight trips included a museum/gallery visit, 13% a Māori cultural site, and cost constrained 32% of planned overnight travel. It supports an affordability-to-participation hypothesis, not a causal or regional welfare estimate.

**Latest New Zealand visitor-behavior rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [MBIE IVS record](records/comparative-new-zealand-mbie-ivs-june-2026-tourism-recovery-purpose-cultural-access.json) adds a mobility-and-place layer: visitor volume reached 94% of 2019 and real spend 98%, while holiday purpose rose to 53% from 51% in 2019. It supports a recovery-and-composition hypothesis, not equal regional benefit, cultural participation, or local welfare.

**Latest Canada pressure-to-care rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [MHRC Poll 29 record](records/comparative-canada-mhrc-poll29-2026-cost-pressure-social-connection-care-exit.json) adds a material-pressure and institutional-continuity layer: 46% reported cost-of-living mental-health impact, 37% bill difficulty, 35% housing difficulty, and 52% of people with recent support ended care earlier than planned or needed. It supports a pressure-to-exit hypothesis, not a clinical, causal, or verified-care-outcome estimate.

**Latest England social-connection rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [CES loneliness record](records/comparative-england-ces-2025-26-loneliness-disability-deprivation-local-gap.json) adds a social-consequence layer: national often/always loneliness was 7%, but disabled people were at 14% versus 3% for non-disabled people and the most deprived areas at 11% versus 4% in the least deprived. It supports an unequal-exposure and local-capacity hypothesis, not a causal mental-health or service-effect estimate.

**Latest ONS real-time consumer rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ONS real-time indicators record](records/comparative-ons-realtime-consumer-pressure-august-2026-footfall-spending-failures.json) adds a near-real-time administrative/card-payment layer: retail footfall fell 2% month-on-month and total Revolut spending fell 1%, while entertainment spending rose 14% year-on-year and sampled Direct Debit failures increased from 2.3% to 2.4%. It supports a mixed consumer-pressure hypothesis, not a household-distress or cultural-attendance estimate.

**Latest China consumer-composition rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [NBS August 2026 retail/CPI record](records/comparative-china-nbs-august-2026-retail-cpi-category-pressure.json) adds a high-frequency non-Western consumer layer: total retail sales grew 0.4% year-on-year in August, online service retail grew 5.1% year-to-date, telecom equipment grew 27.3%, and motor vehicles fell 18.5%; education/culture/recreation prices rose 1.4% while residence prices fell 0.3%. It supports a category-and-price divergence hypothesis, not a household-affordability or preference estimate.

**Checked:** 2026-09-30
**Status:** active long-term program control record  
**Scope:** the complete 14-theme Cultural, Social, and Geopolitical Theme
Mining program

**Latest South Africa social-capacity rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Stats SA Volunteer Activities Survey 2024 record](records/comparative-south-africa-statssa-volunteer-activities-2024-social-capacity-direct-organizational.json) adds unpaid civic contribution: 3.1 million people volunteered, the rate rose to 6.9%, 56.3% volunteered directly versus 42.8% through organizations, and 91.3% cited a cause they believed in; rates varied by sex, population group, labor-market status, and province. It supports a direct-versus-institutional social-capacity hypothesis, not a causal cohesion or welfare estimate.

**Latest Colombia cultural-economy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [DANE CSECC 2025 preliminary record](records/comparative-colombia-dane-cultural-creative-economy-2025-value-added-employment-divergence.json) adds a supply-and-labor layer: real cultural/creative value added grew 2.6%, arts and heritage 4.3%, and functional creations 2.9%, while cultural industries fell 1.1%; total employment rose 1.7% but cultural-industry employment fell 2.3%. It supports a sectoral-composition and worker-transition hypothesis, not a consumer-participation or earnings-security estimate.

**Latest Mexico cultural-participation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [INEGI MODECULT 2025 record](records/comparative-mexico-inegi-modecult-2025-cultural-participation-digital-barriers.json) adds direct participation evidence: comparable adult attendance reached 61.2% in 2025, up from 52.5% in 2024 but still 2.8 points below 2016; the redesigned 12+ measures show strong age, education, Indigenous-language, disability, time, cost, and digital gradients. It supports a cultural-conversion and unequal-access hypothesis, not a causal attendance or cultural-welfare estimate.

**Latest Mexico household-room rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [INEGI ENIGH 2024 record](records/comparative-mexico-inegi-enigh-2024-household-room-cultural-spending-inequality.json) adds direct household-budget evidence: average monthly monetary expenditure was 15,891 pesos, up 7.9% from 2022 in 2024 pesos; education and recreation were 9.6% of spending, but education remained 21.0% below 2016 and household income/spending gaps were large by decile, gender, Indigenous-language status, and state. It supports a household-room and cultural-conversion hypothesis, not a causal or real-time 2026 estimate.

**Latest Brazil retail-category rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [IBGE July 2026 retail record](records/comparative-brazil-ibge-retail-july-2026-category-and-regional-divergence.json) adds a Latin American category-and-region layer: retail volume fell 0.8% month over month but rose 1.2% year over year; food retail rose 2.9% year over year, pharmaceuticals/cosmetics 4.7%, while clothing fell 4.7% and books/stationery fell 4.2% month over month. It supports selective adaptation and regional divergence, not a household-welfare or cultural-participation estimate.

**Latest Canada retail-channel rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada July 2026 retail record](records/comparative-statcan-retail-trade-july-2026-ecommerce-regional-pullback.json) adds a nominal-growth versus short-run-pullback layer: retail sales were $73.7 billion, down 0.7% month over month but up 5.1% year over year, while volume fell 1.1%; e-commerce fell 3.5% to $5.5 billion and 7.5% of retail trade, with Ontario down 2.0% and Alberta up 1.4%. It supports a channel-and-region divergence hypothesis, not a household-welfare or durable consumer-retrenchment estimate.

**Current registry checkpoint:** 1994 machine-readable records and 5963
observations. This is an active, never-ending discovery and synthesis program;
the checkpoint is a control state, not a completion claim.

**Latest Brazil Pix-conversion rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [BCB payment-habits record](records/comparative-brazil-bcb-pix-2023-payment-diary-digital-barriers.json) adds a Latin American payment-diary layer: cash fell to 40.5% of recorded payments, Pix reached 24.9%, and knowledge/connectivity barriers persisted among nonusers and alternative-method choosers. It supports a capability-and-executability hypothesis, not a universal digital substitution claim.

**Latest South Africa payment-behavior rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [SARB Payments Study record](records/comparative-south-africa-sarb-payments-study-2023-cash-digital-income-choice.json) adds a transaction-diary layer: cash dominates volume but cards carry more value, and recall-based digital adoption exceeds what appears in a short observed diary. It supports a measurement-aware payment-conversion hypothesis, not a simple cash-to-digital substitution claim.

**Latest Kenya financial-consumer rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [FinAccess record](records/comparative-kenya-finaccess-2024-digital-finance-usage-debt-distress.json) adds behavior and remedy beneath the prior ICT access layer: mobile-money usage reached 82.3%, daily use 52.6%, credit use 64.0%, and savings use 68.1%; complete loan default was 16.6% among borrowers and 9.8% of mobile-money users reported losses. It supports a digital-finance exposure-and-recourse hypothesis, not a causal product-harm estimate.

**Latest Kenya digital-funnel rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [KNBS ICT record](records/comparative-kenya-knbs-ict-2023-24-mobile-internet-household-gap.json) adds an African digital-access layer: mobile use reached 64.9% of individuals while internet use was 35.0%; household internet connection was 54.1% urban versus 25.1% rural, and county use ranged from 64.7% to 9.1%. It supports a mobile-reach versus usable-digital-optionality hypothesis, not a cultural-use or consumer-welfare conclusion.

**Latest India consumption-room rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [MoSPI HCES record](records/comparative-india-mospi-hces-2023-24-consumption-room-inequality.json) adds a household-consumption layer beneath cultural and consumer trends: rural MPCE was ₹4,122 versus ₹6,996 urban, non-food shares were 53% versus 60%, and top-to-bottom-5% MPCE ratios were about 6.0× and 8.6×. It supports a distributional-access hypothesis, not a direct measure of entertainment spending or cultural attendance.

**Latest Colombia hidden-time rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [DANE ENUT record](records/comparative-colombia-dane-enut-2024-25-unpaid-care-time-gender-migration.json) adds a time-use and care layer beneath cultural and consumer participation: women’s daily unpaid-work participation was 90.0% versus 65.5% for men, and participating women averaged 7h35m versus 3h12m. The preliminary bulletin supports a hidden-time-price hypothesis, not a causal estimate of gender, migration, or cultural outcomes.

**Latest Indonesia social-participation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [BPS socio-cultural record](records/comparative-indonesia-bps-socio-culture-2025-informal-formal-participation.json) adds a non-European civic-culture layer: 88.87% participated in local social-community activity, while only 11.06% held formal organization membership outside work or school; neighborhood-meeting participation was 43.22% for men and 24.81% for women. It supports a hypothesis that informal social reach, formal affiliation, and distributed voice are distinct stages, not interchangeable measures of cohesion.

**Latest social-commerce conversion rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Philippines PSA NICTHS e-commerce record](records/comparative-philippines-psa-nicths-2024-ecommerce-cod-social-selling-trust.json) adds the behavioral transaction layer: buying, social selling, delivery, cash payment, mobile money, and security barriers. It supports a trust-and-habit constraint on digital commerce, not a platform-causal or welfare conclusion.

**Latest cyber-trust rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Philippines PSA NICTHS cybersecurity record](records/comparative-philippines-psa-nicths-2024-cybersecurity-awareness-incidents-reporting.json) adds the protection layer beneath digital commerce: incident exposure is widespread, awareness is education- and place-stratified, SMS fraud dominates reported modalities, and formal reporting is rare. It supports a protection-and-recourse hypothesis, not a forensic incident, financial-loss, or platform-liability conclusion.

**Latest social-time rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada time-use record](records/comparative-statcan-time-use-2022-time-pressure-friendship-social-connection.json) adds a long-run household-time and social-connection layer: perceived time pressure rose, everyday friendship contact fell, and concern about insufficient family/friend time increased. It supports a hidden-price and social-capacity hypothesis, not causal loneliness, mental-health, or cultural-participation evidence.

**Latest safety-by-design rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Ofcom protective-defaults record](records/comparative-ofcom-protective-defaults-social-platforms-2026-choice-architecture-rct.json) adds experimental evidence that protective defaults shape retained user settings across adults and teenagers. It supports a choice-architecture and platform-governance hypothesis, not proof of live-service harm reduction, compliance, or equal effects across users.

**Latest recourse-routing rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [UK Financial Ombudsman record](records/comparative-uk-financial-ombudsman-2025-26-complaints-routing-uphold-recourse.json) adds an institutional-access layer: complaint volume, professional representation, withdrawal, abandonment, and uphold rates all shift together. It supports a routing-and-case-readiness hypothesis, not a causal estimate of representative fees, population harm, or remedy adequacy.

**Latest exit-friction rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Ofcom telecom switching and remedy record](records/comparative-ofcom-telecom-switching-retention-harm-remedy-2026.json) adds a named competition-and-exit episode: affordability pressure produces switching, but firm retention tactics can impose time, hassle, missed payments, and delayed exit. Ofcom's fine and One Touch Switch safeguard support a remedy-path hypothesis, not proof of individual compensation receipt or durable compliance.

**Latest EU youth-social rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat social-network record](records/comparative-eurostat-social-network-use-youth-2025-age-country-gap.json) adds a cross-country population-use layer: youth participation is near-universal but the age gap and national levels vary sharply. It supports a generational and place-stratified platform-sociality hypothesis, not a claim about social benefit, political influence, or wellbeing.

**Latest digital-cultural-conversion rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat online event-ticket record](records/comparative-eurostat-online-event-ticketing-2025-cultural-access-country-gap.json) adds the transaction stage before cultural attendance: online ticket purchases rose to 26.8% of EU individuals, but country rates ranged from 51.5% to 11.4%. It supports a digital-access and place-stratification hypothesis, not proof of attendance, affordability, or cultural welfare.

**Latest cultural-workforce rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat cultural-employment record](records/comparative-eurostat-cultural-employment-2025-workforce-education-country-gap.json) adds the production-side labor layer: cultural work is 4.3% of EU employment, unusually tertiary-educated, and place-stratified. It supports a cultural-supply and occupational-selection hypothesis, not a claim about wages, job quality, output, or audience access.

**Latest leisure-affordability rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat leisure-affordability record](records/comparative-eurostat-leisure-affordability-2025-holiday-participation-inequality.json) adds a household-room constraint beneath cultural supply: 27.5% could not afford a week away and 12.1% could not afford regular leisure activity. It supports a material-access hypothesis, not proof of cultural desire, attendance, or causal poverty pathways.

**Latest handset-ecology rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Indonesia BPS telecommunications record](records/comparative-indonesia-bps-telecommunication-2024-mobile-computer-fixedline-ecology.json) adds a large-population device layer: internet use is high but computer and fixed-line ownership are low, with public and mobile-mobility access material. It supports a mobile-first access hypothesis, not a claim about cultural participation or digital welfare.

**Latest administrative-communication rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Ghana NCA Q2 2026 telecom record](records/comparative-ghana-nca-q2-2026-mobile-data-voice-substitution.json) adds an administrative network layer: mobile data reach is expanding while voice intensity per subscription falls and fibre remains small. The indicators support a communication-mode hypothesis, not person-level substitution, household access, or cultural impact.

**Latest digital-intensity rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Philippines PSA NICTHS 2024 record](records/comparative-philippines-psa-nicths-2024-mobile-digital-intensity-cost.json) adds actual-use intensity beneath household access: cellphone dependence, daily use, hours online, regional gaps, and reported connection spending. It supports a mobile-attention and unequal-reach hypothesis, not a cultural-use or welfare conclusion.

**Latest mobile-first digital-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [South Africa GHS 2025 record](records/comparative-south-africa-statssa-ghs-2025-mobile-first-digital-access-household-room.json) adds infrastructure beneath digital culture: broad internet reach coexists with low fixed-home access and large provincial gaps, while household structure and food-access constraints shape the room available for downstream consumption. It supports an access hypothesis, not cultural-use or causal welfare evidence.

**Latest observed-basket rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [France INSEE cultural-leisure spending record](records/comparative-france-insee-cultural-leisure-spending-2000-2025-service-shift.json) adds a national-accounts counterpart to attendance: cultural services gained composition share while books, press, stationery, and durable cultural goods lost share. The signal supports an ownership-to-service hypothesis, not a participation, welfare, or distribution claim.

**Latest cultural-supply divergence rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [China NBS cultural-enterprise H1 2026 record](records/comparative-china-nbs-cultural-enterprises-h1-2026-channel-creative-divergence.json) adds the supply-side channel: aggregate sector revenue grew, but creative design and information services outpaced content and leisure, while communication channels contracted. The release supports a production/distribution hypothesis, not consumer spending, platform power, or producer welfare.

**Latest active-participation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [South Korea MCST 2025 culture and leisure record](records/comparative-south-korea-mcst-2025-cultural-leisure-active-participation.json) adds a mode-shift check: direct event attendance fell, while active participation, cultural education, leisure continuity, and leisure satisfaction rose. The release supports a bounded change-in-engagement hypothesis, not causal substitution or equal access.

**Latest place-stratified cultural-outings rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [French Ministry of Culture 2025 outings barometer](records/comparative-france-ministry-culture-cultural-outings-2025-mobility-stratification.json) adds a physical-access and mobility layer: national attendance remains near pre-pandemic levels, but age, education, income, and settlement type sharply sort participation. Museum and festival visits also connect to overnight travel, so cultural access can be a tourism and transport bundle rather than a local venue decision.

**Latest digital-domestic-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Brazil Ministry of Culture habits and policy record](records/comparative-brazil-minc-culture-habits-consumption-policy-legitimacy-2025.json) adds a digital/home-first cultural-consumption layer and a legitimacy paradox: time is a more common stated barrier than money, while approval of named cultural policies exceeds public awareness of them. The commissioned survey supports a bounded perception and access hypothesis, not causal policy impact or durable trust change.

**Latest hidden-time conversion rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [India Time Use Survey 2024 record](records/comparative-india-mospi-time-use-2024-culture-leisure-care-gender.json) adds the time-allocation layer beneath cultural participation: broad culture/leisure/media/sports activity was nearly universal, but unpaid domestic and care work was strongly gendered and culture/leisure/media time was lower per female person than per male person. This is diary evidence, not formal cultural attendance or a causal time-displacement estimate.

**Latest inequality-in-cultural-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Mexico INEGI MODECULT 2025 record](records/comparative-mexico-inegi-modecult-2025-cultural-access-disability-education-digital-creation.json) adds explicit age, education, disability, Indigenous-language, event-type, and active-creation gradients. It is a national 12+ expansion with a revised instrument, so it supports an access hypothesis rather than a clean historical trend estimate.

**Latest digital-to-place cultural rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Spain Ministry of Culture habits record](records/comparative-spain-ministry-culture-cultural-habits-2024-2025-digital-active-gender.json) connects routine digital culture to physical participation and active creation: audiovisual and music use were high monthly, while monuments, museums, live events, cinema, photography, painting, and video all showed material annual participation. The source is provisional and does not establish substitution, quality, or causal recovery.

**Latest repeated-participation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Norwegian Cultural Barometer 2025 record](records/comparative-norway-ssb-cultural-barometer-2025-postpandemic-stabilization.json) adds a repeated official survey layer: cinema and theatre remain below 2016 attendance levels, library use has stabilized, children remain strong library users, and unmet demand is associated with money, time, and competing options. The source supports venue-specific recovery and constraint hypotheses, not a causal pandemic or individual-persistence claim.

**Latest direct-cultural-participation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Saudi GASTAT household culture and entertainment record](records/comparative-saudi-gastat-household-culture-entertainment-2024-participation.json) adds a direct attendance/practice split: cultural-venue attendance was 81.6%, entertainment attendance 85.3%, and active cultural practice 39.0%. The venue mix is weighted toward parks, gardens, malls, and public events rather than specialized venues; this is not causal access, affordability, or policy-impact evidence.

**Latest comparative household-culture rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [China NBS 2025 household-consumption record](records/comparative-china-nbs-2025-household-consumption-culture-recreation-urban-rural.json) adds a place-stratified spending layer: education, culture, and recreation spending rose 9.4% to 3,489 yuan per person, while urban spending was 1.85 times rural spending. This is a household-budget signal, not equal cultural access, attendance, quality, or belonging.

**Latest subprime-mobility-remedy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [multistate Credit Acceptance Corporation settlement record](records/us-multistate-cac-subprime-auto-loan-settlement-2026.json) adds a named debt-to-mobility remedy chain. The New York Attorney General reports more than $630 million in debt relief, $60 million in restitution, separate treatment for repossessed and retained vehicles, and forward-looking disclosure and early-off-ramp protections. It establishes a remedy design, not individual receipt, transport recovery, compliance, or durable trust change.

**Latest monitored-remedy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [CFPB Bilt transition record](records/us-cfpb-bilt-transition-redress-2026.json) adds a named service-failure-to-remedy chain. The CFPB reports reimbursement to more than 2,000 eligible consumers, proactive outreach to newly identified customers, technical remediation, continued monitoring, and closure. The record supports a bounded remedy pathway, not universal reach, independent verification, customer satisfaction, or durable trust restoration.

**Latest hidden-time cultural-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Chile ENPCCL 2024 record](records/comparative-chile-enpccl-2024-cultural-participation-time-ses-disability.json) adds a time-and-energy layer to cultural access: 28.7% reported wanting cultural participation but not achieving it, with time/fatigue more common than economic reasons among that group. The sharp socioeconomic and disability gradients keep the mechanism open across care, work, transport, accessibility, and supply.

**Latest transport-accessibility rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada accessibility-and-travel record](records/comparative-statcan-accessibility-travel-barriers-2025-disability-trip-prevention.json) makes the mobility bottleneck observable: 29% of people with disabilities or long-term conditions who considered a trip reported discouragement or prevention from possible accessibility barriers, with a 46% versus 19% daily-activity-difficulty gradient. This is transport-access evidence, not proof of cultural exclusion or provider fault.

**Latest national arts-optionality rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [England DCMS Participation Survey 2024/25 record](records/comparative-england-dcms-participation-survey-2024-25-arts-physical-digital-deprivation.json) adds a population-wide physical/digital and deprivation split: physical arts engagement was 95% in the least deprived areas versus 81% in the most deprived, while digital engagement was 39% versus 31%; 56% engaged physically only. The signal supports unequal cultural optionality, not a causal digital-substitution claim.

**Latest household-adaptation rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ONS August 2026 social-trends record](records/comparative-ons-public-opinions-social-trends-august-2026-cost-water-consumer-adaptation.json) adds current reported behavior under price and drought pressure: non-essential cutbacks, shopping-around, reduced food/essential spending, and widespread water-saving routines. It connects consumer adaptation to infrastructure stress while preserving the distinction between self-reported action and observed spending or water volume.

**Latest observed-spending rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ONS Family Spending FYE 2025 record](records/comparative-ons-family-spending-fye2025-recreation-culture-income-gap.json) adds the transaction-side check: real average household spending rose 5%, but the richest fifth spent 2.7 times the poorest and added over five times as much weekly spending; recreation and culture recovered in aggregate without proving equal access or welfare.

**Latest expectation-to-capacity rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ECB Consumer Expectations Survey August 2026 record](records/comparative-ecb-consumer-expectations-august-2026-income-inflation-spending.json) connects forward-looking pressure to distribution: expected spending growth exceeded expected income growth, while lower-income households anticipated higher unemployment, home-price growth, and mortgage rates. This is an expectations layer, not realized household behavior.

**Latest confidence-reversal rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [European Commission September 2026 consumer-survey record](records/comparative-european-commission-consumer-confidence-september-2026-sentiment-uncertainty.json) adds a harmonised EU sentiment reversal: confidence fell after four months of recovery while inflation expectations and uncertainty rose. It is a macro signal for the open bridge to major purchases and realized household demand, not a transaction result.

**Latest observed-demand rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat July 2026 retail-trade record](records/comparative-eurostat-retail-trade-july-2026-food-nonfood-consumer-demand.json) supplies that transaction-side bridge: food volume rose while non-food and fuel fell month-over-month, even as annual totals remained modestly positive. The category split is evidence of selective demand, not proof of household-level substitution or welfare.

**Latest balance-sheet-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [IMF Japan 2026 household-consumption record](records/comparative-imf-japan-2026-household-debt-consumption-fiscal-shock.json) adds panel evidence that debt and loan-to-value exposure amplify consumption responses to fiscal tightening, while deleveraging reallocates resources away from current consumption. This is a Japan-specific model and mechanism test, not a universal current-demand estimate.

**Latest home-resilience rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [IKEA Life at Home 2026 record](records/comparative-ikea-life-at-home-2026-home-needs-control-comfort-security-climate-action.json) adds a consumer-meaning layer beneath macro pressure: security, comfort, and control are widely important at home but less regularly experienced, while saving money and health benefits motivate climate action. Its next test is a named household or housing episode that follows cost, energy, routines, repair or purchase behavior, social connection, and durable well-being; the commercially commissioned online survey is perception and motivation evidence, not objective housing, income, emissions, or causal outcome evidence.

**Latest home-resilience synthesis rotation — 2026-09-29:** the home-life memo sharpens the subtheme that a home can function as a psychological resilience buffer while failing to deliver the material security, control, or comfort people need. The next bridge follows the need gap into housing conditions, energy burden, time use, domestic routines, and consumer adaptation rather than treating reported home happiness as household welfare.

**Latest discovery-commerce rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [NIQ/World Data Lab discovery-commerce observation](records/comparative-niq-world-data-lab-2026-two-consumers-premium-value-mindsets.json) extends the existing value-polarization record into AI product discovery, social-platform discovery-to-purchase, APAC social/quick-commerce, and retail-media influence. Its next test follows a named product or category from recommendation exposure through verification, price, purchase, quality, return or remedy, repeat choice, and interface control; the commercial toplines do not establish conversion, quality, or consumer welfare.

**Latest child-safety policy-transition rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Snapchat DSA proceeding record](records/comparative-eu-snapchat-child-safety-dsa-proceedings-2026.json) now connects investigation, collective action, and platform redress to the proposed EU KIDS Act's age thresholds and safety-by-design burden shift. Its next test follows rule adoption through age-assurance experience, design change, reporting/remedy, youth participation or substitution, and developmental outcomes; the proposal is not yet law or outcome evidence.

**Latest social-connection implementation rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [WHO social-connection record](records/comparative-who-social-connection-loneliness-2025.json) adds the current policy-conversion checkpoint that eight WHO Member States have adopted policies on social connection, social isolation, or loneliness. Its next test follows one country's policy through funding, local delivery, participation, inclusion, repeat use, and measured connection or health outcomes; adoption is not effectiveness evidence.

**Latest named-country social-policy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Japan Cabinet Office loneliness-and-isolation record](records/comparative-japan-cabinet-office-loneliness-isolation-priority-plan-2026.json) adds a statutory and dated revision route from the 2023 law through the 2024 priority plan and July 10, 2026 partial revision. Its next test follows one municipality or programme through budget, staffing, referral, participant experience, repeat use, inclusion, and measured connection, health, work, or civic outcomes; legal architecture is not service effectiveness.

**Latest household-infrastructure rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Northern Ireland household water-use record](records/comparative-northern-ireland-household-water-usage-2025-26-routines-equipment-deprivation.json) adds a concrete household-adaptation route from showering, laundry, dishwashers, toilets, outdoor equipment, settlement, and deprivation through water demand and environmental infrastructure. The CHS reports 3,459 participating households and 4,004 individuals, but household and individual denominators remain separate; self-reported routines are not metered consumption, bills, or causal welfare evidence. The next test follows one place or household group through actual use, drought or price exposure, equipment, time, and institutional response.

**Latest media-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Ofcom News Consumption Survey record](records/comparative-ofcom-news-consumption-2026-ai-intermediaries-local-trust.json) adds a distribution-and-trust route from online intermediaries, AI summaries, social/video feeds, and local information through source legitimacy, election-time long-form demand, and youth cultural discovery. It reports 73% intermediary access, 21% AI-for-news use, 56% lower trust in AI-written stories, 95% local-news use, and 78% social-news access among children aged 12-15. The 5,397-adult mixed-mode survey changed its definition of news and fieldwork design in 2025-2026; the next test follows a named encounter through verification, correction, civic or consumer action, and durable trust or exit.

**Latest observed-mobility rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [DfT domestic transport-use record](records/comparative-uk-dft-domestic-transport-usage-september-2026-mode-recovery.json) adds a mode-specific operating layer from road traffic, rail, bus, and Tube use through mobility access, time, cost, hybrid-work change, and public-system participation. Motor traffic was indexed at 105% of its pre-COVID comparison while rail was 94%, non-London buses 83%, and London bus/Tube 79%; the index is not a person-level welfare or modal-switching measure. The next test joins observed use to household burden, trip purpose, disability, service availability, care, work, and local participation.

**Latest observed-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada household-spending record](records/comparative-statcan-household-spending-2023-income-tenure-culture-refresh-2026.json) adds a distributional budget layer beneath consumer-confidence and transaction signals. Average 2023 goods-and-services spending was $76,750; shelter, transportation, and food absorbed 32.1%, 15.8%, and 15.7%, while recreation rose 23.9% from 2021. The top income quintile averaged $123,447 versus $40,080 for the bottom, and the bottom quintile devoted 34.8% to shelter and 17.9% to food. These are current-dollar, refreshed-weight household averages, not real consumption, panel transitions, or causal welfare estimates; the next test joins composition to price, quantity, income, debt, participation, and room.

**Latest cultural-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat cultural-participation record](records/comparative-eurostat-cultural-participation-disability-age-2022-refresh-2026.json) adds a harmonized access-gradient route. In Sweden, participation in cinema, live performances, or cultural sites was 66.8% for people reporting no activity limitation, 52.8% for some limitation, and 34.8% for severe limitation; participation was 79.9% among ages 16–24 and 47.2% among ages 65+. These are 2022 EU-SILC cross-sectional percentages, not causal disability effects or measures of cultural belonging; the next test follows accessibility, transport, cost, venue supply, repeat use, and social connection.

**Latest same-instrument social-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Census HTOPS arts/loneliness/income record](records/us-census-htops-arts-loneliness-income-2026.json) adds an unusually useful cross-domain measurement surface. Live performance attendance was 10.6% among adults in households below $35,000 and 37.6% at $150,000 or more; always/usually loneliness was 18.2% and 1.9%, respectively. The release also shows age-patterned in-person contact, but it is an experimental cross-sectional survey with weighted counts and no individual-level linkage between arts and loneliness cells. The next test joins household room, time, transport, accessibility, repeated participation, and connection outcomes.

**Latest diary-time rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ABS Australia time-use record](records/comparative-abs-australia-time-use-2024-disability-carer-free-time-2026.json) adds a diary-based conversion layer. Carers who participated in caring/support averaged 2h45 per day versus 1h46 for non-carers, while participant-level free time averaged 5h25 versus 5h36. The source explicitly warns that two diary days cannot estimate carer prevalence or infrequent-activity participation; the next bridge follows care time into work, mobility, accessibility, social contact, and repeated cultural use.

**Latest cultural-consumer gateway rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat online-event-ticketing record](records/comparative-eurostat-online-event-ticketing-2025-cultural-consumer-gateway.json) adds an observed transaction gateway between digital commerce and live cultural participation. EU online event-ticket purchases reached 26.8% in 2025, up 3.8 percentage points from 2024, while country rates ranged from 51.5% in Ireland to 11.4% in Bulgaria. This is not attendance, household affordability, or cultural-power evidence; the next bridge follows ticket price and fees, event attendance, accessibility, repeat use, and intermediary value capture.

**Latest recurring cultural-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat paid-streaming subscription record](records/comparative-eurostat-paid-streaming-subscriptions-2025-cultural-consumer-access.json) adds a recurring-payment layer: 32.7% of EU internet users had a paid film, series, or sports streaming subscription in 2025, compared with 23.0% for music, 7.2% for online news, and 6.1% for gaming streaming. The film/series/sports rate was 35.6% in cities versus 29.0% in rural areas and ranged from 63.9% in Ireland to 9.3% in Bulgaria. This is subscription presence, not active use, affordability, or creator/provider value; the next bridge follows payment through use, cancellation, discovery, and household room.

**Latest cultural-labor geography rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ONS creative-industries jobs record](records/comparative-ons-creative-industries-jobs-london-regional-demographics-2025.json) adds a place-and-work route: creative jobs were an estimated 13.7% of London jobs versus 5.9% outside London, while London’s creative workforce was 26.0% self-employed and 80.4% degree-or-equivalent by derived workbook shares. APS quality and comparability warnings are central; the next bridge follows concentration into earnings, housing, care, mobility, firm opportunity, worker power, and cultural access.

**Latest cultural-production/access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Census HTOPS arts-production and local-opportunity record](records/us-census-htops-arts-production-local-opportunity-2026.json) adds a practice-and-supply route: personal art creation, practice, or performance was 26.2% among adults 18–34 versus 13.8% among adults 65+, while agreement that local arts opportunities were plentiful was 59.6% below $35,000 household income versus 82.5% at $150,000+. The source is experimental and cross-sectional; the next bridge follows practice and perceived supply through time, transport, price, accessibility, attendance, and local cultural infrastructure.

**Latest real-time cultural-economy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ONS real-time arts-payment-flow record](records/comparative-ons-realtime-arts-payment-flows-edinburgh-festival-2026.json) adds a production-network route: payments from Scottish arts organisations to other UK arts organisations rose 73% in August versus July, while equivalent payments in the rest of the UK fell 40%; Scottish arts payment flows have shown a recurring August peak from 2021–2025. These anonymised payment flows exclude ticket sales and visitor spending and are official statistics in development; the next bridge follows festival production into jobs, artist income, attendance, footfall, and value capture.

**Latest official AI-adoption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat generative-AI use record](records/comparative-eurostat-generative-ai-use-age-purpose-2025.json) adds a harmonized access-and-purpose route: generative-AI use was 65% among EU internet users ages 16–24 versus 14% among ages 55–74; private use was 27%, professional use 16%, and formal education use 10%. Adoption is not capability, trust, productivity, or substitution evidence; the next bridge follows age and purpose through task completion, human support, work control, cultural discovery, and exit.

**Latest enterprise-platform rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat enterprise social-media record](records/comparative-eurostat-enterprise-social-media-size-country-2025.json) adds a firm-side digital-discovery route: social-media use was 60.6% among small EU enterprises, 76.2% among medium enterprises, and 89.1% among large enterprises, with country shares ranging from 88.2% in Malta to 41.5% in Bulgaria. This is enterprise presence, not consumer reach or conversion; the next bridge follows size and place into discovery, sales, labor burden, and platform concentration.

**Latest platform-experience rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat hostile-online-content record](records/comparative-eurostat-hostile-online-content-age-gender-2025.json) adds an exposure-and-safety route: 53.7% of EU internet users ages 16–24 encountered hostile or degrading content, including 57.2% of young women versus 50.4% of young men. This is exposure, not direct victimization, severity, platform response, or harm; the next bridge follows exposure through reporting, moderation, identity safety, civic/cultural participation, and exit.

**Latest youth-material-capacity rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat youth-overcrowding record](records/comparative-eurostat-youth-overcrowding-age-country-2025.json) adds a household-room route: 26.9% of EU young people ages 15–29 lived in overcrowded households versus 16.8% of the total population, with country rates from 60.4% in Romania to 3.5% in Cyprus. This is an objective room-standard measure, not privacy, rent burden, homelessness, or causal cultural behavior; the next bridge follows room into study/work capacity, care, digital use, cultural practice, and consumer autonomy.

**Latest youth-autonomy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Eurostat parental-home exit record](records/comparative-eurostat-youth-parental-home-exit-employment-2025.json) adds a household-formation route: average parental-home exit was 26.3 years in the EU, ranging from 31.5 in Croatia to 21.4 in Finland, with earlier exit generally associated with higher youth employment across countries. This is an aggregate transition indicator, not causal employment or independence evidence; the next bridge follows exit through rent, overcrowding, education, work, care, privacy, and consumer autonomy.

**Latest place-by-category consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Census Monthly State Retail Sales record](records/us-census-monthly-state-retail-sales-experimental-2026.json) adds modeled state/category dispersion: June 2026 total retail growth ranged from -7.6% in Alaska to +11.3% in Louisiana, while sporting-goods/books/music retail ranged from -9.6% in D.C. to +24.8% in Alaska. It is nominal experimental sales growth, not real-volume, welfare, or individual-consumer evidence; the September revision note remains part of the control boundary.

**Latest cultural-social-material tension rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [ABS General Social Survey 2025 record](records/comparative-abs-general-social-survey-2025-cultural-participation-financial-stress-connection.json) adds a same-instrument bridge: cultural attendance was 79% and cultural participation 48%; weekly outside-household face-to-face contact rose from 41.3% in 2020 to 52.2% in 2025, while cash-flow problems rose from 20.7% to 25.3% and dissaving from 22.9% to 25.7%. The bridge preserves repeated-cross-section, questionnaire-change, and noncausal boundaries.

**Latest cultural-production labor rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada National Culture Indicators record](records/comparative-statcan-national-culture-indicators-q1-2026-production-jobs.json) adds a production-side divergence: real culture GDP grew 0.6% in Q1 2026 while culture jobs fell 0.3%; broadcasting GDP rose 1.6% while broadcasting jobs fell 1.8%, and film/video jobs fell 2.3%. The indicators are national economic accounts, not worker-level or audience-outcome evidence.

**Latest real-consumption composition rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Japan Statistics Bureau CTI record](records/comparative-japan-statistics-consumption-trend-index-july-2026-real-category-household.json) adds modeled real-versus-nominal and household-type dispersion: real consumption fell 2.4% year-over-year in July 2026 versus a nominal decline of 0.1%, with education down 11.8% and utilities down 8.7%; single-person households fell 1.6% versus 2.2% for multi-person households. The index is revised and aggregate, not household-level welfare evidence.

**Latest accessibility-to-consumer rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada disability-accessibility record](records/comparative-statcan-disability-accessibility-digital-consumer-barriers-2022-2024.json) adds the interface stage: online barriers affect government access, booking, banking, and shopping, with Internet-use barriers at 28% for very severe disabilities versus 9% for mild disabilities. It preserves the self-report, cross-sectional, disability-definition, and non-transaction-outcome boundaries.

**Latest culture audience/creator rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [New Zealand cultural-participation record](records/comparative-new-zealand-cultural-participation-2025-access-creation-ai-volunteering.json) adds audience/creator/digital conversion: attendance reached 88% while active creation fell to 40%, 72% named accessibility or convenience as a participation enabler, and 65% of digital-tool-using creators used generative AI. It preserves the online-panel, self-report, cross-wave, and noncausal boundaries.

**Latest age/place cultural-behavior rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Denmark Cultural Habits record](records/comparative-statistics-denmark-cultural-habits-q2-2026-age-urban-games-museums.json) adds quarterly age and urbanisation splits: digital games show a 55-point age gap, while museum participation is much flatter; cinema and concert participation show larger dense-to-thin area differences than Danish heritage-site visits. The questionnaire redesign and cross-sectional boundaries remain explicit.

**Latest digital-access infrastructure rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Malaysia DOSM ICT-access record](records/comparative-malaysia-dosm-ict-access-2025-urban-rural-device-gap.json) adds a Southeast Asian device and place layer: household Internet access reached 97.1%, but remained 99.0% in urban areas versus 90.7% in rural areas; individual mobile use was 99.6% versus 81.5% computer use. This is access/use infrastructure, not evidence of online preference, spending, or service quality.

**Latest household-consumption rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [China NBS H1 2026 consumption record](records/comparative-china-nbs-household-consumption-education-culture-recreation-h1-2026.json) adds a catch-up-without-parity pattern: rural per-capita consumption grew 4.6% year over year versus 3.0% urban, and rural education/culture/recreation spending grew 5.4% versus 4.3%, while rural levels remained 7,891 yuan lower overall and 927 yuan lower in that category. The combined category is not a pure cultural-participation measure.

**Latest participation-and-trust rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Korea 2025 Social Survey record](records/comparative-kostat-south-korea-social-survey-2025-leisure-trust-participation.json) adds a useful tension: cultural/art/sport venue attendance rose to 57.7%, domestic travel to 70.2%, organization participation to 69.2%, and volunteering to 14.4%, while generalized social trust fell to 54.6%; loneliness was 38.2%. These are descriptive survey movements, not evidence that participation caused or failed to cause trust.

**Latest local cultural-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [England DCMS museums-and-galleries record](records/comparative-england-dcms-museums-galleries-2025-26-local-physical-digital-gap.json) adds local-authority and modality structure: physical engagement ranged from 75% in the City of London to 25% in Blackburn with Darwen, while digital engagement ranged from 29% in Southwark to 9% in Havant. Most engagement was physical-only; digital access is growing but not a simple replacement for place-based attendance.

**Latest affordability-and-channel rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Poland culture-participation record](records/comparative-poland-statistics-culture-participation-2024-spending-streaming-access.json) connects household budgets to cultural channels: nominal cultural spending rose 21.8%, yet 16.8% of households gave up cinema for financial reasons; paid film streaming reached 48.1% but ranged from 55.1% urban to 36.7% rural and 77.0% at ages 25–34 versus 15.3% at 65+. The category and self-report boundaries remain explicit.

**Latest Latin American digital-access rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Mexico INEGI ENDUTIH record](records/comparative-mexico-inegi-endutih-2025-internet-age-rural-indigenous-device-gap.json) adds a large-population digital trajectory: Internet use reached 86.1%, the urban-rural gap narrowed to 13.7 points, but mobile use was 84.6% versus 38.1% computer/tablet use; Internet use was 97.6% at ages 15–24 versus 30.0% at 75+. It preserves the distinction between access, device, identity, and online behavior.

**Latest hybrid-sociality rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Statistics Canada social-connections record](records/comparative-statcan-social-connections-2025-virtual-inperson-friendship-satisfaction.json) adds a social-outcome layer: 87.8% used both virtual and in-person friend contact, while virtual-only contact was associated with lower friendship satisfaction (63% versus 87%), weaker dependable support (61% versus 79%), and more loneliness (24% versus 15%). The source explicitly warns that these associations are noncausal.

**Latest civic-action rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [England Community and Engagement civic-action record](records/comparative-govuk-community-engagement-civic-action-place-2025-26.json) adds the missing participation-to-voice stage. Civic engagement reached 46% in 2025/26, but was 55% among higher managerial/professional groups and 39% among routine/manual or never-worked/long-term-unemployed groups; local-authority estimates ranged from 61% to 17%. This is self-reported, one-or-more-times civic activity, not evidence of influence, response, or durable power; the next test follows a named local issue through invitation, action, official response, and later trust.

**Latest perceived-efficacy rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [England local-influence record](records/comparative-govuk-community-engagement-local-influence-efficacy-2025-26.json) adds the legitimacy middle stage. Fifty-nine percent said local influence was important, but only 26% felt they could personally influence decisions and 29% wanted more council involvement. The leading stated routes were online petitions and council contact; the leading enabling conditions were knowing the issue and being able to respond online or by email. This is perceived access, not institutional response or decision change; the next bridge must follow a named local decision through notice, contribution, response, and repeat trust.

**Latest named-response rotation — 2026-09-30:** the [Stockton-on-Tees Yarm public-engagement decision record](records/uk-stockton-yarm-public-engagement-decision-scrutiny-2026.json) adds a concrete participation-to-accountability episode. The published record says engagement directly informed a decision not to proceed with a proposed High Street scheme; remaining funding was reallocated, scrutiny challenged the presentation and interpretation of engagement, and Cabinet reconsidered and confirmed the decision. This is a council-reported procedural response, not proof of representative opinion, completed investment, or restored trust; the next test follows the retained funding and further engagement.

## Broad rotation checkpoint — 2026-09-29

The current session deliberately rotated across the broad cultural, social,
consumer, technology, infrastructure, and legitimacy scope rather than treating
one household dataset as the program. Four linked cross-source memos now form
the active synthesis surface:

- [Household optionality, value, culture, and trust](projects/cross-domain/us-household-optionality-value-culture-trust-2026-09-29.md)
  follows price pressure through substitution, protected social consumption,
  unpaid decision labor, value judgments, safety routines, and attribution.
- [Social connection, belonging, and public life](projects/cross-domain/us-social-connection-belonging-public-life-2026-09-29.md)
  separates practical neighborhood trust from formal participation and local influence.
- [AI-mediated judgment, work, and trust](projects/cross-domain/us-ai-mediated-judgment-work-trust-2026-09-29.md)
  follows adoption through verification, privacy, time savings, displacement fear,
  human fallback, and correction.
- [AI infrastructure, local cost, and legitimacy](projects/cross-domain/us-ai-infrastructure-local-legitimacy-household-costs-2026-09-29.md)
  follows national capability claims through local consent, energy demand,
  transmission, cost allocation, household relief, and realized legitimacy.

The consolidated [broad rotation readout](projects/cross-domain/us-broad-rotation-readout-2026-09-29.md)
identifies unequal optionality and conditional trust as the current cross-domain
interpretive pattern. This is a synthesis checkpoint, not a closure condition;
the next rotation should add another source family or open middle-stage arrow
while preserving the 14-theme scope and source-specific boundaries.

**Infrastructure-to-firm adaptation handoff — 2026-09-30:** the NYC congestion-
pricing episode now follows dedicated mitigation funding into an Off-Hour
Deliveries incentive program. The program announcement specifies a $5 million
funding pool, a first phase for up to 14 businesses, and a 7 p.m.–6 a.m.
delivery window, but does not yet establish participation, payment receipt,
delivery shifts, worker schedules, or neighborhood outcomes. This extends the
episode from traffic and transit operation into firm adaptation without claiming
completed public benefit.

**Consumer-remedy freshness handoff — 2026-09-30:** the BrightSpeed route now
has a machine-readable recheck of the CFPB case page and payments index. The
case remains marked “Ongoing: Distribution,” and the index was modified
September 28, 2026 while still classifying it as a Civil Penalty Fund case.
The FY2024 financial report adds 122,507 initial checks mailed and $53,885,244
distributed; the CFPB's geographic map reports $53,335,244.07. The
$549,999.93 difference remains unreconciled, and neither source proves checks
were cashed or that consumers recovered their losses.

**Care-infrastructure usability handoff — 2026-09-30:** the NYC universal
school-meals record now incorporates FY2025 city performance indicators for
daily breakfast and lunch service, student satisfaction, and halal/kosher
certification. This adds a current usability and cultural-fit signal beside
historical student-level learning evidence while preserving the boundary that
aggregate satisfaction and meals served are not unique-student consumption,
household food security, or current causal learning effects.

**AI legitimacy handoff — 2026-09-29:** the [SSRS/CNN AI fear and regulation
record](records/comparative-ssrs-cnn-ai-fear-regulation-public-legitimacy-2026.json)
adds a probability-based public-attitude layer to the AI work, consumer, and
infrastructure lanes. The accompanying [synthesis](projects/cross-domain/ssrs-cnn-ai-fear-regulation-public-legitimacy-2026-09-29.md)
interprets fear and broad regulatory demand as a control-and-recourse signal,
not as evidence that adoption has stopped or that regulation has improved
outcomes. The next test is a named AI use case joined to actual exposure,
benefit, human review, correction, remedy, and later trust or exit.

**Worker-transition synthesis handoff — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup workplace
manager synthesis](projects/cross-domain/gallup-ai-workplace-manager-buffer-displacement-2026-09-29.md)
adds the missing organizational middle between AI exposure and worker
legitimacy: perceived respect and organizational care are associated with a
smaller displacement-fear relationship among frequent users, while fear also
co-moves with burnout, satisfaction, engagement, and job-search pressure. The
panel is observational; the next test is a named workplace deployment joined
to task redesign, training, wages, retention, or actual job transition.

**Intergenerational-security handoff — 2026-09-29:** the [CBO Social Security
projection synthesis](projects/cross-domain/cbo-social-security-long-term-projections-2026-trust-fund-2032-intergenerational-contract-2026-09-29.md)
adds a fiscal-capacity route from a projected OASI trust-fund deadline to
retirement expectations, work, saving, family transfers, care, and political
legitimacy. The 2032 date is a conditional current-law projection, not a
forecast of benefit cuts or household behavior. The next test joins age- and
income-specific expectations to saving, retirement timing, unpaid care,
employer benefits, and a concrete legislative or administrative episode.

**Social Security mechanism synthesis — 2026-09-29:** the [information,
family-mobility, and legitimacy synthesis](projects/cross-domain/us-social-security-information-family-mobility-legitimacy-2026-09-29.md)
adds two stronger middle stages beside the CBO projection. NBER evidence links
personalized DI/OAI amounts to increased applications among older adults with
health or work limitations, and historical linked records estimate that
parental coverage changed sons' later migration, earnings, and neighborhoods.
The synthesis keeps application separate from award/receipt and historical
effects separate from contemporary transferability; the next test requires a
current route through information, award, payment adequacy, care or work
choice, and later household security.

**Latest digital-middle rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [encounter,
interpretation, action, and trust memo](projects/cross-domain/us-digital-encounter-interpretation-action-trust-2026-09-29.md)
connects cultural discoverability, AI-mediated election authenticity, and
post-moderation/local-trust evidence. It identifies an encounter-to-action
conversion problem: availability or reach is not verification, owned
relationship, correction, participation, or exit. The next test joins an
identifiable content encounter to first-party or administrative evidence of
verification, click/visit/share/vote/complaint, correction, repeat action, and
trust, while preserving subgroup and source-unit boundaries.

**Latest geopolitical-response rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [operation,
external response, and public permission memo](projects/cross-domain/us-geopolitical-operation-response-legitimacy-2026-09-29.md)
follows a Baltic drone interception, NATO airspace incidents and information
contestation, KFOR local-security integration, and Pew alliance-legitimacy
attitudes. It adds the response stage while keeping deterrence, adversary
adaptation, host-community consent, burden sharing, and durable leverage open.
The next test requires a dated event-country-party ledger with an attributed
before/after response and an explicit public or host-community denominator.

**Latest consumer-agency rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [payment, AI, and
credit mediation memo](projects/cross-domain/us-consumer-agency-payment-ai-credit-2026-09-29.md)
connects Federal Reserve payment diaries, financial-resilience buffers,
AI-shopping verification, agentic-commerce adoption, and AI-content trust. It
