finds conditional consumer agency: households preserve optionality through
cash, verification, and bounded delegation while credit and mediation can move
search, error, and future-cost burdens onto them. The next test joins one
consumer task to observable payment, recommendation, purchase, return/debt,
and repeat-use or exit data.

**Latest time-conversion rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [family care, work
flexibility, and participation memo](projects/cross-domain/us-time-conversion-family-care-participation-2026-09-29.md)
connects parent time aspirations, leave and childcare access, unpaid-work
production, and menopause information/care navigation. It finds that formal
options become usable time only when money, information, replacement care,
transport, and schedule control align. The next test follows one dated family
or worker care need through actual hours, cost, waiting, continuity, work,
health, and social participation.

**Latest health-cost rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [health cost as household
political economy memo](projects/cross-domain/us-health-cost-care-delay-household-politics-2026-09-29.md)
connects dated MEPS medical events, coverage denials and appeals, KFF care and
basic-needs trade-offs, and rural political attribution. It follows health
costs through payment, delay, debt, work pressure, remedy, trust, and public
judgment while keeping source units separate. The next test joins one dated
care need to coverage, resolution time, completed care, debt/work effects,
remedy, and later attribution or action.

**Latest cultural-consumer rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [identity-bearing
consumption memo](projects/cross-domain/cultural-consumer-identity-heritage-human-trust-2026-09-29.md)
connects youth vintage and anti-algorithm distinction, flexible restorative
travel and human trust, and football heritage as place-based fan voice. It
finds that value is increasingly authenticated through discovery, ritual,
belonging, and agency while firms and regulators try to commercialize or
protect those meanings. The next test follows one identity-bearing choice
through attendance or purchase, repeat participation, institutional response,
and durable loyalty or exit.

**Latest platform-data-control rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [platform, data,
control, and exit memo](projects/cross-domain/us-platform-data-control-exit-2026-09-29.md)
follows privacy and deletion rights through request handling, completion,
verification, service trade-offs, trust, switching, and exit. The rotation
finds that formal control rights are expanding faster than verified durable
user-level control; the next test follows a named request from submission to
actual deletion or opt-out, residual data use, service consequence, remedy,
and durable trust or exit.

**Latest time-and-care rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [time, care, and social reproduction memo](projects/cross-domain/us-time-care-social-reproduction-2026-09-29.md)
follows unpaid care through time allocation, replacement-care scarcity,
transport, work flexibility, income, health, and social participation. It
finds that care is productive infrastructure and that household flexibility
depends on money, schedule control, reliable replacement care, and trusted
relationships; the next test follows a named caregiver episode through care
hours, work changes, out-of-pocket cost, service access, health, and continuity.

**Latest consumer-recourse rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [consumer recourse, remedy, effort, and exit memo](projects/cross-domain/us-consumer-recourse-remedy-effort-exit-2026-09-29.md)
follows a problem from detection through complaint, waiting, effort, actual
receipt, adequacy, residual loss, switching, and restored trust. The rotation
finds that institutional visibility is improving faster than verified recovery;
the next test follows a named denial, fraud, or billing episode from complaint
to realized remedy and subsequent use, debt, switching, or political judgment.

**Latest housing-place-mobility rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [housing, place, mobility, and optionality memo](projects/cross-domain/us-housing-place-mobility-optionality-2026-09-29.md)
follows housing cost and insurance, transport access, moving or staying,
family geography, work, care, social ties, and opportunity. It finds that
movement is not automatically opportunity: moving can raise costs and break
care or social ties, while staying can preserve relationships but limit access;
the next test follows a named household or place episode through housing cost,
insurance, travel, move or stay, work, care, social connection, and political
attribution.

**Latest tariff-and-consumer rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Ipsos Consumer Tracker record](records/comparative-ipsos-consumer-tracker-september-2026-tariffs-price-politics-trust.json) adds a route from trade policy through visible prices, product value, substitution, safety trust, household room, firm legitimacy, and political attribution. The public tracker summary reports 81% expecting tariff-linked price increases and 71% saying they had already seen such increases; it is perception evidence, not product-level tariff incidence or observed purchasing. The next test follows a named product category or household panel through prices, substitution, quantity, debt, complaint, and political judgment.

**Latest tariff-and-consumer memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Ipsos tariff-and-consumer memo](projects/cross-domain/ipsos-consumer-tracker-september-2026-tariffs-price-politics-trust-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that trade policy becomes politically legible through receipts, product value, and trust in protection systems.

**Latest geopolitical-household rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Ukraine living-conditions record](records/comparative-world-bank-ukraine-living-conditions-spring-2026-poverty-resilience.json) adds a route from war, displacement, infrastructure and price shocks through food variety, income, care, informality, disability, social assistance, work access, and household recovery. The source combines modeled estimates and repeated phone surveys, not a causal counterfactual or fully linked panel; the next test follows a named household or subgroup from exposure through benefit receipt, care, housing, work, income recovery, and political attribution.

**Latest geopolitical-household memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Ukraine memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-ukraine-living-conditions-spring-2026-resilience-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that war is experienced through ordinary decisions about food, heating, care, mobility, work, and debt, with public legitimacy tested by restored capability rather than eligibility alone.

**Latest platform-work and financial-health rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO UNGA81 record](records/comparative-ilo-unga81-platform-work-financial-health-2026.json) adds a route from platform and transformed work through income volatility, algorithmic control, workplace finance, social protection, worker voice, and household room. The source is an international agenda and event account, not evidence of platform-worker outcomes or domestic implementation; the next test follows a named worker cohort through allocation, pay, fees, financial-service use, protection, complaint, bargaining, and remedy.

**Latest platform-work and financial-health memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO UNGA81 memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-unga81-platform-work-financial-health-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that financial health is becoming part of work design and institutional responsibility, while workplace-linked finance can also create data, coercion, fee, and dependency risks.

**Latest ASEAN trade-and-work rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO ASEAN labour-rights record](records/comparative-ilo-asean-trade-labour-rights-supply-chain-competitiveness-2026.json) adds a route from trade and buyer requirements through supplier adaptation, labour visibility, worker voice, inspection, enforcement, remedy, continued listing, and regional legitimacy. The source is a regional policy dialogue and study announcement, not worker-level implementation or outcome evidence; the next test follows a named supply chain from clause or audit through worker awareness, complaint, correction, remedy, prices, and community effects.

**Latest ASEAN trade-and-work memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO ASEAN labour-rights memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-asean-trade-labour-rights-supply-chain-competitiveness-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that labour standards are becoming market-entry infrastructure, while the open middle is whether due diligence changes workplace power or merely expands paperwork and supplier control.

**Latest consumer-sentiment rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [European Commission consumer-confidence record](records/comparative-european-commission-consumer-confidence-september-2026-economic-sentiment-recovery-reversal.json) adds a route from prices, employment risk, and uncertainty through expectations, precaution, purchase timing, substitution, saving, borrowing, household room, and political attribution. The September aggregate indicators show renewed weakening after four months of recovery, but they are not household-level welfare or causal evidence; the next test follows a named country or panel from sentiment through realized income, prices, spending, debt, housing, participation, and political judgment.

**Latest consumer-sentiment memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [European Commission consumer-confidence memo](projects/cross-domain/european-commission-consumer-confidence-september-2026-recovery-reversal-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that confidence is a social forecast about whether ordinary commitments feel safe, while household room is revealed through actual spending composition, debt, housing, time, and participation.

**Latest government-AI public-capacity rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank government-AI record](records/comparative-world-bank-government-ai-public-service-scale-evaluation-2026.json) adds a route from public-service shortages and administrative friction through local data, connectivity, evaluation, procurement, vendor independence, staff capacity, auditability, correction, and durable legitimacy. The World Bank evidence is a cross-economy survey, policy framework, and selected implementation examples, not proof of equitable citizen outcomes; its next test follows a named service user through AI-assisted contact, waiting, decision quality, explanation, appeal, correction, and repeat access.

**Latest named-agency AI implementation rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [SSA AI and disability-service record](records/us-ssa-ai-disability-service-oversight-2026.json) instantiates that route in a U.S. benefits agency. SSA reports AI-enhanced IVR, chatbot and disability-evidence processing, while SSA OIG lists a current AI-use audit and SSA publishes selected disability-denial review fields. The route remains open between implementation and claimant outcome: no public same-person link yet joins tool exposure, waiting, explanation, appeal, correction, payment, health, work, or household security.

**Latest government-AI public-capacity memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank government-AI memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-government-ai-public-service-scale-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that AI becomes public capacity only when institutions can distinguish useful systems from demonstrations and preserve public control over maintenance, data, vendors, and remedy.

**Latest social-protection-coverage rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO social-protection floors record](records/comparative-ilo-social-protection-floors-informal-workers-systems-2026.json) adds a route from informal work and social risk through legal entitlement, data, coordination, enrolment, contribution systems, worker organization, benefit access, and household resilience. The ILO reports more than 9 million people with improved access across six countries, but this is programme reporting rather than causal or independently verified outcome evidence; its next test follows a named country and worker group from eligibility and enrolment through contributions, claim, payment, adequacy, continuity, complaint, and post-project survival.

**Latest social-protection-coverage memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO social-protection floors memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-social-protection-floors-informal-workers-systems-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that coverage is a conversion problem: rights become household security only when data, enrolment, payment, worker voice, adequate benefits, continuity, and remedy work together beyond standard employment.

**Latest gender-pay-gap rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO gender-pay-gap record](records/comparative-ilo-gender-pay-gap-2026-care-segregation-bargaining.json) adds a route from unequal care and labour-market access through occupational sorting, progression, undervaluation, bargaining asymmetry, pay gaps, household room, and economic voice. Women still earn around 18% less than men globally and the gap narrowed only two percentage points since 2018; this is an ILO estimate summary, not a causal decomposition. Its next test follows a named occupation, employer, or sector through pay-setting, care time, hours, progression, bargaining, transparency, enforcement, and household outcomes.

**Latest gender-pay-gap memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO gender-pay-gap memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-gender-pay-gap-2026-care-segregation-bargaining.md) sharpens the subtheme that equal pay is a conversion problem: formal rights become lived economic power only when care systems, job valuation, progression, bargaining, transparency, and social protection move together.

**Latest fisheries-labour-governance rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO/RPOA-IUU fisheries record](records/comparative-ilo-rpoa-iuu-fisheries-labour-exploitation-responsible-supply-chain-2026.json) adds a route from cost-cutting, weak enforcement, and hidden labour exploitation through crew welfare, labour/fisheries coordination, standards, inspection, safe migration, and responsible seafood supply-chain legitimacy. It is a workshop and programme announcement, not a worker-outcome or IUU-prevalence study; its next test follows a named vessel, port, fleet, or seafood chain from recruitment and movement through onboard conditions, labour inspection, vessel legality, buyer due diligence, traceability, complaint, remedy, and community effects.

**Latest fisheries-labour-governance memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO/RPOA-IUU fisheries memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-rpoa-iuu-fisheries-labour-exploitation-responsible-supply-chain-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that responsible fisheries now includes worker dignity and living conditions, but formal standards matter only when translated into onboard safety, social protection, voice, inspection, and remedy.

**Latest forced-labour-trade-remedy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO–Canada PRIME PH record](records/comparative-ilo-philippines-forced-labour-trade-remedy-responsible-business-2026.json) adds a route from hidden forced-labour risk, informality, and economic pressure through worker voice, institutional detection, justice and remedy, firm due diligence, import-market rules, and trade legitimacy. It documents a four-year project launch, not prevalence or impact evidence; its next test follows a named Philippine export chain from recruitment and subcontracting through working conditions, complaint, investigation, remedy, buyer due diligence, import decisions, prices, and community effects.

**Latest forced-labour-trade-remedy memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO–Canada PRIME PH memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-philippines-forced-labour-trade-remedy-responsible-business-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that responsible business is moving toward a cross-border legitimacy chain, but market pressure matters only when it reaches worker voice, safe detection, remedy, and enforceable institutional change.

**Latest Pacific-labour-governance rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Pacific labour-governance record](records/comparative-ilo-pacific-regional-labour-governance-mobility-skills-2026.json) adds a route from island labour-market constraints and mobility through regional economic strategy, a Labour Ministers Meeting, tripartite dialogue, skills, social protection, and workforce resilience. It documents political endorsement and intended architecture, not operating performance or worker outcomes; its next test follows a named Pacific labour-mobility or skills episode through recruitment, movement, recognition, employment conditions, protection, family impacts, regional deliberation, and remedy.

**Latest Pacific-labour-governance memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Pacific labour-governance memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-pacific-regional-labour-governance-mobility-skills-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that labour mobility is becoming regional public infrastructure, but a new forum will matter only if it produces worker voice, skills portability, social protection, family continuity, and remedy rather than coordination alone.

**Latest creator-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UNESCO South Africa digital-creators record](records/comparative-unesco-south-africa-digital-creators-information-legitimacy-online-harm-2026.json) adds a route from creator visibility and platform distribution through information interpretation, metric and commercial incentives, verification, AI disclosure, online harm, audience trust, and platform governance. The research is a small-sample, self-reported survey and interview synthesis, not causal evidence; its next test follows a named creator cohort from production and distribution through disclosure, trust, harassment, moderation, income, support, and actual public influence.

**Latest creator-legitimacy memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UNESCO digital-creators memo](projects/cross-domain/unesco-south-africa-digital-creators-information-legitimacy-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that creators are becoming information intermediaries faster than the support systems around them can provide verification, ethics, safety, AI transparency, and meaningful platform voice.

**Latest ageing-migrant-life-course rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO STREAM ageing migrant record](records/comparative-ilo-ageing-migrant-workers-gulf-social-protection-portability-2026.json) adds a route from temporary labour migration through long-term and multigenerational settlement, employer-linked protection, retirement income, healthcare, housing, residency, family care, and cross-border portability. The paper focuses on lower- and middle-income Indian migrant families in the UAE and is a qualitative/policy synthesis, not Gulf-wide prevalence or causal evidence; its next test follows a named migration corridor from recruitment and payroll through contributions, healthcare, residence, housing, retirement, return or settlement, and actual benefit portability.

**Latest ageing-migrant-life-course memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO ageing migrant memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-ageing-migrant-workers-gulf-social-protection-portability-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that portability is life-course infrastructure: a worker can be mobile at recruitment and employment yet become administratively stranded in later life when benefits, healthcare, housing, residence, and family ties do not travel together.

**Latest child-safety-online rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [European Commission Special Panel record](records/comparative-eu-child-safety-online-developmental-social-contract-2026.json) adds a developmental social-contract route from commercial attention architecture through age-appropriate autonomy, privacy-preserving age assurance, safety by design, child participation, complaint remedy, and offline cultural and social infrastructure. The report recommends a harmonised EU-wide restriction below age 13 and shifts proof of safety toward providers, but it is an independent co-chair policy synthesis rather than official Commission position or outcome evidence. Its next test follows a named platform or jurisdiction from rule adoption through age-assurance experience, design change, complaint and remedy, youth participation, offline substitution, and measured developmental or social outcomes.

**Latest child-safety-online memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [child-safety online memo](projects/cross-domain/eu-child-safety-online-developmental-social-contract-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that digital childhood is becoming shared social infrastructure: platform design, privacy, family time, public enforcement, child voice, complaint routes, and offline community capacity are one contested governance chain.

**Latest disability-inclusive-employment rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO disability employment services record](records/comparative-ilo-disability-employment-services-digital-inclusion-2026.json) adds a route from disability-related labour-market barriers through person-centred public employment services, digital matching, reasonable accommodation, assistive technology, retention, progression, and workplace agency. The source maps practice and mutual learning rather than measuring causal employment gains; its next test follows a named person or employer from intake and matching through accommodation, workplace experience, retention, progression, and remedy.

**Latest disability-inclusive-employment memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO disability employment memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-disability-employment-services-digital-inclusion-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that inclusion is not achieved at placement: digital and public-service design must carry accessibility, accommodation, retention, progression, and worker agency into the workplace.

**Latest completed refugee-livelihood-conversion rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UNHCR Uganda Q2 livelihoods dashboard record](records/comparative-unhcr-uganda-refugee-livelihoods-resilience-q2-2026.json) adds a route from refugee and host-community vulnerability through land, productive inputs, agricultural and market skills, producer organizations, employer links, finance, documentation, formalization, and self-reliance. The sector reports $27.8M received against a $153M requirement, 0.6 acres per supported household against a 1.2-acre self-reliance threshold, and subgroup variation from 0.2 acres for Congolese households to 0.9 for Sudanese households. The dashboard is partner reporting, not causal or representative household evidence. Its next test follows a settlement or livelihood group through land, market sale, finance, documentation, employment retention, consumption, and refugee-host relations.

**Latest refugee-livelihood-conversion memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UNHCR Uganda memo](projects/cross-domain/unhcr-uganda-refugee-livelihoods-resilience-q2-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that self-reliance is a conversion gap: inputs and training do not become autonomy without land, buyers, finance, documentation, legal work access, and household room.

**Latest completed employment-centred-trade rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Lebanon METI record](records/comparative-ilo-lebanon-meti-trade-investment-employment-policy-2026.json) adds a route from trade and investment choices through sector/value-chain selection, employment analysis, tripartite coordination, MSME and worker support, skills, gender, formalization, and regional integration. The project establishes a Policy Working Group and plans a sectoral action plan, but it does not yet establish job creation, wages, firm performance, or worker outcomes. Its next test follows the selected Lebanese value chain from evidence and finance through firm behaviour, skills, worker conditions, gender access, formalization, and realized employment.

**Latest employment-centred-trade memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO METI Lebanon memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-lebanon-meti-trade-investment-employment-policy-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that trade and investment gain legitimacy only when sector selection, firm support, skills, worker voice, and employment outcomes are designed as one policy chain.

**Latest completed climate-to-work-conversion rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Jobs in a Changing Climate record](records/comparative-world-bank-jobs-changing-climate-93-economies-2026.json) adds a route from climate exposure through sectoral and regional job change, adaptation and low-emission investment, skills, worker reallocation, firm adaptation, and social protection. The synthesis estimates 43 million equivalent job losses by 2050 across 49 analyzed countries and 25 million equivalent adaptation jobs, with larger figures extrapolated globally; these are modelled and extrapolated estimates, not realized worker outcomes. Its next test follows a named country or sector from climate financing and procurement through transition, household income, regional services, and political response.

**Latest climate-to-work-conversion memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank climate/jobs memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-jobs-changing-climate-93-economies-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that resilience is a labour-market conversion problem: adaptation only becomes broadly protective when capital, skills, mobility, firm adaptation, and social protection connect those losing work to the places and sectors receiving investment.

**Latest completed whole-system-education rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UNICEF Education Strategy record](records/comparative-unicef-education-strategy-2026-learning-system-failures-ai-teachers.json) adds a route from household, crisis, climate, migration, and technology constraints through access and continuity, teacher conditions, system feedback, responsible AI/EdTech, learning, protection, and future work. UNICEF targets 350 million children and young people learning and acquiring skills by 2030, but the strategy is an institutional roadmap, not achieved outcomes or causal evidence. Its next test follows a country or crisis episode through financing, teacher conditions, access, learning, technology use, protection, household burden, and future transition.

**Latest whole-system-education memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UNICEF education memo](projects/cross-domain/unicef-education-strategy-2026-learning-system-failures-ai-teachers-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that education legitimacy depends on converting time in school into learning, protection, belonging, usable skills, and future agency under crisis and technological change.

**Latest completed living-wage-governance rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO living-wage partnership record](records/comparative-ilo-living-wage-public-private-partnerships-2026.json) adds a route from household and family costs through common wage estimation, national data, tripartite institutions, multinational supply chains, company roadmaps, and worker bargaining. The ILO signed three first public-private agreements with the Responsible Business Alliance, Unilever, and amfori; amfori's network covers 2,400 member companies in more than 50 countries. The announcement documents intended architecture, not wage increases, worker welfare, purchasing-practice change, or supply-chain compliance. Its next test follows a named sourcing country or sector from estimate through bargaining, roadmap, payroll, household consumption, and remedy.

**Latest living-wage-governance memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO living-wage memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-living-wage-public-private-partnerships-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that living-wage claims are becoming a shared data-and-bargaining infrastructure connecting family costs, public wage institutions, supply-chain firms, and consumer-facing legitimacy.

**Latest completed cooperative-data rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Lebanon cooperative record](records/comparative-ilo-lebanon-cooperative-data-digital-modernization-2026.json) adds a route from fragmented paper records through cooperative visibility, public capacity, digital administration, finance, market access, refugee-host livelihoods, and worker protection. Lebanon has around 850 registered cooperatives, with nationwide data not comprehensively updated since 2007; associated ILO cooperation reports 400 subsidized loans worth USD 1.33 million, around 2,000 jobs created or sustained, and more than 1,000 farmers and workers reaching new buyers or international markets. The announcement does not establish cooperative survival, member power, job quality, or digital-service outcomes. Its next test follows a named cooperative or district through requalification, registry inclusion, finance, digital use, member voice, buyers, wages, protection, and survival.

**Latest cooperative-data memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Lebanon cooperative memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-lebanon-cooperative-data-digital-modernization-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that public data infrastructure determines which rural livelihoods become legible, financeable, supportable, and politically represented.

**Latest completed productive-resilience rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Mozambique PASP record](records/comparative-world-bank-mozambique-pasp-social-protection-climate-autonomy-2026.json) adds a route from climate and income shock through transfers, skills, productive grants, public ecological work, mobile-money access, household assets, food security, housing resilience, and post-programme autonomy. PASP reports more than 300,000 participants, 65% women beneficiaries, and more than 200,000 electronic-money accounts, but the feature story does not establish representative treatment effects, active account use, ecological impact, or durable autonomy at population scale. Its next test is a province-level episode following transfer adequacy, payment use, work conditions, asset ownership, care time, climate loss, food security, and outcomes after exit.

**Latest productive-resilience memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Mozambique PASP memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-mozambique-pasp-social-protection-climate-autonomy-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that social protection becomes politically and materially durable when it creates household room, ecological protection, payment access, and visible agency rather than only temporary relief.

**Latest completed youth-culture rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Up and Up Gen Z control/community record](records/comparative-upandup-genz-control-community-anti-algorithm-2026.json) adds a route from algorithmic and institutional decisions experienced as imposed through powerlessness, local participation, visible agency, belonging and possible civic translation. Its next test is a quantitative or behavioral study connecting local cultural participation to time, spending, access, trust and civic action; the report is a small qualitative conversation sample.

**Latest completed youth-culture memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Up and Up memo](projects/cross-domain/upandup-genz-control-community-anti-algorithm-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that “anti-algorithm” behavior is less a clean exit from digital life than a search for settings where people can exercise choice, recognition and visible consequence.

**Latest completed place-and-work rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Pew Philadelphia Gen Z place-retention record](records/us-pew-charitable-philadelphia-genz-place-retention-work-quality-2026.json) adds a route from place attachment and affordability through job quality, career pathways, transit/safety, networks, time and the decision to stay, suburbanize or exit. Its next test is a longitudinal metro study linking actual job quality, wages, commute, safety, housing, family stage and moves; the report is qualitative with descriptive Census context.

**Latest completed place-and-work memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Pew Philadelphia Gen Z memo](projects/cross-domain/pew-philadelphia-genz-place-retention-work-quality-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that a city retains young adults only when cultural attachment can be converted into usable career pathways and a livable life outside work.

**Latest completed meaning-of-work rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Harvard Making Caring Common Gen Z work-purpose record](records/us-harvard-mcc-genz-meaningful-work-purpose-financial-security-2026.json) adds a route from desire to help through access to meaningful work, care-sector pay and emotional burden, salary/purpose trade-offs, wellbeing and labor-market legitimacy. Its next test is longitudinal worker evidence linking meaning, pay, hours, emotional load, housing, debt, health, retention and occupational transition; the source summary is self-reported and associational.

**Latest completed meaning-of-work memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Harvard Gen Z work-purpose memo](projects/cross-domain/harvard-genz-meaningful-work-purpose-financial-security-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that a culture of purpose cannot stabilize care labor when workers lack the material room to choose meaning over salary.

**Latest completed music-culture rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [YouGov music consumption and discovery record](records/us-yougov-music-consumption-discovery-genz-streaming-2026.json) adds a route from music importance and expanded listening through algorithmic, social and live discovery to identity, belonging, attention allocation and platform/artist power. **Latest completed music-culture memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [YouGov music memo](projects/cross-domain/yougov-music-consumption-discovery-genz-streaming-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that algorithmic access does not eliminate social and live cultural authority, even when platforms become the largest discovery interface.

**Latest completed financial-guidance rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup financial-guidance record](records/us-gallup-financial-guidance-ai-trust-affordability-2026.json) adds a route from financial uncertainty through low-cost digital research and AI explanation to human-network or professional validation, accountability and remedy. **Latest completed financial-guidance memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup financial-guidance memo](projects/cross-domain/gallup-financial-guidance-ai-trust-affordability-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that AI can widen entry into financial learning without becoming a trusted decision-maker, leaving an open gap between cheap access and accountable help.

**Latest completed rural-political-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [AP–KFF rural-voter record](records/us-ap-kff-rural-voters-cost-healthcare-political-legitimacy-2026.json) adds a route from local cost and healthcare strain through policy visibility, perceived delivery, partisan trust, respect and possible electoral agency. **Latest completed rural-political-legitimacy memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [AP–KFF rural-voter memo](projects/cross-domain/ap-kff-rural-voters-cost-healthcare-political-legitimacy-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that partisan identity and material legitimacy can diverge when residents cannot see, use or attribute promised policy benefits.

**Latest completed information-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup media-trust record](records/us-gallup-media-trust-partisan-rebound-2026.json) adds a route from historically weak confidence through partisan divergence and event-sensitive movement to source choice, correction and shared-fact legitimacy. **Latest completed information-legitimacy memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup media-trust memo](projects/cross-domain/gallup-media-trust-partisan-rebound-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that a small trust rebound is not institutional recovery when most people still lack confidence and partisan evaluation remains structurally separated.

**Latest completed household-resilience rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [NerdWallet financial-resilience record](records/us-nerdwallet-financial-resilience-control-buffer-credit-2026.json) adds a route from perceived financial control through bill payment, liquid buffers, credit dependence, life stage and future household room. **Latest completed household-resilience memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [NerdWallet financial-resilience memo](projects/cross-domain/nerdwallet-financial-resilience-control-buffer-credit-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that households can preserve near-term continuity while remaining one shock away from credit dependence or forced compression.

**Latest completed public-capacity rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Partnership for Public Service public-trust record](records/us-pps-public-trust-government-civil-servants-capacity-2026.json) adds a route from abstract federal-government distrust through partisan attribution, civil-servant judgment, specific service encounters, capacity and accountability. Its next test is a named agency or service episode linking workforce capacity and encounter quality to user outcomes, correction, attribution and durable trust; the report is perception-based and does not provide respondent-level service-to-trust linkage.

**Latest completed public-capacity memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Partnership public-trust memo](projects/cross-domain/pps-public-trust-government-civil-servants-capacity-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that public legitimacy is differentiated: people can distrust government in the abstract while valuing particular civil servants or services.

**Latest completed health-insurance mobility rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup job-lock record](records/us-gallup-job-lock-health-insurance-mobility-2026.json) adds a route from employer coverage dependence through healthcare cost, medical debt, chronic-condition exposure, gender, job lock and reduced labor-market optionality. **Latest completed life-stage care-information rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup-Pivotal menopause record](records/us-gallup-pivotal-menopause-information-care-work-2026.json) adds a route from life-stage information and personal-network authority through provider recognition, treatment continuity, work, self-care and community participation. **Latest completed education-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [MCAAD–Gallup American Dream and schools record](records/us-gallup-mcaad-american-dream-schools-opportunity-2026.json) adds a route from education as a public promise through perceived K-12 performance, opportunity belief, intergenerational hope and institutional legitimacy. **Latest completed education-legitimacy memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [MCAAD–Gallup education memo](projects/cross-domain/gallup-mcaad-american-dream-schools-opportunity-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that schools are judged not only as services but as evidence about whether the American Dream remains institutionally credible.

**Latest completed social-institution rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [IFS churchgoing-marriage record](records/comparative-ifs-churchgoing-marriage-shared-values-2026.json) adds a route from recurring communal practice and shared faith/marriage values through relationship meaning, household wellbeing, belonging and possible civic capacity. **Latest completed consumer-expectations rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Conference Board September consumer-confidence record](records/us-conference-board-consumer-confidence-september-2026-current-expectations-spending.json) adds a multi-clock household-room route from current conditions through expectations, prices/rates, intended spending and political attribution. **Latest completed consumer-expectations memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Conference Board memo](projects/cross-domain/conference-board-consumer-confidence-september-2026-multi-clock-household-room-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that weak confidence is not uniform demand destruction: households can protect some recurring or restorative activities while deferring high-cost, durable or internationally exposed purchases.

**Latest completed nonprofit-infrastructure rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [IU Lilly Family School of Philanthropy nonprofit-recognition record](records/us-iu-lilly-nonprofits-philanthropy-recognition-confidence-2026.json) adds a route from ordinary community activity through nonprofit infrastructure, weak user recognition, conditional confidence, provider-role judgment and funding legitimacy. **Latest completed nonprofit-infrastructure memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [IU Lilly nonprofit-recognition memo](projects/cross-domain/iu-lilly-nonprofit-recognition-confidence-public-service-bargain-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that institutions can be socially essential without being culturally legible, making “community support” weaker than visible support for the organizations that sustain it.

**Latest completed U.S. distributional rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [CBO household-income distribution record](records/us-cbo-household-income-distribution-2023-transfers-taxes-inequality.json) adds a route from market income through means-tested transfers, federal taxes, unequal household room and fiscal legitimacy. Its next test is a named U.S. tax, transfer, Medicaid, SNAP, SSI or labor-market episode that follows distributional change into take-up, adequacy, consumption, debt, health access, administrative friction and political response; the CBO report is descriptive distributional analysis, not causal proof.

**Latest completed U.S. distributional memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [CBO household-income memo](projects/cross-domain/cbo-household-income-distribution-2023-transfers-taxes-inequality-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that income growth, public redistribution and household security are distinct layers: long-run gains can coexist with widening relative distance and contested meanings of fairness.

**Latest completed macro-household recovery rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [New Zealand Treasury Pre-election Fiscal Update record](records/comparative-new-zealand-treasury-pre-election-fiscal-update-2026-nominal-recovery-household-pressure.json) adds a route from energy and geopolitical shocks through household demand, real-wage recovery, nominal fiscal repair, fixed public budgets, service pressure and contested recovery narratives. **Latest completed consumer-confidence rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ANZ–Roy Morgan Australian consumer-confidence record](records/comparative-anz-roy-morgan-australian-consumer-confidence-september-29-2026-financial-outlook.json) adds a route from household financial pessimism through inflation expectations, regional variation, purchase timing and anticipated constraint. **Latest completed consumer-confidence memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ANZ–Roy Morgan memo](projects/cross-domain/anz-roy-morgan-australian-consumer-confidence-september-29-2026-financial-outlook.md) sharpens the subtheme that confidence, household room and purchase timing are distinct layers of consumer life: a weak headline can coexist with selective or necessary purchasing.

**Latest completed corridor-resilience rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Trans-Caspian Transport Corridor record](records/comparative-world-bank-trans-caspian-corridor-logistics-economic-corridor-2026.json) adds a route from geopolitical routing pressure through rail/port bottlenecks, digital interoperability, operator governance, local economic links, jobs and regional legitimacy. **Latest completed fiscal-capacity rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Philippines Public Finance Review record](records/comparative-world-bank-philippines-public-finance-review-fiscal-capacity-targeting-2026.json) adds a route from procurement and tax-administration reform through fiscal room, targeting, administrative visibility, burden distribution, human-capital protection and public trust. **Latest completed fiscal-capacity memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Philippines Public Finance Review memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-philippines-public-finance-review-fiscal-capacity-targeting-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that public finance is a social bargain: administrative visibility can improve protection while also creating exclusion, privacy, burden-shifting and local-discretion risks.

**Latest completed preparedness-finance rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD global-health PPR record](records/comparative-oecd-global-health-ppr-financing-aid-dependence-resilience-2026.json) adds a route from globally shared health risk through delayed prevention benefits, domestic budget competition, unequal per-capita capacity, aid dependence, measurement, and collective resilience. Its next test is a named country or regional health-security episode that follows financing into surveillance, workforce protection, supply continuity, public communication, access, remedy and trust; the OECD brief is a financing and measurement synthesis, not causal evidence.

**Latest completed preparedness-finance memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD global-health PPR memo](projects/cross-domain/oecd-global-health-ppr-financing-aid-dependence-resilience-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that preparedness is an invisible social contract: resilience accounting can make delayed, cross-border protection politically legible, but visibility alone does not guarantee capability, equity or durable commitment.

**Latest completed regional-interoperability rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Africa integration record](records/comparative-world-bank-africa-integration-interoperability-regional-public-goods-2026.json) adds a route from AfCFTA and regional agreements through behind-the-border trade costs, interoperable customs/standards/payments/data, corridors, power pools, regional public goods and multi-level legitimacy. Its next test is a named corridor, power-pool, payment, insurance, customs or standards episode that follows formal agreement into actual cost, clearance, access, remedy and political trust; the report is analytical and prescriptive, not causal evidence.

**Latest completed geopolitical-infrastructure memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank Africa integration memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-africa-integration-interoperability-regional-public-goods-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that regional belonging becomes materially credible when systems interoperate—when goods, money, data, standards and institutions can cross borders predictably.

**Latest completed water-governance rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD/AWC Philippines water-regulation record](records/comparative-oecd-philippines-water-sanitation-regulation-fragmentation-data-2026.json) adds a route from climate and demand pressure through fragmented providers, tariff affordability, service quality, aggregation, integrated data and household trust. Its next test is a named water or sanitation episode that follows tariff changes, investment, outages, quality, complaints, household cost, local political response and remedy across differently regulated communities; the report is a national dialogue and policy assessment, not causal evidence.

**Latest completed water-information memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD/AWC Philippines water memo](projects/cross-domain/oecd-philippines-water-sanitation-regulation-fragmentation-data-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that a centralized water information system is public accountability infrastructure: it determines whether tariffs, coverage, quality, outages, investment and compliance become visible enough to support a legitimate service bargain.

**Latest completed social-economy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD ASEAN inclusive-business record](records/comparative-oecd-asean-inclusive-business-social-enterprise-market-participation-2026.json) adds a route from informal work and underserved demand through social-enterprise experimentation, inclusive value-chain roles, finance, accreditation, impact measurement, affordable access and mission continuity. Its next test is a named ASEAN enterprise or programme that follows prices, worker conditions, customer access, supplier bargaining power, public support, investor control and mission drift after scale; the OECD paper is a regional policy and case synthesis, not causal evidence.

**Latest completed social-economy memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD ASEAN inclusive-business memo](projects/cross-domain/oecd-asean-inclusive-business-social-enterprise-market-participation-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that “inclusive” is becoming a market institution whose legitimacy depends on who controls scale, finance, data and impact—not on mission language alone.

**Latest completed gendered-market-access rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank India trade-facilitation record](records/comparative-world-bank-india-women-trade-facilitation-gendered-infrastructure-2026.json) adds a route from formally open trade procedures through care time, safety, mobility, local-language information, small-firm consultation, finance, grievance confidence and repeat market access. Its next test is a named border or trade-facilitation episode that follows women-led firms through clearance, waiting, safety, complaint/remedy, transaction costs and firm survival; the study is descriptive assessment evidence, not causal proof.

**Latest completed gendered-infrastructure memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [World Bank India trade-facilitation memo](projects/cross-domain/world-bank-india-women-trade-facilitation-gendered-infrastructure-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that a border is a workplace and time-use system: digitization and formal transparency remain practically exclusive when facilities, language, care, finance and trusted remedy are not usable.

**Latest completed rural-value-chain rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Timor-Leste vanilla record](records/comparative-ilo-timor-leste-vanilla-value-chain-skills-decent-work-2026.json) adds a route from a heritage/agroforestry crop through production skills, infrastructure, farmer cooperatives, bargaining power, certification, domestic processing, consumer sustainability meaning and occupational safety. Its next test is whether a named vanilla or origin-product episode changes farmer prices, buyer concentration, cooperative survival, processing, women’s and youth participation, work hazards and the distribution of any sustainability premium; the roadmap is diagnostic and prescriptive, not causal evidence.

**Latest completed rural-value-chain memo rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Timor-Leste vanilla memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-timor-leste-vanilla-value-chain-skills-decent-work-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that a culturally legible product becomes a durable livelihood only when consumer-facing sustainability claims are matched by producer voice, value addition, skills, finance, market alternatives and decent work.

**Latest completed project-afterlife rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO EIIP formalization record](records/comparative-ilo-eiip-formalization-project-afterlife-public-investment-2026.json) adds a route from public infrastructure investment through temporary income, worker registration, social protection, skills, cooperative organization, procurement and post-project reversion risk. Its next test is a named public-investment episode that follows participants after closure into registration continuity, next employment, claims, wages, enterprise revenue, procurement, cooperative survival and household security; the brief is guidance and evidence synthesis, not causal proof.

**Latest completed infrastructure-formalization rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO EIIP project-afterlife memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-eiip-formalization-project-afterlife-public-investment-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the idea that infrastructure creates a formalization gateway only when the project outlives itself through durable rights, market access, local capacity and a next contract.

**Latest completed self-employed-protection rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO comparative social-insurance record](records/comparative-ilo-self-employed-social-insurance-design-portability-2026.json) adds a route from own-account work and irregular earnings through scheme architecture, affordability, benefit fit, administrative access, worker organization, trust and portability. Its next test is a named self-employed or platform-work episode that follows design into take-up, contribution continuity, claims, income security, gendered adequacy, worker voice and movement between employment statuses; the paper is a selected-case synthesis, not causal proof.

**Latest completed contribution-usability rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO self-employed social-insurance memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-self-employed-social-insurance-design-portability-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the finding that formalization can remain nominal when contributions are affordable but benefits are thin, voluntary participation is weak, digital or identity access is missing, or workers cannot carry rights across changing statuses.

**Latest completed climate-work rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Colombia heat-stress record](records/comparative-ilo-colombia-heat-stress-occupational-mortality-climate-risk-2026.json) adds a route from temperature exposure through occupation, mortality, gendered vulnerability, work-rest conditions, informality, occupational-risk insurance and worker-centred adaptation. Its next test is a dated heat episode or workplace intervention that follows exposure into breaks, hours, productivity, injury, mortality, claims, income, enforcement and household consequences; the study is observational and geographically bounded.

**Latest completed climate-protection rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Colombia heat-stress memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-colombia-heat-stress-occupational-mortality-climate-risk-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the subtheme that near-universal general health coverage can coexist with incomplete protection against harm caused by work, making rest time, water, shade, ventilation, risk assessment and worker voice part of climate infrastructure.

**Latest completed AI-work-organization rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Chinese enterprise AI-adoption record](records/comparative-ilo-chinese-enterprise-ai-adoption-hybrid-work-measurement-2026.json) adds a route from organizational adoption model through workflow redesign, measured productivity, task displacement or redeployment, worker capability, human oversight, and transition legitimacy. Its next test is a dated enterprise or sector episode that follows AI deployment into tasks, hours, staffing, wages, career entry, worker voice, quality, and who captures the gain; the brief is purposive qualitative and descriptive survey evidence, not causal proof.

**Latest completed AI-measurement rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Chinese enterprise AI memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-chinese-enterprise-ai-adoption-hybrid-work-measurement-2026-09-29.md) adds the subtheme that output gains become politically incomplete when firms measure speed and throughput but not job quality, workload, autonomy, redeployment, or distribution.

**Latest completed construction-formalization rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Nepal construction informality record](records/comparative-ilo-nepal-construction-informality-formalization-social-protection-2026.json) adds a route from enterprise registration through temporary and subcontracted work, employer-of-record uncertainty, skills, gender, occupational safety, procurement leverage, and portable social protection. Its next test is whether worker-initiated enrollment, flexible contributions, portable benefits, procurement compliance, and injury protection produce continuity across changing employers and sites; the brief is diagnostic policy evidence, not causal proof.

**Latest completed formalization-portability rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Nepal construction formalization memo](projects/cross-domain/ilo-nepal-construction-informality-formalization-social-protection-2026-09-29.md) sharpens the program's subtheme of formalization without employment security: a registered enterprise can still contain overwhelmingly informal workers when project work and subcontracting outrun the social-security account.

**Latest completed institutional-transition rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO skills and lifelong-learning evaluation record](records/comparative-ilo-skills-lifelong-learning-strategy-evaluation-2026.json) adds an institutionalization-gap route from work-transition pressure through policy and governance reform, worker voice, financing, implementation capacity, and durable learning access. Its next test is whether national systems convert frameworks and pilots into accessible, funded, worker-legitimate lifelong-learning infrastructure rather than leaving reforms at the policy or externally funded project stage; the evaluation is strategy-level and not causal proof of employment, wage, productivity, or inclusion effects.

**Latest completed education-infrastructure rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [OECD Education at a Glance 2026 teacher-shortage record](records/comparative-oecd-education-at-a-glance-2026-teacher-shortage-status-place.json) adds a route from skilled-labor scarcity and ageing through uneven teacher supply, place- and subject-specific access, occupational status, family burden, and community confidence. Its next test is whether a named staffing or reallocation episode improves teacher stability and student access in hard-to-staff places without treating demographic decline as automatic relief; the report is comparative indicator and survey evidence, not causal proof of learning or retention effects.

**Latest completed social-protection-usability rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Jordan social-security registration record](records/comparative-ilo-jordan-social-security-registration-behavioral-outreach-2026.json) adds randomized evidence that contribution affordability plus human outreach increased worker registration, while low-cost SMS scaled awareness but did not increase worker registration. Its next test is whether social insurance reforms combine affordable contributions, human navigation, simpler administration, and realized claim access across the worker lifecycle; the Jordan small-firm experiment does not establish durable post-subsidy coverage or benefit adequacy.

**Latest completed formalization-trust rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ILO Jordan firm-level RCT record](records/comparative-ilo-jordan-social-security-rct-firm-level-trust-process-friction-2026.json) adds preliminary implementation evidence that formalization varies by firm size, wage eligibility, nationality and administrative capacity, while data staleness, digital-process friction, inspection-like contact and loopholes can weaken trust. Its next test is whether hybrid outreach and child-benefit incentives produce durable contributions and claims without adding administrative burden or selective exclusion; the report does not establish long-run formalization or benefit adequacy.

**Latest completed AI-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [SCSP–Gallup AI sentiment record](records/us-scsp-gallup-ai-sentiment-trust-preparation-2026.json) adds a public legitimacy and preparation gap: AI use and informal learning rise while trust remains low, economic expectations worsen, and respondents favor training, safety rules and independent testing. Its next test is whether named institutions convert these preferences into accessible training, accountable testing and worker transition capacity; the survey does not establish realized labor-market or policy effects.

**Latest completed worker-transition rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup AI workplace-manager record](records/us-gallup-ai-workplace-manager-buffer-displacement-2026.json) adds a worker-level conversion mechanism: frequent AI users report more displacement fear, while respect and perceived organizational care narrow that association and fear co-moves with engagement, burnout, satisfaction and job search. Its next test is a named workplace implementation episode linking manager practice to actual task redesign, training, retention, wages or job transitions; the panel evidence remains observational.

**Latest completed collective-voice rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup union-influence record](records/us-gallup-union-influence-approval-2026-collective-voice.json) adds a legitimacy-capacity split: 47% want unions to have more influence and 71% approve of them, yet only 27% expect unions to strengthen. Its next test is whether public support converts into organizing, bargaining coverage, worker voice and material outcomes; the poll does not establish that conversion.

**Latest completed life-course household-security rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Edward Jones–Gallup financial-future record](records/comparative-gallup-edward-jones-financial-future-confidence-us-canada-2026.json) adds a current-control/future-security split across generations: older adults have more current-finance confidence, while future-finance confidence remains low across age groups; younger adults report stress and extra work, and Gen X reports retirement uncertainty. Its next test is longitudinal household evidence linking time, income, assets, housing, care and retirement outcomes; the survey is cross-sectional.

**Latest completed place-and-firm rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup local-business-climate record](records/us-gallup-business-owner-local-climate-retention-2026.json) adds a firm-retention mechanism: only 48% of owners view their local area positively, and dissatisfaction is associated with relocation intention, especially among owners expecting growth. Its next test is an observed local ecosystem episode linking customer access, networks, regulation, public services, relocation, firm survival and jobs; the survey remains correlational.

**Latest completed speculative-consumption rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup crypto-ownership record](records/us-gallup-crypto-ownership-risk-reversal-2026.json) adds a selective-risk consumer mechanism: ownership fell from 17% to 11% among investors while 63% still call crypto very risky, with participation concentrated among younger men and higher-income investors. Its next test is whether ownership retreat becomes durable exit, selective exposure or realized household gain/loss; the survey does not establish those outcomes.

**Latest completed measurement-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup unemployment-measurement record](records/us-gallup-unemployment-measurement-narrative-slack-2026.json) adds a measurement-to-legitimacy route: headline unemployment, U-6, prime-age nonparticipation, job fit and meaningful work can produce different accounts of whether the economy is functioning. Its next test is a reproducible layered labor-market dashboard and outcome-linked validation; the source essay's proposed “real unemployment” measure is not treated as official or causal.

**Latest completed sector-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Gallup industry-ratings record](records/us-gallup-industry-ratings-cost-trust-2026.json) adds a cost-and-risk attribution layer: computer, utility, farming and oil-and-gas ratings declined amid household-cost pressure, technology disruption and visible sector risk. Its next test is whether public rating changes connect to prices, service use, regulation, firm adaptation, consumer recourse or infrastructure legitimacy; the survey remains perception-based.

The current concise governing statement is the [broader goal restatement](../BROAD-GOAL-RESTATEMENT_V1.md).

**Latest completed geopolitical-cultural rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [JPPI Israeli Society Index record](records/comparative-jppi-israel-society-index-september-2026-election-security-social-cohesion.json) adds a route from security and territorial judgment through election salience, judicial authority, unity, identity attribution, social repair and education legitimacy. Its next valid test is a named conflict, election, education or institutional episode that follows stated stakes into turnout, policy choice, civic trust, service delivery and durable cohesion rather than treating survey salience or preference as realized political outcome.

**Latest completed household-shortage rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [ONS Great Britain shortages record](records/comparative-ons-great-britain-shortages-august-2026-substitution-availability.json) adds a route from goods availability through extra spending, search time, substitution, reduced purchases and possible unmet food, medicine and fuel needs. Its next valid test is a named supply or household episode that follows shortage exposure into price, quality, diet, health, debt, time and durable room rather than treating continued shopping as unchanged welfare.

**Latest completed supply-chain power rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [UK Groceries Code Adjudicator supplier-survey record](records/comparative-uk-gca-supplier-retailer-code-2026-power-payment-forecasting.json) adds a route from formal retailer compliance through invoice resolution, forecasting cost, de-listing, payment delay, confidential voice and practical supplier exit. Its next valid test is a named supply-chain episode that follows an issue into correction, payment, continued listing, supplier investment, consumer availability and durable bargaining room rather than treating compliance scores or reported issues as remedy.

**Latest completed care-and-labour rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Statistics South Africa Gender Series XIII record](records/comparative-stats-sa-gender-care-work-2026-time-infrastructure-labor.json) adds a gendered care-conversion route from demographic dependency and household infrastructure through education interruption, paid-care concentration, employment and unequal time. Its next valid test is a named care, water, school or employment episode that follows formal provision into actual hours, continuity, household bargaining, earnings and durable wellbeing rather than treating infrastructure access or occupational concentration as care relief.

**Latest completed public-safety rotation — 2026-09-29:** the [Statistics South Africa GPSJS 2025/26 record](records/comparative-stats-sa-gpsjs-2025-26-crime-reporting-safety-self-protection.json) adds a security-conversion route from victimisation through police reporting, perceived safety and privately supplied protection. Its next valid test is a named safety or justice episode that follows exposure into reporting effort, police response, case remedy, household cost, mobility and durable trust rather than treating crime incidence or self-protection as public-security success.

**Latest publication rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [New America National Parent Survey record](records/us-new-america-national-parent-survey-2026-time-care-money-sandwich.json) adds a family-time conversion route: parents broadly want more quality time with young children, but wages, child-care cost and supply, leave, transportation, work schedules, and eldercare determine whether that ideal is reachable. Its next test is whether a named leave, wage, child-care, transportation, schedule, or eldercare intervention changes usable family time, employment continuity, care reliability, household room, and subgroup-specific recovery. The preceding [YouGov values-of-children-and-work record](records/comparative-yougov-values-children-work-2026-success-time-meaning.json) remains an active adjacent route.

**Latest employment-continuity rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Carers UK work and unpaid-care record](records/comparative-carers-uk-work-unpaid-care-2026-employment-tipping-point.json) adds a route from unpaid care inside working life through reduced hours, stress, and employment exit. Its next test is whether care intensity, employer flexibility, formal-care access, and paid leave predict actual employment continuity rather than only reported burden.

**Latest election-information rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Catawba College–YouGov Ballots & Bots record](records/comparative-catawba-yougov-election-ai-authenticity-trust-2026.json) adds a route from AI-generated political-content uncertainty through conditional election legitimacy, trusted local verification, and demand for disclosure and platform accountability. Its next test is whether provenance labels, rapid correction, and local election-office communication preserve acceptance of results when voters encounter suspected synthetic content.

**Latest local-consumer rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [SOCi Local Discovery Index record](records/comparative-soci-local-discovery-ai-verification-loop-2026.json) adds a route from AI-mediated local discovery through cross-channel verification, review-based remedy, category-specific visibility, and unequal discovery access. Its next test is whether local consumers complete the verification loop, whether stale information causes measurable exclusion or diversion, and whether responsive reputation systems restore conversion.

**Latest education-legitimacy rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Public First cross-party education record](records/comparative-public-first-education-cross-party-technical-pathways-delivery-2026.json) adds a route from technical-pathway and adult-readiness preferences through cross-party policy agreement, implementation skepticism, local-capacity concerns, and transparent delivery. Its next test is whether technical routes produce actual enrollment, completion, work transitions, earnings, and durable trust rather than only broad stated support.

**Latest employer-transition rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [WorldSkills UK/Public First Ready to Work record](records/comparative-worldskillsuk-public-first-ready-to-work-employer-skills-2026.json) adds the employer-side route from harder youth entry into work through employability-skill signaling, firm capability, structured demonstrations, and unequal access to credible early-career pathways. Its next test is whether competitions, work experience, and technical routes change interviews, offers, retention, earnings, and subgroup access.

**Latest relational-social-infrastructure rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [IFS Devout Difference record](records/comparative-ifs-devout-difference-religion-marital-social-infrastructure-2026.json) adds a route from religious participation and shared marital norms through reported relationship quality and intimacy, while preserving selection, incomplete public methodology, institutional sponsorship, and non-causal boundaries. Its next test is whether transparent longitudinal or matched designs separate selection, social support, care burden, economic stress, relationship stability, and exit over time. The preceding [KFF/AP Rural Voters record](records/us-kff-ap-rural-voters-cost-healthcare-attribution-2026.json) remains the adjacent political-economy route.

**Latest cultural-institution rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Pew Latter-day Saints community/trust/diversity record](records/us-pew-latter-day-saints-tradition-change-2026-community-trust-diversity.json) adds a religion-as-social-infrastructure route: dense communal ties, family practice, volunteering, and generalized trust persist alongside declining retention, demographic diversification, changing family reproduction, and partisan pluralism. Its next test is whether communal infrastructure converts into observable participation, support, voice, and durable institutional adaptation.

**Latest comparative consumer rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Bank of Korea September consumer-survey record](records/comparative-bok-korea-consumer-survey-september-2026-sentiment-housing-expectations.json) adds a consumer-expectations route: headline confidence rebounds while current living standards remain flat and inflation, housing-price, and interest-rate expectations continue to shape perceived room. Its next test is whether the expectation movement becomes realized spending, saving, borrowing, housing mobility, cultural participation, and political attribution.

**Latest consumer-infrastructure rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Stitch South Africa consumer-payments record](records/comparative-stitch-south-africa-consumer-payments-2026-digital-wallets-bnpl-agentic-commerce.json) adds a payment-fragmentation route: wallet adoption and speed, BNPL and payday liquidity, international platform choice, checkout failure, and AI-mediated discovery reorganize usable consumer options. Its next test is whether payment flexibility becomes durable affordability, successful completion, repayment safety, remedy, repeat access, and trustworthy agentic commerce.

**Latest political-ecology rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [More in Common Strong Roots record](records/comparative-more-in-common-strong-roots-2026-nature-cross-party-coalitions.json) adds a cross-party nature-and-place route: shared environmental pride can survive climate-policy disagreement, while development framing and perceived political neglect shape stewardship legitimacy. Its next test is whether nature attachment becomes concrete local acceptance, conservation delivery, equitable green-space access, household cost incidence, participation, and durable trust.

**Latest information-environment rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [IPIE 2026 expert-survey record](records/comparative-ipie-information-environment-2026-platform-accountability-ai-search-research-pressure.json) adds a platform-accountability and research-capacity route: AI summaries can increase search speed while reducing source traffic and viewpoint diversity, amid low news trust and rising pressure on information-environment researchers. Its next test is whether these intermediary changes affect observed exposure, source choice, civic understanding, correction, participation, and durable legitimacy.

**Latest place-and-participation rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Pew urban/suburban/rural public-life record](records/us-pew-urban-suburban-rural-public-life-engagement-2026-place-specific-routes.json) adds a geography-as-route layer: overall engagement distributions are similar across community types, while social/religious participation, interaction medium, protest, signage, and official contact differ. Its next test is whether place-specific routes convert into belonging, political influence, institutional feedback, equitable access, and durable trust.

**Latest citizen-futures rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [JRC Our Futures citizen-resilience record](records/comparative-jrc-our-futures-europe-citizen-resilience-narratives-2026.json) adds a narrative foresight route: citizens connect democracy, climate, migration, affordability, technology, geopolitical shocks, institutional capacity, and personal agency in imagined 2040 futures. Its next test is whether these narratives become policy demand, participation, adaptation, solidarity, visible delivery, and durable legitimacy.

**Latest social-pressure rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [St Patrick's Irish mental-health and stigma record](records/comparative-stpatricks-ireland-mental-health-stigma-cost-loneliness-2026.json) adds a pressure-to-care route: cost, housing, loneliness, climate concern, digital exposure, stigma, silence, and support-seeking meet in everyday mental-health experience. Its next test is whether pressure becomes disclosure, reachable care, reduced discrimination, and durable social participation.

**Latest publication rotation — 2026-09-28:** the [Helen Clark Foundation New Zealand cohesion record](records/comparative-helen-clark-nz-social-cohesion-2026-belonging-financial-stress.json) adds a material-belonging route: national belonging remains high while local connection, government trust, safety, and youth hope are unevenly distributed by financial stress, housing tenure, age, gender, and friendship networks. Its next test is whether a named housing, food, community, or youth intervention converts material room into durable belonging, participation, and trust. The preceding AthenaLab Chile, BSA NHS, PwC consumer-sentiment, and BSA welfare records remain adjacent legitimacy rotations.

The current focused bridge checkpoint is the [material-to-action status
ledger](US-BROAD-MATERIAL-TO-ACTION-STATUS_V1.md).

The latest reproducibility checkpoint is the [CES medical-affordability/action
audit](projects/us-health-cost-household-choice/cces-medical-affordability-action-reproduction-audit-2026-09-16.md):
the existing local 2018/2020 module files reproduce the committed
hardship/attribution/action screen, while the episode-level remedy, recovery,
trust, and exit arrows remain open.

This ledger keeps the program moving across sessions. It is not a progress
report that can be closed by completing one analysis. A research pass changes
one or more evidence rows; the program remains active until the user changes
or ends it.

## Governing outcome

Maintain a US-centered, cross-source living trend atlas that discovers,
compares, tests, and revisits recurring cultural, societal, political,
consumer, institutional, financial, firm, infrastructure, and geopolitical
themes. Preserve the path from measured condition to behavior, institutional
response, power or risk redistribution, meaning, and wider consequence while
marking every unsupported arrow as open.

The program is successful only when it keeps improving the map across people,
households, consumers, workers, firms, places, institutions, infrastructure,
markets, and states. One dataset, one finding, one bridge, or one session is
never the completion condition.
