individual recovery, actual aid, or political causality.

The consumer lane then added an income-stratified April-to-June HTOPS pass.
Across all three household-income bands, April expense difficulty was followed
by more June searching for jobs, food, benefits, credit, housing, and medical
help. The route differed by resource position: lower-income respondents showed
especially large job- and benefit-search gaps, while higher-income respondents
had much higher AI use overall but almost no difficulty-related AI-use gap. The
derived record preserves income as a household band, not liquid cash, and keeps
search separate from completion, remedy, recovery, trust, and exit.
The same derived record now counts search domains: expense-difficulty
respondents searched at least two domains at 40.3%, 45.7%, and 39.4% across
the low-, middle-, and high-income bands, versus 21.8%, 27.1%, and 26.5% among
their not-difficult counterparts. This supports a multi-front navigation-load
interpretation while preserving the noncausal, non-recovery boundary.
The same record now overlaps search load with June AI use: among difficult-
expense respondents, AI users searched at least two domains more often than
non-AI users in all three income bands. This supports an added-navigation-layer
interpretation, not AI substitution or a causal digital-access claim.

The worker-remedy lane also promoted the Alam Shah case as a cultural-access
counterexample. Injury, language and interpreter needs, walkability, family
obligations, and fear of repeat dismissal sit alongside formal reinstatement,
continuity, translated communication, and lost-pay orders. The later-outcome
search found no public proof of actual return, payment, durable safety, or later
security, so the case strengthens the usable-remedy concept without claiming
lived recovery.

The evidence matrix now also carries three previously unlinked cross-domain
bridges: firm AI adoption to unequal worker control; AI capability to
replaceability and geopolitical leverage; and insurance failure to restitution
versus lived coverage/care recovery. These additions preserve separate firm,
worker, consumer, country, and settlement units while making the missing
middle stages explicit.

The remedy synthesis now promotes the DOJ IER FY2026 worker cases and the
public follow-up recheck: reported back/front pay, a reinstatement offer, and
an employer verification-procedure change are visible, while accepted return,
payment adequacy, continued tenure, household security, trust, and exit are
not. The follow-up gap is preserved as non-observation, not remedy failure.

The latest evidence-matrix promotion also adds two deep-culture boundaries:
material insecurity can lower contact and action without uniformly lowering
institutional approval, while subjective financial worry can precede lower
trust and different reported vote patterns; and disability care can fail at
the physical, communication, digital, or provider interface even when formal
coverage and capacity exist. These are bounded timing/interface findings, not
causal voting or national accessibility estimates.

The infrastructure lane is now traceable at project level: Virginia records
public speakers, risk-allocation rules, a land-consent decision that changed an
approved route, and a separate project reaching reported energization. These
stages strengthen the public-voice and realization arrows without claiming
resident compensation, household bill effects, reliability improvement, or
durable acceptance.

The migration/meaning lane now also promotes a mobility-and-belonging finding:
people can report better living standards than their parents and hope for their
children while still seeing the American dream as hard or unavailable; identity
can be central while experienced as help, barrier, or no difference. This adds
relational progress, future hope, identity valence, and opportunity meaning to
the map without inferring assimilation, wealth mobility, or later action.

The remedy lane then quantified a receipt-stage ladder from CFPB follow-up:
three completed distributions reported 69%, 86%, and 88% of distributed funds
cashed, while later reports documented reissue or redistribution of unresolved
funds. The [remedy synthesis](US-REMEDY-IMPLEMENTATION-RECOVERY-SYNTHESIS_V1.md)
now records this as persistence and reachability evidence, not a household
recovery rate; individual receipt, account correction, adequacy, residual loss,
trust, switching, and recovery remain open.

## 2026-09-19 continuation checkpoint

The central [theme end-to-end coverage audit](US-BROAD-THEME-END-TO-END-COVERAGE-AUDIT_V1.md)
now makes the recovery ladder explicit across all 14 themes: formal response
is coded separately from received, usable, adequate, durable, and trusted lived
status. The retained same-case stage audit provides the measured bottleneck:
29 of 33 local surfaces show a dated event and 29 show an institutional or firm
response, but only 8 show an observed alternative/non-use stage, 3 show an
observed individual remedy or defined outcome follow-up, 1 shows an observed
meaning/trust/action stage, and none fully observes recovery/persistence/exit.

At this 2026-09-19 checkpoint, the full [remedy-stage portfolio audit](data/us-broad-remedy-stage-coverage-audit-2026-09-18.json)
sat inside the then-current 89-ledger, 284-event portfolio: 75 events had an
explicit remedy stage, 31 were marked only as institution-reported received,
and none had verified recipient receipt, verified durability, or a defined
outcome follow-up. This was a documentation-coverage result, not a remedy
failure rate. The current portfolio is tracked separately below and by the
machine-validated publication gate.

The companion [open-middle blocker audit](US-BROAD-OPEN-MIDDLE-BLOCKER-AUDIT_V1.md)
shows where the portfolio is thinnest: usable alternative/non-use/switching/
exit fields appear in 101 open-arrow mentions, later outcome or durability in
100, and trust/meaning/action in 91. Receipt/implementation appears in 66,
while money/time/adequacy and unit/attribution each appear in 59. The counts
overlap; they are planning diagnostics, not prevalence rates. This makes the
next depth decision concrete: seek one lawful same-unit episode with a real
alternative and later durability before adding another aggregate response
count.

The [remedy implementation and recovery synthesis](US-REMEDY-IMPLEMENTATION-RECOVERY-SYNTHESIS_V1.md)
adds two worker-voice boundaries. The DOJ IER DACA case reports restored wage,
back pay, seniority, and complaint withdrawal; the OSHA Texas asbestos case
reports protected safety voice, retaliation, reinstatement, and damages. A
public follow-up recheck found no later payment, safe-return, durable-work,
trust, or exit record for either case. These are strong middle-chain cases,
not verified lived-recovery endpoints.

The political-meaning rotation now preserves a non-monotonic action result:
the CCES job-loss panel shows lower local action after reported job loss,
near-similar congressional contact, and slightly higher favorable Congress
approval. Material hardship therefore does not imply one direction of trust,
withdrawal, or organizing. The consumer-culture rotation adds the parallel
constrained-agency result: expense difficulty is associated with much more
search for jobs, food, benefits, and credit, while broad AI use is not
monotonic; search is option-building effort, not a completed alternative.
The migration/public-help layer keeps protective non-use separate from apathy:
perceived status or surveillance risk can reduce use of work, care, benefits,
police, school, or community routes while changing safety, dignity, and
belonging.

The new [HTOPS same-respondent cross-lag finding](projects/us-consumer-culture/findings/us-consumer-culture-004.md)
uses 6,564 linked respondents to carry April expense difficulty to June
search, AI-use, and Congress-confidence outcomes. Difficulty is followed by
substantially more job, food, benefit, credit, housing, and medical searching,
while AI use and high Congress confidence are close or lower. This strengthens
the navigation-effort arrow but leaves result quality, completion, remedy,
recovery, and exit open.

The retained March 2026 HTOPS archive was tested as a possible later outcome
wave for the June 2025 panel. It has 12,541 `SCRAMID` values versus 7,485 in
June, with zero exact-key overlap. The [linkage recheck](projects/us-consumer-culture/htops-2025-to-2026-linkage-recheck-2026-09-19.md)
therefore preserves March as a separate cross-section rather than inventing a
same-person nine-month search or recovery result.

The capacity/dependence rotation rechecked the official Polish JASSM-ER route:
the public page still states a 2026–2030 delivery window, with no public
confirmation in the retained route of specific-order shipment, acceptance,
training, operational availability, replaceability, or changed external
behavior. Procurement remains commitment/planned capability, not verified
geopolitical leverage.

The next decisive action remains the authorized UAS Older Ages or PSID
structural pass if account-controlled files are supplied. Workspace recheck
still finds no authorized respondent file. Until then, continue bounded public
episode comparisons and preserve the end-to-end promotion gate; do not turn
search, non-use, administrative response, procurement, or later status into
recovery or leverage by inference.

The latest continuation adds a JASSM-ER realization boundary: a [June 2026
Polish F-16 exercise report](https://www.gov.pl/web/suomi/puolan-ilmavoimat-ramstein-flag-2026-harjoituksessa2) documents aircraft readiness and allied
interoperability but does not document JASSM-ER carriage, inventory, weapons
integration, acceptance, or delivery. Aircraft activity therefore remains a
counterexample to treating platform readiness as missile-order realization.
The disaster-response rotation also promoted the [Los Angeles wildfire FEMA
stage ledger](projects/us-repeat-energy-crises/data/fema-la-wildfire-ihp-stage-event-ledger-v1.json)
into the broad evidence map. It adds a current administrative sequence from
registration through referral, eligibility, and reported assistance amount,
while preserving the critical aggregate boundary: no survivor-level payment,
adequacy, repair/placement, stable housing, trust, or action endpoint is
observed. Administrative reach is therefore a measurable middle stage, not a
recovery rate.
The current canonical broad-ledger portfolio is 98 ledgers, 302 events, and
454 typed arrows, including 164 explicitly open arrows; these counts are
validated by the publication gate and supersede older handoff snapshots.

The [canonical BDS county-sector divergence record](records/us-bds-county-sector-divergence-2026.json)
promotes the sector-specific layer into the trend registry: retail,
transportation, health/social assistance, and accommodation/food show distinct
county flow profiles, including a same-county counterexample with opposing
sector directions. The [canonical BDS–HRSA overlay record](records/us-bds-hrsa-capacity-adequacy-overlay-2026.json)
and [canonical BDS–PLACES bridge record](records/us-bds-places-health-flow-outcome-2026.json)
keep employer flow, shortage designation, modeled need, provider access, and
resident outcomes as separate stages. These records advance the place-capacity
lane without closing service use, household time/cost, recovery, trust, or
political-action arrows.

The [BDS county-sector divergence layer](projects/us-local-business-place/bds-county-sector-divergence-layer-v1.md)
extends that county realization layer with a storage-bounded 2023 sector
screen. Retail, transportation, health, and accommodation/food have distinct
county medians and negative-net-job shares, and one same-county panel shows
opposing sector directions. This adds sector-specific employer flows while
preserving the missing links to applications, firms, services, households,
trust, political action, and geopolitical consequence.

The [BDS–HRSA capacity-versus-adequacy overlay](projects/us-local-business-place/bds-hrsa-capacity-adequacy-overlay-v1.md)
joins realized county-sector employer flows to current primary-care shortage
components. It confirms a key atlas boundary: health-sector employer growth is
not provider adequacy, and an HPSA component is not a county-wide household
care outcome. The cross-vintage overlay advances institutional-capacity
context while leaving provider access, travel, wait, use, recovery, trust, and
political response open.

The [BDS–PLACES health-flow bridge](projects/us-local-business-place/bds-places-health-flow-outcome-bridge-v1.md)
adds a same-year county outcome surface after sector realization. Negative and
nonnegative health-sector net-job counties have nearly identical medians on
selected modeled social-need and routine-care measures. This keeps employer
flows, modeled outcomes, provider access, and resident episodes as separate
objects and preserves the missing links to use, time/money cost, recovery,
trust, and action.
The latest bounded cash-policy pass adds the [Child Tax Credit sentiment
finding](projects/us-policy-sentiment-cash-benefit/findings/us-policy-sentiment-cash-benefit-002.md):
benefit loss was associated with lower consumer sentiment, especially among
lower-income families with multiple children. It strengthens the material-
pressure-to-meaning stage, but leaves trust, spending, voting, and durable
political action open.

The latest place-and-mobility pass adds the [transportation cost-and-time
finding](projects/us-transportation-household-access/findings/us-transportation-household-access-001.md):
movement can create access to work and services while consuming a large share
of money and work time. The national BLS and Census surfaces are not joined to
the same households, so mode choice, reliability, welfare, and place
attachment remain open.
The latest housing-protection pass adds the [rent-guarantee finding](projects/us-rent-guarantee-insurance/findings/us-rent-guarantee-insurance-001.md):
a private guarantee can open a lease and protect the landlord while leaving
repayment risk with the renter. The model and provider terms are not a tenant
outcome study; approval, claims, reimbursement, eviction, recovery, and later
housing stability remain open.
The latest policy-uncertainty pass adds the [student-loan belief-and-action finding](projects/us-student-loan-policy-uncertainty/findings/us-student-loan-policy-uncertainty-001.md): expectations of forgiveness or payment pauses changed loan payments and nondurable spending before relief existed, while delinquency after payments resumed remained a separate risk.
The latest work-and-politics pass adds the [employment-and-voting finding](projects/us-employment-mothers-voting/findings/us-employment-mothers-voting-001.md): policy-induced employment among lower-income mothers was associated with lower turnout and civic engagement and more conservative policy preferences. The measured political endpoint does not identify the same mother's time, care, workplace, or household-security mechanism.
The latest firm-politics pass adds the [corporate-PAC reputational-risk finding](projects/us-corporate-pac-reputational-risk/findings/us-corporate-pac-reputational-risk-001.md): firms temporarily reduced visible political giving when issue salience made ties risky, then returned toward baseline. PAC visibility is not the same as lobbying, policy influence, or durable legitimacy.
The latest climate-geopolitics pass adds the [carbon-tariff finding](projects/us-carbon-tariff-climate-geopolitics/findings/us-carbon-tariff-climate-geopolitics-001.md): a tariff-only model can lower foreign emissions while raising US emissions, while a combined domestic-and-border policy performs better in the model. Actual incidence, partner response, and implementation remain open.
The latest geoeconomic-coercion pass adds the [target-selection finding](projects/us-geoeconomic-coercion-targets/findings/us-geoeconomic-coercion-targets-001.md): trade and financial dependence can make private firms immediate targets of state power; target selection, firm response, public cost, alliance behavior, and strategic outcome remain separate.
The follow-on retaliation pass adds the [external-response finding](projects/us-geoeconomic-coercion-targets/findings/us-geoeconomic-coercion-targets-002.md): foreign governments can answer tariff pressure with countermeasures and bargaining, while pauses or reductions remain distinct from compliance and strategic success.
The latest digital-exit pass adds the [social-coordination finding](projects/us-digital-habits-attention/findings/us-digital-habits-attention-016.md): short-run wellbeing can improve after deactivation while social dependence makes individual exit costly; actual deletion, replacement, and coordinated non-use remain open.
The follow-on FTC digital-risk pass adds the [social-scam finding](projects/us-digital-habits-attention/findings/us-digital-habits-attention-017.md): reported social-media scam losses reached $2.1 billion in 2025; true incidence, recovery, platform responsibility, and durable trust remain open.
The latest care/work pass adds the [leave-usability finding](projects/us-aging-care-strain/findings/us-aging-care-strain-019.md): formal leave access can coexist with unmet need because workload, job risk, and lost income block usable time; later care, health, job, and household outcomes remain open.
The latest housing/place pass adds the [housing-stock usability finding](projects/us-housing-insurance-risk/findings/us-housing-insurance-risk-013.md): nominal affordability can shrink sharply after availability and physical adequacy are added; household search, safety, lease-up, and durable staying remain open.
The latest public-safety pass adds the [victimization/reporting finding](projects/us-safety-net-access/findings/us-safety-net-access-025.md): lived victimization can exceed police-visible victimization; reporting, response, remedy, safety, trust, and later action remain open. The [NCVS/NIBRS comparison](projects/us-public-safety-measurement/findings/us-public-safety-measurement-001.md) adds that the survey and police-record rates have different coverage and denominators, so they cannot be treated as one neutral crime count. The [FEC campaign-finance finding](projects/us-political-participation/findings/us-political-participation-001.md) adds that political money is a resource channel, not a proxy for participation or influence. The [EAVS finding](projects/us-election-administration/findings/us-election-administration-001.md) adds the administrative conversion route from attempted ballot to counted ballot.
The latest cultural-time pass adds the [ATUS everyday-life finding](projects/us-aging-care-strain/findings/us-aging-care-strain-020.md): socializing, religious activity, and volunteering have different reach and timing; belonging, organizational continuity, and political action remain open.
The latest cultural-infrastructure pass adds the [arts/social-connectedness finding](projects/us-cost-trust-politics/findings/us-cost-trust-politics-066.md): arts participation is socially patterned and associated with reported connection; causal and durable effects remain open.
The latest school-safety pass adds the [student fear/avoidance finding](projects/us-school-safety/findings/us-school-safety-001.md): aggregate safety improvement can coexist with student fear and avoidance; participation, learning, support, and trust remain open.
The latest broadband-access pass adds the [availability/adoption finding](projects/us-digital-access/findings/us-digital-access-001.md): service availability is not subscription or usable access; price, quality, installation, and meaningful use remain open.
The latest nutrition-climate-health pass adds the [2026 Global Nutrition Report record](records/comparative-global-nutrition-report-2026-climate-health-financing-healthy-diet.json): 2.6 billion people cannot afford a healthy diet, while climate shocks and financing gaps can stress food systems and health systems together. The report’s integrated framework connects food access, health delivery, social protection, governance, financing, operational capacity, data, gender equity, and accountability; it remains a global publication synthesis rather than respondent-level ingestion, household microdata, or a causal estimate of climate effects.
The latest connected-consumer pass adds the [Assurant Connected Consumer Trends 2026 record](records/comparative-assurant-connected-consumer-trends-2026-technology-sentiment-support-ownership.json): device adoption is being evaluated through reliability, support, repair, trust, AI complexity, and total ownership cost. It is a company-sponsored aggregate survey and commercial report, not respondent-level ingestion, observed claims or transactions, a causal support-effect estimate, or proof of equal consumer benefit across markets.
The latest higher-education pass adds the [UNESCO Higher Education Global Trends 2026 record](records/comparative-unesco-higher-education-global-trends-2026-mobility-ai-equity.json): enrollment expansion is outpacing completion, equitable mobility, refugee access, gendered leadership, and institutional AI readiness. It is an aggregate comparative report, not respondent-level ingestion, student transcripts, causal higher-education-return estimates, or proof that expansion produces equal quality or opportunity.
The latest child-centred education pass adds the [UNICEF Education Strategy 2026 record](records/comparative-unicef-education-strategy-2026-learning-equity-skills-systems.json): education is being treated as social infrastructure for learning, equity, and future skills under technological, social, and environmental change. It is a public strategy, not respondent-level ingestion, student-level learning data, an implementation audit, a causal reform estimate, or proof of equal outcomes across countries.
The latest participation-and-cohesion pass adds the [OECD Cohesion Policy participation record](records/comparative-oecd-citizen-participation-cohesion-policy-2026-pilots-trust-capacity.json): participation can improve policy relevance and legitimacy when it has time, institutional capacity, feedback, and a real place in the policy cycle; late or weakly integrated input can become procedural. It is a public pilot synthesis, not respondent-level ingestion, a representative survey, a randomized causal estimate, or proof of durable outcomes.
The latest culture-and-consumer-signal pass adds the [Spate Global Culture Shifts 2026 record](records/comparative-spate-global-culture-shifts-2026-search-social-wellness.json): ambient pressure, nostalgia, self-optimization, wellness, analog practices, and spirituality are being translated into platform-visible demand and brand strategy. It is commercial trend forecasting, not respondent-level ingestion, raw search or social data, a representative survey, clinical evidence, a causal purchase model, or proof of durable global prevalence.
The latest cultural-measurement pass adds the [Eurostat culture-statistics guide 2026 record](records/comparative-eurostat-culture-statistics-2026-participation-barriers-measurement.json): official cultural-participation measures can expose cost, time, proximity, disability, income, household, digital, and public-spending conditions, while periodic modules and single-response barriers leave overlap unresolved. It is a methodological guide, not respondent-level ingestion, a current participation estimate, a causal effect, or a complete cultural census.

The latest agrifood-finance pass adds the [FAO-CIRAD climate-finance quality record](records/comparative-fao-cirad-climate-finance-quality-agrifood-2026-equity-governance-local-ownership.json): climate finance is being reframed as a quality, access, cost-of-capital, governance, equity, and local-ownership problem rather than a volume problem alone. It is official systems-level finance evidence, not respondent-level ingestion or a causal evaluation of projects.

The latest displacement-and-capacity pass adds the [UNHCR Global Appeal 2026 record](records/comparative-unhcr-global-appeal-2026-displacement-funding-inclusion-host-systems.json): forced displacement is being managed as a long-term protection, public-service, legal-status, livelihoods, host-system, and development-finance problem while humanitarian funding remains inadequate. It is a planning appeal, not a realized-outcome evaluation or respondent-level dataset.

The latest development-cooperation pass adds the [OECD-UNDP effectiveness progress record](records/comparative-undp-oecd-development-cooperation-effectiveness-2026-country-ownership-results-accountability.json): cooperation quality is being judged through country ownership, results, inclusive partnerships, transparency, and mutual accountability, with reporting from 44 partner governments and 93 development partners. It is a monitoring and policy report, not a causal impact evaluation or respondent-level dataset.

The latest cultural-technology pass adds the [UNESCO-ICOM museum AI record](records/comparative-unesco-icom-museum-ai-adoption-governance-capacity-2026.json): museum AI experimentation is advancing faster than internal policy, training, data governance, copyright, and institutional capacity. It is a survey summary, not respondent-level ingestion, a representative estimate of all museums, or a causal visitor-outcome study.

The latest macro-conditions pass adds the [World Bank long-term growth prospects record](records/comparative-world-bank-long-term-growth-prospects-2026-productivity-investment-demography-trade.json): potential growth is expected to reach a three-decade low as investment, productivity, labor-force expansion, and trade weaken, tightening the conditions for poverty reduction, climate action, public investment, and household opportunity. It is a conditional structural outlook, not realized welfare or a causal country-by-country forecast.

The latest operational-climate pass adds the [UNDP Greening Moonshot record](records/comparative-undp-greening-moonshot-2026-operations-emissions-energy-resilience.json): climate action is being operationalized through emissions reduction, green energy, efficiency, mobility, buildings, travel, savings, workplace safety, service continuity, and institutional credibility. It is a self-reported organizational mid-term report, not a global emissions estimate or causal evaluation.

The latest cultural-AI dialogue pass adds the [UN Office for Partnerships AI and Human Development dispatches](records/comparative-unop-ai-human-development-dispatches-2026-trust-culture-labor-knowledge.json): AI is being debated through trust, language, responsibility, cultural identity, knowledge ownership, embodied systems, household life, journalism, and labor—not only model capability. It is a selective qualitative account, not a representative survey or respondent-level dataset.

The latest democratic-institution pass adds the [citizens’ assemblies handbook record](records/comparative-newdemocracy-undef-undp-citizens-assemblies-2026-deliberation-agency-trust.json): civic lottery, time, information, deliberation, and follow-through are being treated as infrastructure for trusted participation beyond elections. It is a practice handbook, not respondent-level ingestion, a representative opinion dataset, or a causal evaluation.

The latest digital-resilience pass adds the [ITU-UNDRR digital-systems-failure record](records/comparative-itu-undrr-digital-systems-fail-2026-cascades-resilience.json): digital disruption is being treated as a cross-border cascade across essential services, with analogue fallback, dependency mapping, early warning, shared awareness, and coordination becoming resilience requirements. It is an expert scenario and preparedness report, not an incident census, prediction, respondent-level dataset, or causal evaluation.

The latest everyday-consumption pass adds the [NCA coffee data-trends record](records/comparative-nca-coffee-data-trends-2026-specialty-out-of-home-routines.json): coffee remains a widespread household routine while specialty formats, espresso drinks, out-of-home recovery, drive-through, and app ordering diversify the consumption occasion. It is a trade-association summary of a commissioned survey, not transaction data, respondent-level ingestion, a causal estimate, or proof of durable cultural change.

The latest care-finance pass adds the [PMNCH global health-financing record](records/comparative-pmnch-global-health-financing-crisis-2026-program-cuts-rights.json): funding uncertainty is being reported as program downsizing, suspension, closure, service prioritization, localization, and a demand for flexible financing and affected-group voice. It is a partner-network survey summary and advocacy publication, not a beneficiary-level outcome dataset, representative organizational census, or causal evaluation.

The latest poverty-measurement pass adds the [World Bank global poverty update](records/comparative-world-bank-global-poverty-update-2026-extreme-poverty-uneven-progress.json): extreme poverty remains a one-in-ten global condition in the 2024 estimate, with only marginal projected decline and sharply uneven regional progress. It is modeled survey-and-nowcast evidence, not respondent-level ingestion, a multidimensional deprivation measure, or a causal welfare evaluation.

The latest commodity-price pass adds the [World Bank Pink Sheet September 2026 record](records/comparative-world-bank-pink-sheet-september-2026-energy-food-fertilizer-metals.json): August benchmark prices show energy and metals pressure alongside food increases and fertilizer easing, creating uneven transmission risks for states, firms, farmers, and households. It is benchmark price/index evidence, not retail inflation, household expenditure, firm profit, or welfare data.

The latest conflict-health pass adds the [WHO Middle East situation report](records/comparative-who-middle-east-conflict-health-systems-2026-displacement-continuity.json): conflict is producing a compound systems problem through displacement, attacks on health care, damaged power and water infrastructure, medicine shortages, access limits, emergency adaptation, and underfunding. It is a dated operational situation report, not a harmonized conflict census, causal attribution, beneficiary panel, or outcome evaluation.

The latest public-health sensing pass adds the [WHO Weekly Epidemiological Record 101(38)](records/comparative-who-weekly-epidemiological-record-101-38-2026-surveillance-signals.json): public-health governance is operating as a signal-filtering, escalation, reporting-quality, laboratory, and prevention system rather than a simple event count. It is a surveillance publication, not a complete event census, population prevalence estimate, respondent-level dataset, or causal vaccine evaluation.

The latest education-AI governance pass adds the [UNESCO common-good consultation record](records/comparative-unesco-ai-education-common-good-deliberative-governance-2026.json): AI in education is being framed through agency, deliberation, sovereignty, safety, age appropriateness, procurement, total cost, explainability, equity, and inclusion. It is a discussion-paper and consultation record, not a learner-level dataset, adoption survey, outcome evaluation, or proof of equitable implementation.

The latest education-culture pass adds the [UNESCO multilateralism think-piece record](records/comparative-unesco-education-multilateralism-classroom-cooperation-2026.json): classrooms are being imagined as everyday spaces for cooperation across difference, local-global knowledge exchange, intellectual humility, and civic life without consensus. It is a qualitative IdeasLAB think piece, not a classroom study, learning-outcome evaluation, or causal evidence.

The latest climate-social-protection pass adds the [ILO-AFD social-insurance record](records/comparative-ilo-social-protection-climate-insurance-2026-just-transition.json): climate shocks and climate policies are being linked to lifecycle risk, social insurance, livelihood protection, work transitions, coverage gaps, and just-transition design. It is a literature review whose underlying analysis was conducted in 2022 and not updated, not a current coverage census, household panel, or causal evaluation.

The next labor/platform-economy pass adds the [ILO Cambodia digital-platform-workers record](records/comparative-ilo-cambodia-digital-platform-workers-2026-algorithmic-work-security.json): app-mediated work is expanding access to income while concentrating unpaid waiting, operating costs, algorithmic penalties, injury exposure, classification gaps, and weak collective voice. The evidence is a Phnom Penh diagnostic, not a national or causal estimate.

The next AI-health governance pass adds the [WHO AI-health-research ethics record](records/comparative-who-ai-health-research-ethics-2026-oversight-equity.json): AI diffusion is being treated as a lifecycle accountability problem involving ethics review, data power, public trust, institutional capacity, local participation, and the risk of data colonialism or ethics dumping. It is a governance framework, not an implementation audit or outcome evaluation.

The next housing-security pass adds the [OECD homelessness and housing-exclusion record](records/comparative-oecd-homelessness-housing-exclusion-2026-pathways-hidden.json): housing insecurity is a layered pathway through poverty, housing-market pressure, institutional transitions, relationships, health, employment and social support, with hidden homelessness routinely missed by visible counts. It is a literature review and cross-national survey synthesis, not a harmonized current prevalence census.

The next climate-politics pass adds the [OECD climate-sentiment and just-transition record](records/comparative-oecd-climate-sentiment-2026-just-transition-public-support.json): climate concern is broad, but support for action depends on perceived effectiveness, economic security, fair burden sharing, transparent compensation, and whether public norms and policy communication are credible. It is descriptive public-attitudes evidence, not a causal support forecast.

The next digital-identity pass adds the [W3C Identity & the Web record](records/comparative-w3c-identity-web-2026-privacy-governance-trust.json): digital credentials are being framed as social infrastructure where privacy, access, trust, cultural meaning, human rights, surveillance, interoperability and vendor power are negotiated through standards and governance. It is an exploratory W3C Team report, not a membership consensus, standard or deployment evaluation.

The next cultural-AI pass adds the [Government of Canada AI and Culture Summit record](records/comparative-canada-ai-culture-summit-2026-creator-sovereignty-trust.json): cultural-sector AI adoption is being negotiated through creator rights, copyright, trust, co-design, Indigenous and linguistic sovereignty, domestic infrastructure, literacy and institutional readiness. It is an organizer synthesis of a 314-person summit, not representative opinion or formal consensus.

The next community-culture pass adds the [Circle 2026 Community Trends record](records/comparative-circle-community-trends-2026-human-connection-belonging.json): amid AI-generated content and algorithmic noise, community is being marketed as connection, belonging, trust, retention and intentionally designed human space rather than attention alone. It is a commercial public summary, not representative population evidence or an independent causal evaluation.

The latest critical-risk governance pass adds the [OECD critical-risk governance record](records/comparative-oecd-critical-risk-governance-2026-polycrisis-preparedness.json): governments are broadening risk frameworks toward compound threats, anticipatory planning, whole-of-society participation and critical dependencies, while accountability, systematic learning, preparedness investment and cross-border cooperation lag. It is a comparative governance review, not a causal resilience evaluation or proof that formal arrangements translate into public safety.

The latest trade-infrastructure pass adds the [WEF TradeTech Paradox record](records/comparative-wef-tradetech-paradox-2026-connectivity-fragmentation.json): digital trade infrastructure is being positioned as connective tissue across fragmented geopolitics, linking institutions, regulators, logistics, finance and enterprises, while resilience still depends on governance, human capacity, ethics and interoperability. It is a strategic insight report with case studies, not a causal technology evaluation or proof of inclusive trade outcomes.

The latest housing-and-migration pass adds the [SVR Germany housing and participation record](records/comparative-svr-germany-housing-migration-2026-participation-neighborhoods.json): housing is being treated as integration infrastructure connecting affordability, tenure, neighbourhood resources, education, labour-market access, discrimination, social segregation and legal choice. It is Germany-specific annual-report evidence, not a U.S. estimate, respondent-level panel or causal neighbourhood evaluation.

The latest intercultural-dialogue pass adds the [UNESCO–IEP dialogue record](records/comparative-unesco-iep-intercultural-dialogue-2022-released-2026-peace-inclusion.json): dialogue is being measured and positioned as social infrastructure connecting identity, inclusion, vulnerability reduction, rights, conflict prevention, resilience and peace. The underlying report is a 2022 publication highlighted by UNESCO in 2026, not new 2026 outcome data or a causal evaluation.

The latest social-cohesion measurement pass adds the [World Bank social-cohesion record](records/comparative-world-bank-social-cohesion-development-outcomes-2023-bonding-bridging-linking.json): cohesion is being operationalized through bonding, bridging and linking relations, with diagnostics and interventions tied to community trust, intergroup cooperation and citizen-state relationships. It is a 2023 working-paper synthesis, not a current prevalence estimate or proof that cohesion programming produces durable peace.

The latest process-legitimacy pass adds the [World Bank process-legitimacy record](records/comparative-world-bank-process-legitimacy-2025-development-contestation.json): development change is being judged through procedural fairness, representation, negotiation and the treatment of groups that lose, across contests between social groups, elites, local and national authorities, and national and global actors. It is a 2025 analytical framework, not a legitimacy survey or causal project evaluation.

The latest AI-diffusion pass adds the [World Bank generative-AI use record](records/comparative-world-bank-generative-ai-use-2025-income-digital-divide.json): consumer-facing GenAI use is expanding rapidly but remains sharply stratified by country income, infrastructure, platform concentration and localization. It is web-traffic proxy evidence through mid-2025, not a complete adoption, task-exposure, productivity or household-welfare estimate.

The latest creative-work pass adds the [UK Creative Industries AI Adoption Plan record](records/comparative-uk-creative-industries-ai-adoption-2026-augmentation-trust-skills.json): AI adoption is being negotiated through augmentation, creative control, trust, rights, skills, firm-size gaps, entry routes and regional infrastructure rather than automation alone. It is a UK policy plan using cited business evidence and case studies, not a causal workforce evaluation.

The latest child-digital-safety pass adds the [ITU Protection through Online Participation record](records/comparative-itu-pop-child-online-protection-2026-reporting-support-pathways.json): online environments are being treated as both risk surfaces and entry points for children to report harm, seek help and reach protection services. It is global policy guidance, not an incident census, platform audit or implementation evaluation.

The latest ageing-and-AI pass adds the [ITU AI and ageing record](records/comparative-itu-ai-ageing-2026-digital-inclusion-health-security-participation.json): AI is being evaluated through health, economic security, participation and connectivity, with age, gender, geography, affordability, skills and trust shaping the distribution of benefits. It is a global policy and practice synthesis, not a later-life outcome evaluation.

The latest shock-smoothing pass adds the [World Bank international risk-sharing record](records/comparative-world-bank-international-risk-sharing-government-private-saving-2026-shock-smoothing.json): public saving, private precautionary saving, remittances, transfers and investment income absorb international shocks differently across advanced economies and EMDE, with fiscal space and capital-account openness shifting the balance. It is aggregate accounting evidence, not respondent-level or household causal evidence.

The latest housing-resilience pass adds the [World Bank housing robustness record](records/comparative-world-bank-housing-robustness-2026-poverty-disaster-learning.json): safer housing follows a two-stage ladder in which poverty constrains the removal of fragile housing and governance shapes hazard-specific learning from repeated disasters. It is cross-country aggregate evidence, not an event-damage audit or household causal evaluation.

The latest adaptive-protection pass adds the [World Bank disaster-poverty record](records/comparative-world-bank-adaptive-social-protection-2026-disaster-poverty-coverage-intensity.json): disaster policy must trade off how many people are reached against the intensity of support, with the optimal sequence varying by measure and country income. It is modeled cross-country scenario evidence, not a disaster-loss census or implementation audit.

The latest AI-skills pass adds the [OECD AI and skills record](records/comparative-oecd-ai-skills-2026-adoption-training-human-work.json): AI adoption is constrained by skills while raising demand for high-level, digital, data and human capabilities, making training, social dialogue and job-quality monitoring part of the technology transition. It synthesizes OECD survey evidence, not a causal training evaluation or complete labor-market forecast.

The latest everyday-health pass adds the [PwC Voice of the Consumer record](records/comparative-pwc-voice-consumer-2026-everyday-health-data-trust.json): health is becoming a continuous consumer practice spanning food, sleep, wearables, diagnostics, coaching, AI, pharmacies and trusted human guidance, with affordability and data trust shaping participation. It is a commercial survey and market-insight publication, not clinical or causal evidence.

The latest Generation Equality pass adds the [UN Women institutionalized feminist systems-change record](records/comparative-un-women-generation-equality-2026-institutionalized-feminist-systems-change.json): commitments become more durable when financing, accountability, shared feminist and youth leadership, public-system integration and data-driven learning are institutionalized. It is a program learning synthesis with selected cases, not a causal implementation evaluation.

The latest Pacific data-system pass adds the [UN Women Pacific Gender Data Outlook Index record](records/comparative-un-women-pacific-gender-data-outlook-index-2026-data-capacity-accessibility.json): gender-data capacity is policy infrastructure when enabling laws, financing, production, accessibility and use connect evidence to priorities, resources and accountability. It covers 11 of 22 Pacific jurisdictions and is a diagnostic, not an outcome or causal index.

The latest belonging pass adds the [Belonging Forum UK Belonging Barometer record](records/comparative-belonging-forum-uk-belonging-barometer-2026-place-power-purpose.json): belonging acts as a practical barometer of whether relationships, local places, economic room, services and institutions make participation feel possible and fair. It is a nationally weighted UK survey, not a causal or longitudinal evaluation.

The latest digital-systems pass adds the [DataReportal Digital 2026 Mid-Year record](records/comparative-datareportal-digital-2026-midyear-ai-social-media-connectivity.json): connectivity, social media and AI are expanding together, but aggregate scale hides different definitions, offline populations, platform dependence and unequal quality of access. It is a multi-source estimate and survey synthesis, not a direct global census or causal welfare study.

The latest AI-attitudes pass adds the [Public First AI Global 2026 record](records/comparative-public-first-ai-global-2026-backlash-sovereignty-sophistication.json): adoption is broadening faster than trust, capability, privacy comfort and sovereignty arrangements can stabilize. It combines a 15-country survey with economic modeling, not observed causal outcomes.

The latest consumer-outlook pass adds the [NIQ Consumer Outlook 2026 record](records/comparative-niq-consumer-outlook-2026-financial-caution-trust-commerce.json): households are protecting essentials, seeking secondary income as insurance, and demanding lived value, local relevance and autonomy across physical and digital commerce. It combines survey and market-measurement evidence, not a household panel or causal welfare evaluation.

The latest hybrid-value pass adds the [Capgemini consumer 2026 record](records/comparative-capgemini-consumer-2026-value-ai-human-touch-fairness.json): consumers want fair prices and reliable quality, but also controlled AI convenience and meaningful human support at complex or trust-sensitive moments. It is a 12-country survey with executive interviews, not transaction or causal evidence.

The latest cultural-identity pass adds the [Culturati Cultural Mosaic 5.0 record](records/comparative-culturati-cultural-mosaic-2026-polycultural-identity-creators-ai.json): cultural identity, trusted voices, creators and AI-mediated discovery are treated as one consumer decision system across Hispanic, Black, Asian and General Market audiences. The public overview is a scope signal, not the full syndicated findings.

The latest civic-geopolitical pass adds the [CIVICUS 2026 State of Civil Society record](records/comparative-civicus-state-civil-society-2026-resistance-civic-space-geopolitics.json): democratic retreat, impunity, technology power, civic-space contraction, funding pressure and distributed resistance are read as a linked global condition. It is an advocacy and analytical synthesis using selected cases, not a representative movement census or causal estimate.

The latest labor-market pass adds the [ILO Employment and Social Trends 2026 record](records/comparative-ilo-employment-social-trends-2026-fragile-stability-job-quality.json): stable headline unemployment coexists with a large jobs gap, informality, working poverty, weak income recovery, unequal productivity and persistent gender and youth exclusion. These are ILO estimates and projections, not respondent-level or causal evidence.

The latest food-culture pass adds the [Circana Eating Patterns in America 2026 record](records/comparative-circana-eating-patterns-america-2026-food-occasions-channels.json): eating is measured as a connected system of occasions, home and away-from-home contexts, channels, preparation infrastructure and commercial decisions. The public overview provides scope and method signals, not the full findings or household-level causal evidence.

The latest institutional-confidence pass adds the [Gallup Global Confidence in Institutions 20-Year Review record](records/comparative-gallup-global-confidence-institutions-20-year-2026-regime-trust.json): global confidence is not in universal decline but diverges by institution, regime type, country and political position. It is a World Poll trend analysis using country medians and subgroup associations, not a causal legitimacy index.

The latest poverty-fragility pass adds the [World Bank Fragility at the Core of Global Poverty record](records/comparative-world-bank-poverty-fragility-2026-feedback-data-conflict.json): extreme poverty is increasingly concentrated in fragile and conflict-affected settings, with poverty and fragility reinforcing one another. The public evidence reviewed is a reproducibility catalog and data architecture, not a complete causal intervention evaluation.

The latest climate-mobility pass adds the [JRC Climate Security, Migration and Fragility record](records/comparative-jrc-climate-security-migration-fragility-2026-risk-multiplier-resilience.json): climate stress is framed as a risk multiplier whose effects depend on existing vulnerabilities, while mobility, remittances, social protection, safe pathways and inclusive urbanization can support resilience under effective institutions. It is a policy brief, not a global causal event study.

The latest humanitarian-capacity pass adds the [OCHA Global Humanitarian Overview 2026 record](records/comparative-ocha-global-humanitarian-overview-2026-needs-funding-service-collapse.json): conflict and climate shocks create needs, but funding contraction and response capacity determine whether health, food, protection, cash, water and shelter lifelines continue. It is a humanitarian planning and system assessment, not evidence of individual receipt or durable recovery.

The latest organizational-AI pass adds the [Deloitte Global Human Capital Trends 2026 record](records/comparative-deloitte-human-capital-trends-2026-human-machine-adaptation-trust.json): organizations are moving from balancing AI-related tensions to making deliberate choices about human–machine work, adaptation, trust and cultural debt. It is executive and leader survey evidence, not proof of worker outcomes or causal productivity gains.

The latest professional-workforce pass adds the [ACCA Global Talent Trends 2026 record](records/comparative-acca-global-talent-trends-2026-ai-hiring-hybrid-work-professional-trust.json): professionals report rising AI use and confidence in learning while expressing a legitimacy boundary around human judgment, hybrid flexibility, promotion visibility, social impact and organizational support. It is an aggregate accountancy and finance professional survey/report, not a representative general-workforce panel, respondent-level dataset or causal evaluation.

The latest human-purpose pass adds the [ITDF Human Purpose in Peril record](records/comparative-itdf-human-purpose-peril-2026-ai-meaning-agency-social-bonds.json): Americans are adopting LLMs as everyday learning, shopping, health, work, finance, housing and social-rehearsal infrastructure while fearing losses of purpose, agency, critical thinking and human connection. It is a nationally representative U.S. adult survey with an LLM-user oversample, not a longitudinal behavioral or causal social-outcome study.

The latest civic-conversation pass adds the [More in Common/UCL Disagreeing Well record](records/comparative-more-in-common-disagreeing-well-2026-silence-civic-spaces.json): Britons value open disagreement and understanding but often withhold views, fear negative labels, avoid formal forums and see political institutions modeling poor disagreement. It is a Britain-focused polling and focus-group synthesis, not a longitudinal behavioral or causal polarization study.

The latest geopolitical-public-opinion pass adds the [Rockefeller Foundation/Focaldata global cooperation record](records/comparative-rockefeller-focaldata-global-cooperation-2026-security-leadership-aid.json): publics feel the world worsening but still want cooperation tied to preventing conflict, aid, national benefit and demonstrable results, with fragmented support for U.S. or Chinese leadership and stronger interest in coalitions. It is a 34-country public-opinion survey with country medians and an information treatment, not observed foreign-policy behavior or causal geopolitical change.

The latest climate-consent pass adds the [Potential Energy Fixing Climate Communications record](records/comparative-potential-energy-climate-communications-2026-cost-health-energy-independence.json): climate support broadens when communication makes pollution, health, household cost, protection and energy independence concrete, while abstract net-zero, ban, mandate and sacrifice language can narrow the audience. It combines six-country surveys, focus groups and randomized message tests, not durable behavior or climate-policy outcome evidence.

The latest social-connection pass adds the [Meta-Gallup Global State of Social Connections record](records/comparative-meta-gallup-global-social-connections-2023-142-countries-loneliness.json): most people report feeling connected, but loneliness is substantial, uneven across countries and higher among young adults than older adults, while connection and loneliness remain distinct. It is representative 2022-2023 survey evidence published in 2023, not current monitoring or causal health evidence.

The latest culture-infrastructure pass adds the [4Culture King County Culture and Communities record](records/comparative-4culture-king-county-culture-communities-2026-participation-barriers-belonging.json): cultural participation is widely experienced and valued as connection, identity, learning, wellbeing and community quality of life, while cost, time, caregiving and accessibility shape depth and equity. It is a regional program-baseline survey with self-reported outcomes, not a national census or causal impact study.

The latest well-being-measurement pass adds the [OECD Slovak Republic well-being record](records/comparative-oecd-slovak-wellbeing-2026-policy-coherence-dashboard.json): well-being dashboards are becoming policy-coordination infrastructure linking material conditions, quality of life, relationships, sustainability, inequality and future resources to strategy, appraisal and budgeting. Slovakia shows mixed progress—some material and life-satisfaction gains alongside education, housing, poverty, debt and trust problems—so measurement must preserve trade-offs and distribution rather than collapse progress into GDP or one score.

The latest institutional-confidence pass adds the [OECD Slovak trust record](records/comparative-oecd-slovak-trust-2026-public-services-voice-ai.json): national-government trust fell to 23% in 2025, while political voice, benefit fairness, data legitimacy, evidence use and confidence in government AI safeguards also weakened. It links legitimacy to concrete service encounters and decision processes, not just abstract attitudes; it is representative survey evidence and cross-country association, not observed administrative performance or causal political behavior.

The latest culture-and-emergency pass adds UNESCO’s [Voices of Courage record](records/comparative-unesco-voices-courage-2026-women-culture-emergencies-resilience.json): emergencies intensify women’s cultural-work precarity through livelihood loss, unsafe workspaces, displacement, unpaid care, restricted expression and weak support, while women’s cultural labor sustains heritage, solidarity and recovery. It is a global survey with testimonies and case studies, not a complete crisis census or causal resilience evaluation.

The latest health-economy pass adds the [WHO Economics of Health for All record](records/comparative-who-economics-health-for-all-2026-cross-sector-policy-finance.json): health is framed as an economic-system outcome shaped by tax, trade, industry, labor, care, commercial practices, social protection, finance, climate and public participation. It is a draft global strategy and policy framework, not an implementation evaluation or proof that cross-sector coordination improves health.

The latest Latino mobility-and-meaning pass adds the [Pew American Dream record](records/comparative-pew-latinos-american-dream-2026-mobility-identity-household-security.json): financial security, opportunity, homeownership, family/community wellbeing, freedom and equal treatment are fused into a cultural account of economic mobility. Latino optimism about intergenerational progress coexists with a sense that the dream is harder to reach, especially across nativity and generation. It is representative survey and open-ended coding evidence, not longitudinal mobility or household wealth data.

The latest identity-and-belonging pass adds the [Pew Latino identity record](records/comparative-pew-latino-identity-2026-belonging-discrimination-generation-politics.json): identity is central for 61% of Latino adults, but 33% say it hurts their ability to get ahead and reported discrimination remains widespread. Identity importance, self-labels and linked fate shift across immigrant generations without simply disappearing. It is representative survey evidence, not an administrative discrimination census or causal political-behavior study.

The latest Muslim-American identity pass adds the [Pew Muslim Americans record](records/comparative-pew-muslim-americans-2026-25-years-911-identity-discrimination-political-visibility.json): demographic and political visibility have grown since 9/11 while discrimination, unfair-news perceptions and polarized public views of Muslims and Islam remain persistent. The source joins public opinion, Muslim American experience, FBI reported incidents, population and mosque estimates, and representation without treating them as one causal series.

The latest social-exclusion systems pass adds the [Swedish SOU 2026:53 record](records/comparative-sweden-sou-2026-53-parallel-social-structures-integration-exclusion-trust.json): a government inquiry frames social exclusion as an interaction among segregation, discrimination, institutional distrust, informal systems and constrained rights, while explicitly warning that its politically charged concept has no single definition or prevalence measure. It is a policy knowledge base and recommendation set, not a national causal evaluation.

The latest AI-distribution pass adds the [TASC Ireland AI and Inequality record](records/comparative-tasc-ireland-ai-inequality-2026-work-distribution-voice.json): AI exposure, complementarity, gender, digital access, algorithmic management, ownership, worker voice and global environmental costs are treated as one distributional question. It is an independent think-tank synthesis, not a representative worker panel or causal forecast.

The latest Ireland household-inequality pass adds the [TASC State We Are In 2026 record](records/comparative-tasc-ireland-inequality-2026-household-living-standards-housing.json): taxes and transfers reduce measured disposable-income inequality while real purchasing power, housing tenure, deprivation, emergency accommodation and access to essential services remain sharply unequal. It is an independent think-tank synthesis of official and secondary evidence, not a new household panel or causal evaluation.

The latest US youth-health pass adds the [CDC Youth Health in Focus 2025 record](records/us-cdc-youth-health-in-focus-2025.json): mental-health and dietary indicators improved between 2023 and 2025, while sleep, daily activity, physical education and risky digital behavior remain stubborn constraints. It is nationally representative YRBS evidence summarized by CDC, not a causal intervention evaluation or proof that gains are evenly distributed.

The latest US consumer-reality pass adds the [TruStage What Matters Now 2026 record](records/us-trustage-what-matters-now-2026-financial-reality-consumer-inclusion.json): financial strain, race, generation and neurodivergence shape how consumers interpret security, while family influence, digital channels, app experience and community relevance shape financial-service discovery and institutional choice. It is commissioned Ipsos/TruStage consumer research, not objective household-finance data or a causal evaluation.

The latest US social-connection pass adds the [RAND–Connection Institute Social Connection in America 2026 record](records/us-rand-connection-institute-social-connection-america-2026-weak-ties-time-community.json): loneliness is embedded in time scarcity, infrequent contact, weak formal participation, limited cross-background interaction and skepticism that virtual contact substitutes for in-person connection. It is a public summary of a new national tracking initiative, with incomplete public sampling and weighting detail, not a causal social-isolation evaluation.

The latest US young-men culture pass adds the [Equimundo State of American Men 2026 record](records/us-equimundo-state-american-men-2026-economic-insecurity-ai-manhood-democracy.json): economic insecurity and nostalgia coexist with prediction-market use, AI adoption, influencer commerce, contested masculinity, online-to-in-person connection and conditional democratic commitment. It is a mixed-method YouGov/Equimundo/YMRP study of young adults, not a causal account of men, platforms, or political behavior.

The latest US civic-literacy pass adds the [Annenberg Civics Knowledge 2026 record](records/us-annenberg-civics-knowledge-2026-constitutional-literacy-rights.json): Americans recognize some high-salience constitutional rights and procedures while many cannot name the branches of government or the full First Amendment. It is a nationally representative knowledge survey, not a measure of legal competence, political behavior, or democratic performance.

The latest US AI-infrastructure pass adds the [Annenberg Data Centers 2026 record](records/us-annenberg-data-centers-2026-local-opposition-ai-regulation-community-legitimacy.json): opposition to local data centers rose from 49% to 61% while broad AI expectations and demand for regulation remained statistically stable. It is a nationally representative public-opinion survey, not an assessment of actual energy, privacy, employment, environmental, or neighborhood effects.

The latest US climate-health pass adds the [Annenberg Heat and Health 2026 record](records/us-annenberg-heat-health-2026-climate-cost-routines-cooling-access.json): extreme heat and wildfire smoke are affecting daily routines, utility costs, sleep, exercise and outdoor activity, while cooling-center awareness and pregnancy-specific risk knowledge remain limited. It is nationally representative public-health survey evidence, not clinical outcome or adaptation-effectiveness data.

The latest US public-health-capacity pass adds the [TFAH Underfunded and Under Strain 2026 record](records/us-tfah-public-health-system-2026-underfunding-workforce-capacity.json): only about 3% of national health expenditures went to governmental public health in 2024, CDC staffing fell by roughly 28% from FY2024 to May 2026, and grants alone did not guarantee operational capacity. It is a nonprofit policy analysis using cited administrative and survey evidence, not a causal national health evaluation.

The latest US public-health-worker pass adds the [PH WINS Pulse Check 2026 record](records/us-debeaumont-phwins-pulse-2026-public-health-worker-strain-retention-politics.json): 35% report worsening burnout, 47% say outside politics negatively affect their work unit, and 31% of workers age 35 and younger plan to leave within a year. It is a follow-up workforce pulse among prior respondents, not a census of public-health workers or a causal retention evaluation.

The latest US health-politics pass adds the [Nature Human Behaviour political-health polarization record](records/us-nature-political-health-polarization-2026-ideology-trust-care-mortality.json): a longitudinal Add Health analysis finds no significant liberal–conservative biomarker or mortality gap before the 2010s but worse health and higher mortality among conservatives during the 2010s and by the 2020s; a separate contemporary survey is consistent with a medical-trust and care-engagement mechanism. It is observational and does not establish that ideology causes mortality.

The latest US housing pass adds the [JCHS State of the Nation’s Housing 2026 record](records/us-jchs-state-nations-housing-2026-affordability-household-formation-mobility.json): housing affordability is now constraining household formation, residential mobility, homeownership entry, and renter security together. It links a structural housing-market shift to household room and life-course timing without treating aggregate housing estimates as individual histories.

The latest US healthcare-access pass adds the [Commonwealth Fund coverage-denials record](records/us-commonwealth-coverage-denials-2026-care-delay-debt-trust.json): one in five privately insured working-age adults reported a denial of doctor-recommended care, with downstream delay, worsening health, added cost, debt, appeal time, and trust loss. It is a survey-and-focus-group mechanism record, not an adjudication of insurer error or a causal clinical estimate.

The latest US belonging pass adds the [Belonging Barometer 2026 record](records/us-american-immigration-council-belonging-barometer-2026-local-agency-safety.json): local psychological safety and relationships are stronger than perceived influence over local decisions, with age, income, and local-news use associated with stronger belonging. It measures perceived belonging and agency, not objective inclusion or causal civic impact.

The latest US worker-experience pass adds the [Federal Reserve Worker Perspectives 2026 record](records/us-fed-worker-perspectives-2026-survival-mode-job-quality-ai-screening.json): under cost pressure, low- to moderate-income workers describe shifting from mobility and long-term investment toward survival, staying put, side work, and defensive job search. It is qualitative context behind labor statistics, not a national prevalence estimate or causal evaluation.

The latest US security-workforce pass adds the [Berkeley Labor Center security guard record](records/us-berkeley-security-guard-workforce-2026-job-quality-public-safety.json): private security now performs frontline public-safety work, but low wages, thin benefits, high turnover, uneven training, and elevated injury risk weaken the readiness that sites and the public rely on. It links job quality to service reliability without treating the report’s synthesis as a universal causal estimate.

The latest US education-workforce pass adds the [RAND State of the American Teacher 2026 record](records/us-rand-state-american-teacher-2026-wellbeing-retention-side-work.json): nationally representative teacher data show persistent well-being pressure, 18% intending to leave, and 30% holding outside jobs averaging 13 hours weekly. It links teacher working conditions to school capacity while keeping the distinction between retention intent and actual departures.

The latest US entertainment-consumption pass adds the [Luminate 2026 Midyear record](records/us-luminate-midyear-2026-music-tv-film-consumption-diversity-ai.json): streaming growth now coexists with Spanish-language expansion, a CD rebound, library-content dominance, younger theater attendance, fandom spending, and selective AI adoption. It reads commercial engagement as a cultural signal, not as a direct measure of household welfare or identity change.

The latest US food-household pass adds the [ReFED American Households 2026 record](records/us-refed-households-2026-grocery-price-behavior-food-waste.json): grocery price pressure is changing shopping, preparation, storage, leftover use, and reported waste, but the same roughly $50 weekly increase lands nearly seven times harder as a share of income for households under $40,000 than for households earning $150,000 or more. It keeps waste reduction distinct from food security and health.

The latest US digital-participation pass adds the [Purdue mobile-dependent households record](records/us-purdue-mobile-dependent-households-2026-digital-participation.json): no-internet access declined while mobile-only and smartphone-only access expanded, so connectivity now needs to be read through device capacity, data limits, affordability, disability, housing, and place. It is a tract-level comparison with explicit non-causal and non-service-outcome boundaries.

The latest US financial-resilience pass adds the [Financial Health Pulse 2026 record](records/us-finhealth-pulse-2026-financial-vulnerability-insurance-confidence.json): vulnerability rose while bill payment, debt manageability, planning, essential-cost confidence, and insurance confidence weakened, with sharper pressure among low-income and student-loan households. It is a recurring national financial-health signal, not a causal household panel or claims/default audit.

The latest US climate-health pass adds the [Climate Central heat-illness record](records/us-climate-central-heat-illness-er-visits-2026-climate-health-burden.json): modeled climate attribution accounts for 27% of the US warm-season heat-related emergency-department visit rate during 2018–2025, with regional and event peaks that convert climate exposure into hospital demand and household burden. It is an attribution model and response pathway, not an individual causal or adaptation-effectiveness estimate.

The latest US aging-capacity pass adds the [America’s Health Rankings 2026 Senior Report record](records/us-ahr-senior-report-2026-aging-care-workforce-food-behavioral-health.json): the older population is expanding, care workforce growth is geographically uneven, several behavioral-health risks are worsening, and food insecurity persists despite increased nutrition-support reach. It is a multi-source status synthesis, not a causal aging or care-adequacy evaluation.

The latest US institutional-functioning pass adds the [Freedom House United States 2026 record](records/us-freedom-house-2026-institutional-functioning-rights-oversight.json): the country remains classified Free, but the assessment records declines in aggregate freedom, legislative-executive functioning, anticorruption safeguards, and political-expression protections alongside continued elections, courts, protest, and civic resilience. It is an expert-coded country assessment, not a causal political-behavior study or legal ruling.

The latest US macro-geopolitical pass adds the [CBO Budget and Economic Outlook 2026–2036 record](records/us-cbo-outlook-2026-2036-tariffs-immigration-aging-ai-growth.json): fiscal stimulus, tariffs, reduced net immigration, population aging, AI investment, trade adjustment, and debt jointly shape the forecast for growth, prices, labor supply, and household room. It is a baseline forecast, not a realized policy effect or distributional household history.

The latest US civic-infrastructure pass adds the [GAO public-library facilities record](records/us-gao-public-library-facilities-2026-deferred-maintenance-civic-infrastructure.json): libraries are expected to provide technology, programming, community, voting, and emergency capacity while 38% report a poor building system, 70% report deferred maintenance, and 61% report a potential health or safety concern. It connects physical assets to public capacity without claiming a causal participation effect.

The latest US municipal-infrastructure pass adds the [NLC Municipal Infrastructure Conditions 2026 record](records/us-nlc-municipal-infrastructure-conditions-2026-finance-water-mobility.json): cities prioritize water, sewer, stormwater, roads, and treatment while rising costs, workforce shortages, local-funding dependence, and limited financing capacity shape what can be repaired. It is a municipal survey and funding synthesis, not a national engineering audit or resident-outcome study.

The latest US energy-affordability pass adds the [Duke Cost of Keeping Cool record](records/us-duke-energy-affordability-extreme-heat-2026-cooling-access.json): county-level energy burden, electricity-dependent household safety, extreme-heat compounding, cooling access, and policy design belong in the same affordability map. It is a bounded report synthesis using modeled county estimates and an abstract-level source description, not a household-bill, clinical-outcome, or policy-effectiveness evaluation.

The latest US home-environment pass adds the [EIA 2024 RECS housing-characteristics record](records/us-eia-recs-2024-home-energy-devices-routines-2026.json): the home is simultaneously an energy system, media environment, appliance infrastructure, and smart-device interface, with persistent regional and housing-type differences. It is preliminary housing-characteristics evidence, not a household-bill, causal-savings, attention, or final consumption/expenditure estimate.

The latest US civic-foresight pass adds the [Civic Life in America 2036 record](records/us-civic-futures-civic-life-2036-ai-trust-connection.json): participation-trust gaps, AI-mediated relationships, social connection, economic precarity, and polarization are used to frame four alternative civic futures. It is a scenario and expert-interview synthesis, not a forecast, representative survey, or observed AI-governance outcome.

The latest US media-politics pass adds the [Gallup influencer-audiences record](records/us-gallup-influencer-audiences-democratic-norms-2026.json): political podcaster and influencer audiences diverge from their partisan peers, combining stronger protest support with sharper skepticism about institutional accountability. It is a small-subgroup, observational survey analysis, not evidence of creator causality, persuasion, or realized political action.

The latest US election-legitimacy pass adds the [Gallup election-concerns record](records/us-gallup-election-concerns-democratic-legitimacy-2026.json): concern spans pressure on election officials, ballot handling, intimidation, political money, gerrymandering, and leader accountability, with partisan and social-media-use differences. It is cross-sectional perception evidence, not an election-security audit or turnout forecast.

The latest US-anchored geopolitical pass adds the [MPI migration-diplomacy record](records/us-mpi-migration-diplomacy-transactionalism-2026-geopolitical-leverage.json): migration is increasingly negotiated through visas, sanctions, tariffs, aid, labor pathways, security, and bilateral leverage as multilateral cooperation frays. It is a comparative policy synthesis, not a causal evaluation of U.S. policy or migrant outcomes.

The latest US labor-supply pass adds the [Business Economics immigration-policy record](records/us-business-economics-immigration-policy-labor-supply-2026.json): immigration is linked to population and labor-supply growth, immigrant-intensive industries, fiscal incidence, and the amount of employment growth compatible with stable unemployment. It is based on a public abstract and panel summary, not a causal wage, price, fiscal, or household-outcome estimate.

The latest US behavioral-health pass adds the [SAMHSA 2025 NSDUH record](records/us-samhsa-nsduh-2025-behavioral-health-youth-adult-treatment-2026.json): several adolescent and young-adult indicators improved or held steady from 2021 to 2025, while adult mental-health burden remains large, treatment receipt varies by age, and substance use reaches a broad population. It is national survey evidence, not a causal account of the improvements, treatment quality, or individual clinical need.

The latest US cultural-health pass adds the [UF-UCL arts-engagement record](records/us-ufl-ucl-arts-engagement-time-use-2003-2023-2026.json): daily arts participation is low but stable across two decades, while creative activity shifts toward home and solitary settings after accelerating around the pandemic. It is time-use and researcher-interpretation evidence, not a causal health-benefit estimate or annual participation rate.

The latest US youth-work pass adds the [Pew teen summer-employment record](records/us-pew-teen-summer-employment-1948-2026-work-socialization.json): summer employment has partly rebounded but remains far below historical levels, varies by age and race/ethnicity, and is concentrated in hospitality, retail, and logistics. It is descriptive federal employment evidence, not a causal account of youth opportunity, job quality, or household necessity.

The latest US education-capacity pass adds the [NCES Condition of Education 2025 record](records/us-nces-condition-education-2025-enrollment-learning-pathways.json): public K-12 enrollment is lower in many states, at-home instruction remains above its pre-pandemic level, undergraduate and postbaccalaureate routes diverge, and learning outcomes and labor-market returns move on different tracks. It is an indicator synthesis, not a causal education-quality or credential-return evaluation.

The latest US mobility-infrastructure pass adds the [FHWA Our Nation's Highways 2026 record](records/us-fhwa-our-nations-highways-2026-mobility-dependency.json): motor vehicles dominate passenger trips, roads organize work/family/social routines, arterials carry disproportionate travel, local roads provide disproportionate access, and trucking carries most domestic freight by weight. It is a national infrastructure description, not a household transport-cost, safety, emissions, or inequality estimate.

The latest US grid-capacity pass adds the [DOE National Transmission Needs Study record](records/us-doe-national-transmission-needs-2026-ai-manufacturing-electrification.json): AI data centers, manufacturing, electrification, storms, congestion, and interregional reliability are turning transmission into a growth and affordability planning problem. It is a draft needs assessment, not a project, household-rate, reliability, or realized-consumer-benefit evaluation.

The latest US climate-governance pass adds the [National Academies drought-assessment record](records/us-national-academies-drought-assessment-nonstationarity-2026.json): changing drought conditions are making historical baselines less reliable and require connected monitoring, impact data, early warning, water management, infrastructure, and adaptation planning. It is a consensus framework, not a household-loss estimate or proof of adaptation effectiveness.

The latest US shared-institution pass adds the [Knight-NORC public-library record](records/us-knight-norc-public-library-value-services-2026.json): libraries retain broad legitimacy by bundling information, technology, learning, work-navigation, and community-space functions, even as valuation intensity varies across groups. It is a perception survey, not a usage, service-quality, fiscal-return, or funding-cut impact evaluation.

The latest US care-and-life-course pass adds the [AARP/National Alliance for Caregiving sandwich-generation record](records/us-aarp-sandwich-generation-caregiving-2026-work-family-compression.json): nearly 17 million caregivers are simultaneously raising children and caring for adults, with similar weekly hours but greater care intensity, medical-task exposure, work disruption, physical strain, and financial strain. It is subgroup survey evidence, not a causal estimate of family status, care adequacy, or policy effect.

The latest US national-mood pass adds the [Pew 250th-anniversary national-mood record](records/us-pew-national-mood-250th-2026-future-expectations-polarization.json): present dissatisfaction and long-horizon pessimism coexist with modestly improved expectations since 2023, while party control and age strongly organize how people read the country’s direction.
