It is repeated cross-sectional opinion evidence, not a causal account of material conditions, mobilization, or future outcomes.

The latest US cultural-institution pass adds the [AAM Museums & Public Opinion 2026 record](records/us-aam-museums-public-opinion-2026-cultural-civic-economic-legitimacy.json): museums retain broad perceived legitimacy as educational, economic, informational, and civic assets, with strong stated support for public funding and limited support for content censorship. It is a public report with limited methodological disclosure, not a usage, access, learning, local economic, or causal trust evaluation.

The latest US public-power pass adds the [UMass corruption and economic-attribution record](records/us-umass-corruption-public-power-2026-partisan-double-standard-costs.json): near-universal belief that federal corruption exists coexists with shared standards for public-power abuse but partisan disagreement over which institution is culpable, alongside price pressure and political attribution. It is national opinion evidence, not verified corruption, causal price attribution, enforcement, or behavioral evidence.

The latest US-to-world pass adds the [Pew U.S. role in the world record](records/us-pew-role-world-2026-partisan-global-legitimacy-intervention.json): Americans sharply divide over whether U.S. power is respected, peaceful, attentive to other countries, or strengthening, while broad majorities still describe the U.S. as interfering abroad. It is public-opinion evidence about perceived global legitimacy, not a measure of foreign-policy conduct, partner trust, or geopolitical outcomes.

The latest US cultural-demographic pass adds the [Pew Hispanic identity and counting record](records/us-pew-hispanic-identity-counting-2026-population-race-ancestry.json): a rapidly growing Hispanic population is simultaneously being shaped by Census categories, racial self-identification, ancestry, generation, Afro-Latino identity, and intermarriage. It is a demographic and survey synthesis, not a unified market, electorate, or assimilation trajectory.

The latest US AI-infrastructure pass adds the [Pew data-center public-opinion record](records/us-pew-data-centers-2026-local-legitimacy-energy-costs-community-consent.json): as awareness rises, more Americans connect data centers to environmental, home-energy, quality-of-life, jobs, and tax concerns, and 60% are uncomfortable with a new local facility. It is perception evidence, not a realized-impact, rate, emissions, water-use, or consent evaluation.

The latest US consumer-health pass adds the [Pew supplements record](records/us-pew-supplements-2026-wellness-consumer-health-belief-market.json): 78% of adults report taking at least one supplement, with category, age, income, gender, and perceived-importance differences that connect health beliefs to consumer markets and household practice. It is self-reported use and belief evidence, not a clinical efficacy, safety, spending, or care-substitution evaluation.

The latest US cultural-memory pass adds the [Pew 9/11 memory and generational-transmission record](records/us-pew-911-memory-generational-transmission-2026-national-trauma-education.json): 9/11 remains vividly remembered by direct witnesses, while younger adults increasingly encounter it through school, family, friends, media, and inherited narratives. It is survey and historical-attitude evidence, not a memory-accuracy, trauma, policy-attitude, or causal political-effects evaluation.

The latest US work/identity pass adds the [Pew working-class identity record](records/us-pew-working-class-identity-2026-politics-income-occupation.json): 60% identify as working class, including half of upper-income and financially comfortable adults, while partisan gaps persist across income, stress, education, occupation, and race. It treats class language as a cultural and political classification layer, not as a hidden income, hardship, or respondent-level causal variable.

The latest US workplace-data pass adds the [Gallup employee data-use record](records/us-gallup-employee-data-trust-2026-workplace-monitoring-voice.json): only 20% report a great deal of trust that leaders use employee data responsibly, while purpose-specific comfort is higher for development and team effectiveness than for performance, staffing, or scheduling decisions. It is a published workplace signal with limited public method detail, not an audit of actual surveillance or a causal productivity, engagement, or bargaining study.

The latest US women's-health pass adds the [Gallup-Pivotal women's health record](records/us-gallup-pivotal-womens-health-2026-care-work-civic-participation.json): care dismissal, diagnostic delay, and treatment barriers are reported alongside reduced career capacity, caregiving, relationships, and civic participation, with menopause as a concentrated life-stage example. It is survey evidence of reported experience and spillover, not a clinical causal study or a measure of employer accommodation.

The latest US care-affordability pass adds the [KFF women's health affordability record](records/us-kff-womens-health-affordability-2026-cost-care-tradeoffs.json): cost delays needed care even among insured women, changes prescription use, and forces tradeoffs against food, clothing, and electricity. It is nationally representative survey evidence of reported affordability and household-room pressure, not a clinical-outcome or causal insurance evaluation.

The latest US gender-representation pass adds the [Pew woman-president skepticism record](records/us-pew-woman-president-skepticism-2026-gender-representation-legitimacy.json): skepticism that a woman will become president has risen even as most Americans say presidential gender does not matter, while party and gender identity shape the importance assigned to representation. It is expectation and symbolic-importance evidence, not an electoral forecast or a causal account of gender bias.

The latest US-anchored comparative pass adds the [Pew South Korea-U.S. relationship record](records/comparative-pew-south-korea-us-relationship-2026-alliance-legitimacy-dependence.json): South Korean favorability, perceived U.S. reciprocity, and confidence in U.S. peace contribution have fallen while the U.S. remains the most important ally. It shows strategic dependence and public legitimacy moving on different clocks, not a causal evaluation of alliance policy.

The latest US health-information pass adds the [Pew race and social-media health-information record](records/us-pew-health-information-race-social-media-2026.json): Hispanic, Black, and Asian adults are more likely than White adults to use social media for health information and to use influencers for questions they do not want to ask a doctor. It is a race-differentiated source-pathway signal, not a misinformation, clinical-substitution, or health-outcome study.

The latest US AI-health pass adds the [Pew AI chatbots for health record](records/us-pew-ai-chatbots-health-2026-navigation-privacy-mental-health.json): chatbots are becoming a low-cost health-navigation layer for symptoms, diagnoses, lab results, treatments, sensitive questions, and decisions about seeing a doctor, while helpfulness coexists with privacy hesitation and concern about mental-health support. It is survey evidence of use and judgment, not a clinical, privacy, or outcome evaluation.

The latest US AI/work pass adds the [Pew AI concern and young-adult jobs record](records/us-pew-ai-concern-young-adults-jobs-2026.json): concern now exceeds excitement about AI, young adults' concern has climbed sharply since 2021, and 71% expect AI to lead to fewer U.S. jobs over the next two decades. It is public expectation and sentiment evidence, not a labor-market forecast, adoption measure, or causal account of concern.

The latest US family-mobility pass adds the [Urban families, core cities and suburbs record](records/us-urban-families-core-suburbs-2026.json): fertility and child retention differ across metropolitan geographies, with family-sized housing, child care, schools, commute patterns, and socioeconomic constraints shaping distinct place-based pathways. It is a multi-metro correlational analysis, not a causal fertility, migration, housing-preference, or policy-impact evaluation.

The latest US care-portfolio pass adds the [CSSP family care arrangements record](records/us-cssp-family-care-arrangements-2026.json): families commonly combine and sequence parental, formal, and family/friend/neighbor care, while affordability, trust, cultural fit, availability, and work design separate care used from care wanted. It is qualitative interview evidence, not a nationally representative prevalence, causal, or child-outcome study.

The latest US corporate-legitimacy pass adds the [Stanford CEO activism and consumer response record](records/us-stanford-ceo-activism-consumer-response-2026.json): support for CEO advocacy is increasingly issue-specific, younger adults are more cautious about CEO speech on AI, and consumers report remembering and changing purchasing behavior in response to executive positions. It is a public-summary survey signal with incomplete public tables, not a verified sales, employee, or political-outcome evaluation.

The latest US household-literacy pass adds the [Capita-Book Harvest home literacy record](records/us-capita-book-harvest-home-literacy-2026.json): parents overwhelmingly value books, but home-library supply, cultural fit, cost, and reading time vary by race, income, work pressure, and family resources. It is partner-sponsored YouGov survey evidence, not a causal education, literacy, or civic-outcome evaluation.

The latest global youth-well-being pass adds the [UNU–UN Youth Office youth mental-health record](records/comparative-unu-un-youth-mental-health-2026-ecosystem-wellbeing.json): the flagship reframes youth mental health from individual symptoms and treatment toward prevention and the surrounding systems of housing, education, decent work, digital life, climate, peace, culture, sports, governance, and youth agency. It is a global policy framework that compiles cited evidence, not a new prevalence survey or causal evaluation of the ecosystem model.

The latest US teen-AI pass adds the [Google U.S. Future Report record](records/us-google-future-report-2026-teen-ai-learning-judgment.json): AI is already routine in teen learning and creativity, while verification, productive friction, school norms, trusted-adult guidance, and self-limitation remain uneven. It is Google-sponsored survey and qualitative evidence with inconsistent public headline sample descriptions, not an independent behavioral-trace, causal learning, or school-policy evaluation.

The latest US civic-expression pass adds the [Freedom Forum First Amendment record](records/us-freedom-forum-first-amendment-2026-expression-knowledge-surveillance.json): Americans broadly value the First Amendment while knowledge gaps, fear of violence or social tension, government-retaliation concerns, surveillance, and online-information distrust shape practical participation. It is a proprietary-method online panel survey, not a probability-sample population estimate, legal assessment, behavioral trace, or causal participation study.

The latest US youth-civic-capacity pass adds the [Chamber Foundation Civic and Economic Literacy record](records/us-chamber-foundation-civic-economic-literacy-2026-teen-optimism-practice.json): teens recognize division but remain more optimistic than adults that national problems can be fixed, while knowledge and opportunities to practice listening, tradeoffs, and participation lag stated civic values. It is Chamber Foundation/RXN survey and program-framing evidence, not a causal civic-education or participation evaluation.

The latest UK comparative digital-lives pass adds the [Nuffield–Ada Lovelace youth digital-experience record](records/comparative-nuffield-ada-young-people-digital-lives-2026-platform-agency.json): young people describe online spaces as both community infrastructure and an inescapable system of platform control, surveillance, unequal exposure, and harm, with AI raising concerns about work, creativity, relationships, and public services. It is peer-led qualitative evidence from 49 young people, not a prevalence, behavioral-trace, clinical, legal, or causal evaluation.
## Latest comparative rotation — renewable-energy jobs

The [IRENA–ILO Renewable Energy and Jobs 2025 record](records/comparative-irena-ilo-renewable-energy-jobs-2025-inclusive-workforce-geopolitics.json) adds a transition-distribution layer: renewable employment reached 16.6 million globally in 2024, but growth slowed to 2.3% despite record installations. The report links the conversion gap to scale, automation, manufacturing overcapacity and grid constraints, while highlighting geographic concentration and continuing inclusion barriers.

The [UNDP Peoples' Climate Vote 2024 record](records/comparative-undp-peoples-climate-vote-2024-global-action-fairness-geopolitics.json) adds a global public-legitimacy layer: broad demand for stronger climate action coexists with questions of lived exposure, policy fairness, adaptation responsibility and geopolitical cooperation.

The [European Commission energy Eurobarometer record](records/comparative-european-commission-eurobarometer-energy-affordability-consumer-protection-2025.json) adds an EU household-control layer: transition legitimacy is tied to affordable bills, vulnerable-consumer protection, transparent information, practical energy agency and reliable coordination.

The [European Commission Citizens' Energy Package record](records/comparative-european-commission-citizens-energy-package-2026-affordability-security-autonomy.json) adds the institutional-response layer: affordability, clean-energy finance, grid investment, consumer rights and strategic autonomy are being assembled into one state-capacity agenda.

The [World Bank Women, Business and the Law 2026 record](records/comparative-world-bank-women-business-law-2026-law-policy-enforcement-gender-economy.json) adds a law-to-capability layer: formal rights, supportive institutions and enforcement remain sharply separated, especially across safety, childcare and entrepreneurship.

The [World Bank W-LEX record](records/comparative-world-bank-wlex-womens-lived-experience-2026-law-practice-gender-perception.json) adds a lived-experience test of that implementation gap, using comparable national survey evidence on how women and men perceive work, pay, care, safety, mobility and economic opportunity.

The [World Bank gender-and-fiscal-policy record](records/comparative-world-bank-gender-fiscal-policy-2026-tax-budget-care-data.json) adds the fiscal mechanism: tax rules, budget systems, care spending and sex-disaggregated data can either reinforce or reduce the gap between rights and usable economic participation.

The [UK Social Connection Framework record](records/comparative-uk-dcms-social-connection-framework-2026-young-men-belonging-infrastructure.json) adds a youth-belonging and social-infrastructure layer, linking loneliness, stigma, NEET status, online harms, trusted adults, accessible places and community participation.

The [UK NatCen boys and young men loneliness record](records/comparative-uk-dcms-natcen-boys-young-men-loneliness-2026-risk-protective-factors.json) supplies the empirical companion: intermittent loneliness is common, while mental health, bullying, family support, friendship, economic inactivity and in-person contact structure persistent disconnection.

The [UK Offload and Movember pilot evaluation record](records/comparative-uk-dcms-offload-movember-youth-connection-pilot-2026-evaluation.json) adds a named intervention and evaluation route, testing whether place-based community sport can turn connection into wellbeing, aspiration and re-engagement.

The [UNDP Bosnia and Herzegovina SCORE 2025 record](records/comparative-undp-bosnia-social-cohesion-score-2025-local-resilience-system-pessimism.json) adds a comparative social-cohesion diagnostic: everyday intergroup coexistence and local cooperation can remain relatively resilient while pessimism about politics, economic uncertainty and institutional performance suppresses civic capacity.

The [WHO social determinants of health equity record](records/comparative-who-social-determinants-health-equity-2025-life-chances-public-services.json) adds a structural-health layer: housing, education, work, income, taxation, discrimination, climate, displacement and universal public services are treated as upstream conditions of health and life chances rather than separate health-care topics.

The [Deloitte Gen Z and Millennial Survey 2026 record](records/comparative-deloitte-genz-millennial-survey-2026-stability-housing-ai-work-purpose.json) adds a generational work-and-life layer: cost, housing, sustainable workload, purpose, AI adoption, digital fatigue and leadership expectations are reshaping how younger adults sequence ambition and major life decisions.

The [Full Fact Report 2026 record](records/comparative-full-fact-uk-information-environment-2026-ai-uncertainty-democratic-trust.json) adds a democratic-information infrastructure layer: AI-mediated search, synthetic media, platform incentives, media literacy and fragmented public responsibility are changing how people establish facts, trust institutions and participate in public life.

The [Future of International Cooperation Report 2026 record](records/comparative-stimson-future-international-cooperation-2026-global-trust-multilateral-governance.json) adds a global-governance trust layer: information integrity, AI capacity, diplomacy, trade, security, justice and shared public goods are being treated as linked cooperation problems.

The latest social-contract rotation adds the [Earth for All Survey 2026 record](records/comparative-earth4all-social-contract-2026-inequality-trust-democracy.json): across 17 countries, public demand for economic-system change is broad, but long-term confidence that governments will deliver it is weak. This adds a delivery-gap route from perceived inequality and system unfairness through social division, democratic legitimacy, and consent for climate and economic transformation; it is weighted public-opinion evidence, not an objective inequality estimate or causal forecast.

The latest information-environment rotation adds the [IPIE 2026 expert-survey record](records/comparative-ipie-information-environment-2026-platform-accountability-ai-search.json): access to diverse viewpoints is separating from trust in their accuracy, while platform accountability and AI-mediated search create new trade-offs between answer speed, news traffic, and viewpoint diversity. It also records the research-capacity pressures that limit society's ability to study the problem.

The latest community-power rotation adds the [NPC Everyone's Environment record](records/comparative-npc-everyones-environment-community-power-2026-climate-nature.json): community knowledge is often collected without gaining influence over policy, funding, governance, or delivery. The report identifies grassroots-capacity and evidence-influence loops and tests usable access to nature as a case where proximity alone does not equal safety, belonging, quality, or voice.

The latest UK consumer rotation adds the [KPMG Consumer Pulse Q3 2026 record](records/comparative-kpmg-uk-consumer-pulse-q3-2026-financial-security-discretionary-culture.json): financial caution is reallocating discretionary life rather than stopping consumption. Everyday cutbacks, savings, job stickiness, delayed big-ticket purchases, social and cultural occasions, resale, and channel hybridity coexist in the same consumer environment.

The latest culture-and-place rotation adds the [HERA En/counter/points record](records/comparative-hera-encounter-points-belonging-public-space-culture-2026.json): belonging is shaped through communities, places, and histories, and the research-to-practice pathway shows how museum toolkits and public-space guidance can translate inclusive-belonging research into institutional routines while leaving audience and causal outcomes open.

The latest AI-commerce rotation adds the [Product.ai Trust in AI Commerce record](records/us-product-ai-trust-commerce-2026-ai-recommendation-verification-agency.json): AI is entering product discovery, but shoppers retain verification practices and low spending-autonomy thresholds. The public CSV and codebook make the denominator, subgroup, and agency boundaries inspectable.

The latest agentic-commerce rotation adds the [Visa Earning Consumer Trust in the Age of Agentic Commerce record](records/comparative-visa-agentic-commerce-payment-control-reversibility-2025-2026.json): AI shopping agents may compress discovery, evaluation, checkout, and post-purchase work, but adoption depends on payment-level visibility, data control, fraud protection, refunds, recognizable providers, and the ability to stop or reverse the interaction. This extends the AI-commerce theme from recommendation trust into transaction governance and recourse.

The latest cultural-discoverability rotation adds the [European cultural discoverability record](records/comparative-eu-cultural-discoverability-platform-curation-ai-2026.json): digital abundance does not guarantee cultural visibility. Platform ranking, recommendation, metadata, language, digital skills, promotion capacity, and AI-generated content shape which creators and cultural expressions can actually be found, making discoverability a cultural-policy and pluralism issue rather than only a consumer-search issue.

The latest AI-search distribution rotation adds the [One Further cultural-sector AI Overviews record](records/comparative-one-further-ai-overviews-cultural-sector-2026-search-traffic-distribution.json): across the same 100 UK cultural organizations, estimated organic search traffic fell 18% year on year and 79 organizations declined, even as average visibility inside AI Overviews rose from 5.8% to 15.6%. This makes the visibility-versus-direct-audience-ownership tradeoff concrete.

The latest organizational-culture rotation adds the [Culture Amp Performance Culture record](records/comparative-cultureamp-performance-culture-ai-2026-organizational-voice-resourcing.json): the report frames sustained performance as a collective system linking engagement, confidence, leadership, voice, energy, learning, and resource alignment. It adds an organizational mechanism to the broader pattern that AI and measurement only create value when paired with visible human judgment, employee voice, and credible action.

The latest social-connection rotation adds the [Belgian Health Interview Survey record](records/comparative-belgium-social-connection-postpandemic-youth-living-alone-2026.json): national post-pandemic connection was broadly stable, but poor social support among 15-24-year-olds and low social satisfaction among people living alone were higher than before the pandemic. This sharpens the program against one-size-fits-all decline narratives and toward subgroup-specific social infrastructure and life-course exposure.

The latest global relationship-quality rotation adds the [Global Flourishing Study 22-country record](records/comparative-global-flourishing-social-relationship-quality-22-countries-2026.json): social relationship quality varies across national contexts and life situations, with age, religious participation, partnership status, and employment associated with different relational outcomes. It adds a comparative social-resource layer without treating cultural orientation as a simple universal explanation.

The latest life-course rotation adds the [Global Flourishing childhood-antecedents record](records/comparative-global-flourishing-childhood-antecedents-adult-relationships-2025.json): childhood family relationships, abuse, belonging, health, and financial security are associated with adult relationship quality across 22 countries, but the pathways vary substantially by country. This deepens the social-capacity theme from present-day connection into accumulated exposure and upstream institutional conditions.

The latest crisis-cohesion rotation adds the [crisis interdependence record](records/comparative-crisis-interdependence-social-cohesion-2026.json): natural hazards, pandemics, economic crises, and wars affect cohesion through different combinations of mutual dependence, unequal vulnerability, conflicting interests, and voluntary versus imposed coordination. This supplies a mechanism framework for interpreting why some crises produce solidarity while others produce resentment, scapegoating, or institutional distrust.

The latest firm-side/global SME rotation adds the [Mastercard Dreamonomics record](records/comparative-mastercard-dreamonomics-2026-sme-controlled-growth-digital-cybersecurity.json): small businesses describe growth as controlled momentum under rising costs, uncertainty, revenue volatility, digital-tool fragmentation, and cybersecurity exposure. It adds regional variation and business-infrastructure dependence while preserving Mastercard-sponsored survey, incomplete-method, self-report, and non-causal limits.

The latest U.S. household rotation adds the [KPMG Consumer Pulse Summer 2026 record](records/us-kpmg-consumer-pulse-summer-2026-household-room-discretionary-travel-ai.json): households report protecting essentials and future security by shifting budget share away from discretionary room, moving some meals home, retaining travel aspirations under affordability constraints, and using AI selectively in travel planning. It adds a household-side adaptation route while preserving survey, forecast, sponsor, and non-causal limits.

The latest macro-geopolitical rotation adds the [OECD Economic Outlook March 2026 record](records/comparative-oecd-economic-outlook-march-2026-energy-geopolitics-household-exposure.json): conflict-driven energy and fertiliser disruption transmits through commodity prices, inflation, supply chains, labour markets, and fiscal buffers, with lower-income households and import-dependent economies more exposed. It adds an official macro-to-household mechanism while preserving scenario, forecast, aggregation, and causal limits.

The latest institutional-legitimacy rotation adds the [Trustlab institutional-trust record](records/comparative-trustlab-institutional-trust-governance-performance-six-countries-2026.json): across six OECD countries, perceived reliability, integrity, and service performance were stronger correlates of stated institutional trust than political affiliation alone, while explicit, implicit, and experimental trust measures diverged. It adds a governance-encounter mechanism while preserving historical-data, cross-country, measurement, and causal limits.

The latest rights-delivery rotation adds the [International SERF Index 2026 record](records/comparative-serf-index-2026-social-economic-rights-capacity-200-countries.json): social and economic rights fulfillment is benchmarked against feasible performance at each income level across health, education, food, housing, and work. It adds a delivery-versus-capacity and distributional measurement layer while preserving coverage, benchmark, missingness, and comparability limits.

The latest wealth-distribution rotation adds the [UBS Global Wealth Report 2026 record](records/comparative-ubs-global-wealth-report-2026-wealth-pyramid-median-mobility-ownership.json): global personal wealth expanded rapidly while median wealth declined in most markets, millionaire ranks grew, debt normalized, and wealth composition increasingly shaped liquidity, inheritance, and cultural assets. It adds an ownership and relative-position layer while preserving UBS modeling, currency, sampling, and causal limits.

The latest wealth-measurement rotation adds the [Graduate Center Wealth Project warehouse record](records/comparative-gc-wealth-project-data-warehouse-2026-transmission-tax-measurement.json): harmonized databases link wealth composition, distribution, inheritance and gift taxation, source metadata, and reproducible aggregate evidence across countries and periods. It strengthens the program's ability to distinguish ownership claims from measurement artifacts and respondent-level evidence.

The latest GenAI-work rotation adds the [ILO GenAI jobs and work-organization record](records/comparative-ilo-genai-jobs-productivity-work-organization-2026-aggregation-paradox.json): task-level time savings and productivity gains remain uneven and have not yet broadly become measured output, earnings, or employment gains; effects depend on task complexity, worker judgment, organizational redesign, and who captures saved time. It adds a comparative empirical route across seven countries while preserving early-evidence, measurement, adoption-lag, and causal limits.

The latest displacement-and-cooperative-capacity rotation adds the [ILO PROSPECTS Jordan cooperative record](records/comparative-ilo-jordan-prospects-cooperatives-refugee-inclusion-2026.json): cooperative legal reform, institutional capacity, shared ownership, women’s and youth participation, refugee-host economic activity, food and water capability, and social cohesion can reinforce one another, but work-permit restrictions, childcare, transport, finance, donor dependence, and weak monitoring still limit durable inclusion.

The latest legitimacy-and-attribution rotation adds the [Institute for Government public-understanding record](records/comparative-uk-institute-government-perception-gap-experience-trust-2026.json): people can report mostly good service experiences while distrusting government’s intentions, assigning blame and credit asymmetrically across national, regional, and local tiers, and using news, social networks, and place identity to interpret responsibility. It adds a comparative political-cultural mechanism while preserving survey, qualitative, regional, and causal limits.

The 2026-09-29 Medicare Advantage SNF prior-authorization recheck keeps the
health-access route live at the institutional-response stage: all three CMS
recommendations attached to the HHS-OIG review remain open and unimplemented,
with a tracker update expected 2026-12-07. This is a current oversight-status
observation, not evidence of changed denial practice, timely admission, care
receipt, recovery, household burden, trust, or plan exit. The next decisive
input remains an authorized beneficiary or plan/provider episode.
